 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>Plugins from the AI labs, for revenue teams</h1>
   <p class="lede">Anthropic and OpenAI, both AI labs and vendors, publish plugins: sets of skills that their assistants run for one kind of work. Salesforce, a CRM vendor, publishes one for Claude. Choose one to see what it holds.</p>
   <p class="who"><span><b>Preview.</b> Compiled by Claude and not yet reviewed.</span><span>Checked on November 21, 2026</span><span>For revenue leaders and operators</span></p>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="from-anthropic">From Anthropic</h2>
   <ul class="plist"><li><a class="pl" href="#overview"><span class="pn">Claude Sales plugin</span><span class="pf">Version 2.1.0, 36 skills</span><span class="pd">Sales work from first touch to renewal: research, calls, pipeline, forecast and CRM updates.</span><span class="pgo">Full entry</span></a></li><li><a class="pl" href="#marketing"><span class="pn">Claude Marketing plugin</span><span class="pf">Version 1.2.0, 8 skills</span><span class="pd">Planning a campaign, drafting and checking content, and reporting on results.</span><span class="pgo">Skill list</span></a></li><li><a class="pl" href="#customer-support"><span class="pn">Claude Customer support plugin</span><span class="pf">Version 1.3.0, 5 skills</span><span class="pd">Sorting tickets, drafting replies, escalating and writing help articles.</span><span class="pgo">Skill list</span></a></li><li><a class="pl" href="#small-business"><span class="pn">Claude Small business plugin</span><span class="pf">Version 1.35.2, 44 workflows</span><span class="pd">An owner&#x27;s sales, marketing, finance and hiring. Of its 44 workflows, 18 serve sales, marketing or customers.</span><span class="pgo">Skill list</span></a></li></ul>
   <h2 id="from-openai" style="margin-top:44px">From OpenAI</h2>
   <ul class="plist"><li><a class="pl" href="#chatgpt-sales"><span class="pn">ChatGPT for Sales</span><span class="pf">Version 1.1.7, 18 skills</span><span class="pd">Researching accounts, preparing and following up meetings, planning deals, reviewing a forecast and coaching reps.</span><span class="pgo">Reported by ChatGPT</span></a></li></ul>
   <h2 id="from-salesforce" style="margin-top:44px">From Salesforce</h2>
   <ul class="plist"><li><a class="pl" href="#salesforce-in-claude"><span class="pn">Salesforce in Claude</span><span class="pf">Beta, 37 skills</span><span class="pd">Researching accounts, preparing and following up meetings, working deals, reviewing pipeline and keeping Salesforce up to date.</span><span class="pgo">Public pages only</span></a></li></ul>
   <dl class="depth"><div><dt>Full entry</dt><dd>Every skill, one by one: what it reads and can change, who it suits, what to settle before rollout and how to configure it.</dd></div><div><dt>Skill list</dt><dd>Every relevant skill, restated from its own description. Nothing is rated.</dd></div><div><dt>Reported by ChatGPT</dt><dd>Every skill, as the provider's own assistant reported it from an installed copy. We did not read the files, and nothing is rated.</dd></div><div><dt>Public pages only</dt><dd>What its makers publish about a plugin in beta. No skill file was read, and nothing is rated.</dd></div></dl>
  </div>

 <section class="sec soft tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="leader-skills">Skills for leaders in plugins made for other teams</h2>
   <p>Anthropic also publishes plugins for operations, data, product, design, search, legal, people and finance teams. 32 of their skills do work that a revenue leader, revenue operations or enablement also does.</p>
   <p class="note">What each skill does is restated from its own description. Which skills are shown, and who may reach for each, is our read.</p>
   <div class="lsk"><details class="more"><summary>Operations <span class="cnt">7 of 9 skills, version 1.3.0</span></summary><div class="scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Operations  <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="not-listed">What is not listed</h2>
   <ul class="lim">
    <li><strong>Partner plugins.</strong> Five more plugins in Anthropic's repository are built by partners: Apollo (built by Apollo.io), Common Room, Brand voice (Tribe AI), Slack (Salesforce) and Zoom, which is for developers who build Zoom integrations.</li>
    <li><strong>Other teams' plugins.</strong> Five of Anthropic's own are outside go-to-market (GTM) work: Engineering, Bio-research, Productivity, Plugin management and PDF viewer.</li>
    <li><strong>Google.</strong> Its skills in the Gemini app and in Google Workspace are reusable instructions that a team writes itself or adapts from a template. One search on November 21, 2026 found no set of sales skills built by Google.</li>
    <li><strong>Microsoft.</strong> Its documentation, last updated on November 3, 2026, describes two sales plugins for Copilot Cowork. Sales works with Dynamics 365 Sales and needs a license for it. Sales for Cowork needs no CRM license, and Microsoft calls it CRM-agnostic. Both also need a license for Microsoft Copilot, and Microsoft says Cowork is billed on usage. The documentation lists 18 skills by name: by our count of its table, 17 are in Sales and 6 in Sales for Cowork, with 5 in both. A Microsoft post of November 17, 2026 says an updated Sales plugin is generally available and counts more than 57 skills across its Sales and Customer Service plugins. The documentation names no public copy of the skills' files, and neither plugin has been installed here, so they have no page yet. Both pages were read on November 23, 2026.</li>
   </ul>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:14px">A listing is never paid for, and it is not an endorsement. <a href="#method">How these pages were put together</a></p>
  </div>
 </section>

<div id="overview" data-view="overview" data-group="sales" data-title="Claude Sales plugin">
 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>Anthropic's Sales plugin for Claude</h1>
   <p class="lede">Anthropic, the AI lab that makes Claude, is the vendor of this plugin. It gives Claude 36 skills for sales work. This entry sets out what each skill does, reads and can change, with nothing to install, and then how to configure the plugin for a company.</p>
   <div class="acts"><a class="btn pri" href="#skills">Browse the 36 skills</a><a class="btn" href="#configure">Configure it for your company</a></div>
   <p class="who"><span><b>Preview.</b> The editor has changed some ratings. His review of the rest is still to come.</span><span>Read against Sales plugin 2.1.0 by Claude on November 21, 2026</span><span>For revenue leaders and operators</span></p>
   <p class="sub">Compiled with Claude, an Anthropic model, from Anthropic's own files. On November 22, 2026 models from two other labs checked it against those files in two rounds, and on November 23, 2026 one of them checked the statements that had changed since. Claude made the corrections after each. The text as it now stands has had no check from outside. Its editor decides what is published.</p>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2>What the plugin's files show</h2>
   <ol class="finds"><li><b><span class="hl">13 of the 36</span> skills read and propose.</b><span class="x">By their own steps they change nothing in a connected tool and send or post nothing. What one of them produces is shown in Claude, and it can be a page that others read, as the battlecards of <a class="skl" href="#skill-competitive-intelligence"><code>competitive-intelligence</code></a> are. A change one of them proposes is made by another skill, once you accept it or where a schedule was set up to make it. Every skill's file also carries one rule: an action you ask for goes through a connected tool. So this counts what each skill's own steps set up, by the furthest it goes in its own file. It is not a count of the most a skill can do.</span><span class="tag">Our count from the files</span></li><li><b><span class="hl">22</span> go further by their own steps.</b><span class="x">Six can post to team chat. <a class="skl" href="#skill-daily-briefing"><code>daily-briefing</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-weekly-wrap"><code>weekly-wrap</code></a> post their own output. The other four post a message drawn from it: <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-context"><code>account-context</code></a> a note to an account's owner, <a class="skl" href="#skill-lead-triage"><code>lead-triage</code></a> a lead handoff, <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a> its Monday questions for reps and <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> a follow-up after a one-to-one. Three write for a customer and change no record. 13 list a change to a record in their own write line: a CRM update, a booked meeting or a contact added to a sequence. In all, six are written to email a customer: <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-summary"><code>call-summary</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-inbox-sweep"><code>inbox-sweep</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-schedule-meeting"><code>schedule-meeting</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-draft-outreach"><code>draft-outreach</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-close-plan"><code>close-plan</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-handle-objection"><code>handle-objection</code></a>. Two more can email someone you name: <a class="skl" href="#skill-daily-briefing"><code>daily-briefing</code></a> sends its briefing to any address you give, and the request for missing information that <a class="skl" href="#skill-route-lead"><code>route-lead</code></a> drafts goes to whoever can supply it. Each acts when you ask, within the setting on the connector's tool. <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a> is counted apart: with write tools on, its dashboard's tiles carry write actions, and each runs when you click it.</span><span class="tag">Our count from the files</span></li><li><b><span class="hl">22 other skills</span> name one skill for record changes.</b><span class="x">That skill is <a class="skl" href="#skill-update-opportunity"><code>update-opportunity</code></a>: 21 hand their changes to it and <a class="skl" href="#skill-route-lead"><code>route-lead</code></a> follows its pattern. It is not the only writer. Eight others write on their own: <a class="skl" href="#skill-schedule-meeting"><code>schedule-meeting</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-log-activity"><code>log-activity</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-stakeholder-map"><code>stakeholder-map</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-draft-outreach"><code>draft-outreach</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-route-lead"><code>route-lead</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-plan"><code>account-plan</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-expansion-whitespace"><code>expansion-whitespace</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-health"><code>customer-health</code></a>. On four more the change is applied through it: <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-summary"><code>call-summary</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-close-plan"><code>close-plan</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-renewal-radar"><code>renewal-radar</code></a>. <a class="skl" href="#skill-renewal-radar"><code>renewal-radar</code></a> also creates the renewal deals you accept, through the creation flow its file names. 18 of the 36 files open their write line with the word none, and five of those can still post, reply or make a customer document: <a class="skl" href="#skill-lead-triage"><code>lead-triage</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-handle-objection"><code>handle-objection</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-create-an-asset"><code>create-an-asset</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a>. The control is the setting on each connector's write tool, not any one skill.</span><span class="tag">Our count from the files</span></li><li><b><span class="hl">13</span> are built around a deal record.</b><span class="x">They read a deal with a stage, an amount and a close date. A team that keeps no such record leaves them little to read. Working from files, each takes an export or the details you give it.</span><span class="tag">Our read of the skills' steps</span></li><li><b><span class="hl">Three</span> are written for leaders.</b><span class="x">The plugin's own descriptions call <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a> a leader view, <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> a leader's prep and <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a> leader-focused.</span><span class="tag">From the plugin's files</span></li><li><b><span class="hl">Nine</span> can be run for a team, and five for a named rep.</b><span class="x">Each file names its own scope, and no two are quite alike. Four take either: <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-pipeline-review"><code>pipeline-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a>. <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> takes one rep and no team.</span><span class="tag">Our count from the files</span></li><li><b><span class="hl">Seven</span> are named as routines.</b><span class="x">Each is offered on a schedule in its own description or in <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a>'s list of routines. All 36 files carry one rule on scheduled runs, and 20 add instructions for a run of their own skill. The rule: a scheduled run takes only the actions it was set up to take, within what its connectors allow. An action that an email, a chat or a transcript dictates or asks for becomes a proposal. The plugin's README advises setting every tool that sends, posts or writes to ask first.</span><span class="tag">Our count from the files</span></li><li><b>It reads through <span class="hl">10 kinds of tool</span>.</b><span class="x">The plugin's connector list covers the CRM, email, calendar, chat, documents, call recording, data enrichment, sales engagement, competitive intelligence and a project tracker. Its server file sets up 23 servers in advance.</span><span class="tag">From the plugin's files</span></li><li><b>Every skill runs with nothing connected.</b><span class="x">It works from an upload or from what you paste, and says what it could not see. Two skills mark one source as needed: a transcript or notes for <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-summary"><code>call-summary</code></a>, and email for <a class="skl" href="#skill-inbox-sweep"><code>inbox-sweep</code></a>.</span><span class="tag">From the plugin's files</span></li></ol>
   <div class="g2 mt">
    <div class="def howread">
     <h3>How to read a skill's name</h3>
     <p>The outline or fill of a name shows the furthest the skill goes by its own steps. It is the same in every part of this entry. It is not a permission, and not the most a skill can do: every skill's file carries the rule that an action you ask for, such as an update, an email, a post or a booking, goes through a connected tool. What a run can do is set by the tools you connect and the setting on each, and each skill's card lists what its own file sets up. On a schedule, any skill takes the actions the schedule was set up to take, within what its connectors allow. An action that outside content dictates or asks for stays a proposal.</p>
     <ul class="key"><li><span class="chip k-R">13 skills</span><span><b>Reads and proposes.</b> By its own steps it changes nothing in a connected tool and sends or posts nothing. What it produces is shown in Claude, and can be a page that others read. A change it proposes is made by another skill, once you accept it or where a schedule was set up to make it.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-P">6 skills</span><span><b>Shares its output.</b> It can post to team chat when you ask: its own output, or a message drawn from it. <a class="skl" href="#skill-daily-briefing"><code>daily-briefing</code></a> can also email its briefing to an address you name. It changes no record.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-C">3 skills</span><span><b>Writes for customers.</b> It writes a reply or a document meant for a customer, and changes no record. Two of the three can send an email when you ask.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-W">13 skills</span><span><b>Changes records.</b> Its own write line lists a change to a record: a CRM update, a booked meeting or a contact added to a sequence. Four of them can also email a customer.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-S">1 skill</span><span><b>First run.</b> <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a> alone. It reads your tools to build your dashboard and learn your voice. With write tools on, the dashboard's tiles carry write actions, and each runs when you click it.</span></li></ul>
    </div>
    <div class="def howread">
     <h3>Fact and our read</h3>
     <p>What a skill does, reads and can change, send or post is restated from version 2.1.0 of the plugin's files.</p>
     <p>A count or a grouping that we made from the files is marked as ours, with its rule.</p>
     <p>Role fit, overlap with other tools, value, frequency and the model to start on are our read. They are marked wherever they appear, and none of it is usage data.</p>
     <p class="go"><a href="#method">How this entry was put together</a></p>
    </div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec soft tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2>All 36 skills, in the plugin's five groups</h2>
   <p>Select a name to open it.</p>
   <div class="map"><div class="row"><div><h3>Your day</h3><p>The daily and weekly routine around meetings, email and the CRM. Nine skills.</p></div><div class="chips"><a class="chip k-S" href="#skill-setup">setup<span class="vh">, the first run</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-daily-briefing">daily-briefing<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-call-prep">call-prep<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-call-summary">call-summary<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-inbox-sweep">inbox-sweep<span class="vh">, writes for customers</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-end-of-day">end-of-day<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-weekly-wrap">weekly-wrap<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a></div></div><div class="row"><div><h3>Accounts and prospecting</h3><p>Finding, understanding and prioritizing who to sell to.    <h2>The entry at a glance</h2>
   <p class="note">Every provider entry carries the same fields in the same order.</p>
   <dl class="glance"><div><dt>Provider and offering</dt><dd><p>Anthropic, a vendor. The <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/knowledge-work-plugins/tree/main/sales">Sales plugin</a> is its set of sales skills for Claude, read here at version 2.1.0.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Category</dt><dd><p>AI lab</p></dd></div><div><dt>In one sentence</dt><dd><p>A set of 36 skills that Claude runs for sales work, reading from the tools a company connects or from files a person uploads.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Go-to-market (GTM) jobs it covers</dt><dd><ul class="jobs"><li id="cjob-lead-qualification"><span>Lead qualification</span><span class="chips"><a No skill of its own for two jobs: onboarding and adoption, and support. <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-health"><code>customer-health</code></a> touches both: it reports what a customer has adopted and reads support cases where it can see them. For support, Anthropic publishes a <a href="#customer-support">Customer support plugin</a>. Outside the list, because it is each person's own setup run: <a class="chip k-S" href="#skill-setup">setup<span class="vh">, the first run</span></a>.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Systems it works Sharing a plugin with colleagues and publishing one to an organization need a Team or Enterprise plan. The plugin's folder holds a LICENSE file with the Apache 2.0 text, which we read in Anthropic's public repository on GitHub. Its files state no price.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Controls</dt><dd><p>Each connector tool can be set to allow, ask first or block. The plugin's files tell Claude to stay inside those settings, to make a change you ask for and to show a change it proposes before making it. Those are instructions to a model. The setting on the tool is the control. The files do not describe how the provider stores or uses customer data.</p></dd></div><div><dt>What the provider reports</dt><dd><p>No result with a number appears in the plugin's files.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Evidence in the register</dt><dd><p>None yet.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Not public or not known</dt><dd><p>We found no usage data for the plugin in its files or on Anthropic's pages about it, read on November 21, 2026. How the skills perform against a live CRM, and how often each is used, are not known to us.</p></dd></div><div><dt>On this site</dt><dd><p>This entry's four parts: <a href="#skills">the skills</a>, <a href="#fit">fit by role, sales motion and week</a>, <a href="#rollout">what to settle before rollout</a> and <a href="#configure">how to configure it</a>. The <a href="https://claude.ai/artifact/LXB6jHQSey282n21U5eMtp#charter">agent charter</a> and the <a href="https://claude.ai/artifact/LXB6jHQSey282n21U5eMtp#checklist">launch checklist</a> under Tools also apply to skills that run on a schedule. <a href="#plugins">All plugins</a> lists this one beside the others.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Last checked</dt><dd><p>November 21, 2026, for the plugin's README, connector notes and 36 skill files, and for Anthropic's pages on Salesforce in Claude and Claude for Sales. November 16, 2026, for the configuration half and the help pages it cites.</p></dd></div></dl>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec soft tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2>Three names that get mixed up</h2>
   <dl class="names">
    <div><dt>Sales plugin</dt><dd>The subject of this entry. It comes from Anthropic, has 36 skills and works with whichever CRM you connect.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>Salesforce in Claude</dt><dd>A separate plugin from Salesforce, in beta since September 15, 2026, with 37 skills. It has <a href="#salesforce-in-claude">a page of its own</a>, from public pages only.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>Claude for Sales</dt><dd>The title of Anthropic's solutions page for sales teams, which groups both plugins with other features.</dd></div>
   </dl>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">As Anthropic's own pages describe them on November 21, 2026.</p>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2>How this entry is laid out</h2>
   <div class="halves">
    <div class="def">
     <h3>Explore it, with nothing to install</h3>
     <p>Skills lists all 36, each with what to say and what to give it. Fit sorts them by role, by six sales motions and through the week. Before rollout covers the tools you already own, whether your records give the skills enough to read and which model to run them on.</p>
     <p class="go"><a href="#skills">Browse the skills</a></p>
    </div>
    <div class="def">
     <h3>Configure it for your company</h3>
     <p>The configuration guide by Nathan Fierley: what you get, who changes what, the 17 company facts worth writing down and an edition to hand to Claude.</p>
     <p class="go"><a href="#configure">Open the guide</a></p>
    </div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>The 36 skills, one by one</h1>
   <p class="lede">Each row opens on a run card: when to reach for the skill, what to say and what to give it. Under the card sit what the skill reads, where it delivers and what it can do when you ask, then our read on who it suits, which model to start on and what to check first.</p>
   <p class="sub">Every tool a skill reads is optional unless it is marked needed. With a tool missing, the skill works from an upload or from what you paste, and says what it could not see.</p>

   <div class="nojs"><ul class="key"><li><span class="chip k-R">13 skills</span><span><b>Reads and proposes.</b> By its own steps it changes nothing in a connected tool and sends or posts nothing. What it produces is shown in Claude, and can be a page that others read. A change it proposes is made by another skill, once you accept it or where a schedule was set up to make it.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-P">6 skills</span><span><b>Shares its output.</b> It can post to team chat when you ask: its own output, or a message drawn from it. <a class="skl" href="#skill-daily-briefing"><code>daily-briefing</code></a> can also email its briefing to an address you name. It changes no record.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-C">3 skills</span><span><b>Writes for customers.</b> It writes a reply or a document meant for a customer, and changes no record. Two of the three can send an email when you ask.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-W">13 skills</span><span><b>Changes records.</b> Its own write line lists a change to a record: a CRM update, a booked meeting or a contact added to a sequence. Four of them can also email a customer.</span></li><li><span class="chip k-S">1 skill</span><span><b>First run.</b> <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a> alone. It reads your tools to build your dashboard and learn your voice. With write tools on, the dashboard's tiles carry write actions, and each runs when you click it.</span></li></ul></div>

<div class="grp" id="group-day"><h2>Your day</h2><p>The daily and weekly routine around meetings, email and the CRM.</p><div class="sklist">
<details class="sk" id="skill-setup" data-id="setup" data-text="setup setup first run: what is connected, your voice and a starter dashboard first run. it checks which tools answer, says what each one unlocks, works out your role and book from the crm, and builds a starter dashboard with no interview. where two tools answer for the same job, such as two mailboxes, it takes the one on your crm&#x27;s email domain and asks one question only if that does not settle it. it also asks once if colleagues appear on a second email domain. a dashboard of meetings, deals closing soon, emails waiting and three actions, three skills to start with, and a summary of how you write when it finds at least five usable sent emails. first run no usual conflict on your first day with the plugin, and again to check what is connected or to relearn your voice. set me up. every connected tool crm calendar email call transcripts voice and signature">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-S">setup</span><span class="vh">, the first run.</span></span><span class="tg">First run: what is connected, your voice and a starter dashboard</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">First run. It checks which tools answer, says what each one unlocks, works out your role and book from the CRM, and builds a starter dashboard with no interview. Where two tools answer for the same job, such as two mailboxes, it takes the one on your CRM&#x27;s email domain and asks one question only if that does not settle it. It also asks once if colleagues appear on a second email domain.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="setup">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>On your first day with the plugin, and again to check what is connected or to relearn your voice.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-setup" data-base="Set me up.">Set me up.</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-setup">Copy</button></p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Set me up” or “What&#x27;s connected, and what am I missing?”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Upload whatever you have, such as a pipeline export. Paste 5 to 10 emails you have sent so it can learn your voice.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A dashboard of meetings, deals closing soon, emails waiting and three actions, three skills to start with, and a summary of how you write when it finds at least five usable sent emails.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">Every connected tool</span><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Calendar</span><span class="t">Email</span><span class="t">Call transcripts</span></dd></div>

<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>First run.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. <div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-10">Voice and signature</a> (it learns this from your sent mail, or from 5 to 10 emails you paste, and does not ask for it).</dd></div>

<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span></div>

<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With call transcripts connected, the request is enough to start. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Paste the transcript or your notes. This skill cannot run without them.</p></dd></div>

<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t need">Call transcripts, needed</span><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Email</span><span class="cond rn">Meeting-notes documents can stand in for a transcript. For the follow-up&#x27;s recipients it also reads the calendar event, or CRM contacts, and never takes one from the transcript.</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Delivers to</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM<span class="tw">: the changes you accept, through update-opportunity and log-activity</span></span><span class="t">Email<span class="tw">: the follow-up draft</span></span><span class="t">Team chat<span class="tw">: the team summary</span></span></dd></div>

Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">Proposes changes to next step, stage, amount and close date plus an activity log, and applies the ones you accept through update-opportunity and log-activity.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="yes">Creates the follow-up as a draft to the external attendees and sends it when you ask.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="yes">Drafts a summary for the team channel and posts it when you ask.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">A scheduled run applies only the updates it was set up to make. A value, a record or a contact taken from a transcript, an email, a chat or enrichment is never written on a schedule, even when the schedule was set up to make that kind of update.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></dd></div>

<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-03">Qualification method</a>; <a href="#fact-10">Voice and signature</a> (voice: learned in setup or from sent emails you paste, then from an uploaded style guide, then from your sent mail where it can read it, and otherwise a neutral tone. Signature: from setup or from sent emails you paste. It asks for neither).</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>It needs a transcript or notes. With neither it stops and asks, and it will not write a follow-up from a meeting title. Recipients of the follow-up come from the calendar event or the CRM&#x27;s contacts, never from the transcript alone.</dd></div>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Suits</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built for</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Account executive</span><span class="t">Account manager or CSM</span><span class="t">Founder or full-cycle seller</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Also useful to</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t off">SDR or BDR</span><span class="t off">Frontline manager</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Before you rely on it</dt><dd><strong>Agree the rule first.</strong> If your call recorder already writes summaries and next steps to the CRM, this adds a second set.<span class="rule">Pick one author for call notes in the CRM.</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Model to start with</dt><dd><strong>Opus 5.5.</strong> It can change a record or write for a customer, so a slip costs more than a few seconds. That is a rule about cost, and it is unmeasured: the test did not cover writes. Set every tool it can write or send through to ask first. In our small test Haiku 5.5 credited a competitor&#x27;s claim to the customer. The other three models made no such slip. <a href="#models-test">The test</a>. <a href="#models">How we chose</a>.</dd></div>

<details class="sk" id="skill-inbox-sweep" data-id="inbox-sweep" data-text="inbox-sweep inbox sweep unread customer email sorted, with replies drafted sorts unread customer email into what needs a reply, what is only for information and what it should leave alone, then drafts replies in priority order. up to 10 reply drafts from as many as 25 emails, plus a list of what it could not answer and why. each draft is saved inside its original thread where the email tool allows it, and given as text to paste where it does not. writes for customers agree the rule first when customer email has piled up. sweep my inbox and draft replies to the customer emails that need one. email crm calendar voice and signature">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-C">inbox-sweep</span><span class="vh">, writes for customers.</span></span><span class="tg">Unread customer email sorted, with replies drafted</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Sorts unread customer email into what needs a reply, what is only for information and what it should leave alone, then drafts replies in priority order.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="inbox-sweep">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>When customer email has piled up.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-inbox-sweep" data-base="Sweep my inbox and draft replies to the customer emails that need one.">Sweep my inbox and draft replies to the customer emails that need one.</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-inbox-sweep">Copy</button></p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Which customer emails need a reply?”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With email connected, the request is enough to start. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Paste the emails that need a reply. Add a few emails you have sent, or a style guide, so the drafts sound like you.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>Up to 10 reply drafts from as many as 25 emails, plus a list of what it could not answer and why. Each draft is saved inside its original thread where the email tool allows it, and given as text to paste where it does not.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t need">Email, needed</span><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Calendar</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Delivers to</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">Email<span class="tw">: the reply drafts</span></span></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>Writes for customers.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">None of its own. Logging and record updates go through log-activity and update-opportunity when you ask.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="yes">Creates reply drafts and sends one when you ask. Replies go to the thread&#x27;s own participants.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="nope">The sweep&#x27;s digest is shown in Claude, and its file sets up no send or post for it. The reply drafts are under Customer email.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">A scheduled run saves drafts only when the schedule was set up to. Otherwise it gives a digest with replies to paste.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-lead-triage">lead-triage<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><span class="cond">for a first contact from someone new</span><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><span class="cond">when you ask for logging</span><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><span class="cond">when you ask for an update</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Draws on</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-S" href="#skill-setup">setup<span class="vh">, the first run</span></a><span class="cond">as one source of your voice</span></dd></div>

<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-10">Voice and signature</a> (voice: learned in setup or from sent emails you paste, and otherwise from 30 to 40 of your recent sent emails to people outside the company. Working from files: sent emails you paste or an uploaded style guide, and otherwise a neutral tone. Signature: from setup or from sent emails you paste).</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>It needs a mailbox or pasted emails. A live sweep covers the last 72 hours by default and offers to widen, never widening by itself. Pasted mail skips that filter. It opens whole threads before sorting, because a search preview can show only the oldest message. It flags personnel, legal and executive escalations and does not draft them. Email on a deal closing within 30 days comes first, so the cap of 10 drafts can push other urgent mail down.</dd></div>
</dl>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">

If replies are meant to go through a sequencing tool or a support desk, they are not tracked there.<span class="rule">Say which customer email belongs in the support desk or the sequencer, and have reps answer it there.</span></dd></div>

In our small test all four models showed the rep an email that asked for a contract change, and none acted on it. <a href="#models-test">The test</a>. <a href="#models">How we chose</a>.</dd></div>

<details class="sk" id="skill-schedule-meeting" data-id="schedule-meeting" data-text="schedule-meeting schedule meeting find a time, book it and record it finds a time, then proposes times by email, books the invite or sends your booking link. it records a booked meeting in the crm, against the right account and deal, when you ask for that. proposed times, a booked invite or your booking link, and a crm record of a booked meeting when you ask for one. changes records agree the rule first when a next meeting needs a time. find time with [contact] next week for [purpose]. calendar crm email scheduling link voice and signature crm habits">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-W">schedule-meeting</span><span class="vh">, changes records.</span></span><span class="tg">Find a time, book it and record it</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Finds a time, then proposes times by email, books the invite or sends your booking link. It records a booked meeting in the CRM, against the right account and deal, when you ask for that.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="schedule-meeting">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>When a next meeting needs a time.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-schedule-meeting" data-base="Find time with [contact] next week for [purpose].">Find time with <span class="slot">[contact]</span> next week for <span class="slot">[purpose]</span>.</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-schedule-meeting">Copy</button></p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Find 45 minutes with the Acme team next week” or “Send my booking link to Acme”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. It asks which you want if you have not said: times proposed by email, or the invite booked. Where it can write to neither email nor calendar, it gives both as text and does not ask. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Say when you are free, or paste your calendar for the window.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>Proposed times, a booked invite or your booking link, and a CRM record of a booked meeting when you ask for one.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">Calendar</span><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Email</span><span class="t">Scheduling link</span><span class="cond rn">It reads email for an attendee&#x27;s address when the CRM has none: it shows the address from a thread&#x27;s From or To line and uses it once you pick it. It may also read availability and proposed times from an email or chat thread, which it treats as outside content. It never takes an attendee from the text of one.</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Delivers to</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">Calendar<span class="tw">: the invitation, which goes to every attendee</span></span><span class="t">CRM<span class="tw">: the record of the booked meeting</span></span><span class="t">Email<span class="tw">: the email that proposes times or carries your booking link</span></span></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>Changes records.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">Creates the calendar event and logs the booked meeting in the CRM, when you ask.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="yes">Drafts the email that proposes times or carries your booking link, and sends it when you ask. Creating the event emails the invitation to every attendee.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="nope">Its summary of what was booked is shown in Claude, and its file sets up no send or post for it. The invitation, the email and the CRM record are in the rows above.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">A scheduled or routine run books and logs only what it was set up to. Everything else stops at proposed times and drafts.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips">No other skill.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Draws on</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-S" href="#skill-setup">setup<span class="vh">, the first run</span></a><span class="cond">as one source of your voice</span></dd></div>

<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-10">Voice and signature</a> (learned in setup or from emails you paste. It does not ask); <a href="#fact-16">CRM habits</a> (how meetings are logged, which it reads from the CRM first. It asks once only if that does not settle it).</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>Booking sends the invitation, and it says so before it creates the event. A meeting is 30 minutes by default, and 60 for a demo or a quarterly business review. A booking link logs nothing to the CRM until a meeting is booked.</dd></div>
</dl>

<details class="sk" id="skill-log-activity" data-id="log-activity" data-text="log-activity log activity a call, meeting or email logged to the right records records a call, a meeting or an email thread in the crm after the fact, attached to the right account and contact, and to the deal when the conversation was about one, with no form to fill in. a completed activity with a subject, a date and a summary of three to six lines, shown before it is saved and checked afterward. changes records agree the rule first after a call, meeting or email worth recording. log my [call] with [account]. crm call transcripts email calendar crm habits">

<div class="body">
<p class="does">Records a call, a meeting or an email thread in the CRM after the fact, attached to the right account and contact, and to the deal when the conversation was about one, with no form to fill in.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="log-activity">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>

<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-16">CRM habits</a>.</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>It reads from the CRM whether your team logs meetings as tasks or as calendar-style events, and drafts the one your setup expects. If the CRM rejects the entry, it reports the exact error and gives you text to paste. It does not retry with guessed values.</dd></div>

<div class="ourread">
<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Suits</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built for</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">SDR or BDR</span><span class="t">Account executive</span><span class="t">Account manager or CSM</span><span class="t">Founder or full-cycle seller</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Before you rely on it</dt><dd><strong>Agree the rule first.</strong> Where email and calendar already sync to the CRM, or a recorder logs calls, a manual log duplicates the entry.<span class="rule">List which activity types are captured automatically, and log only the rest.</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Model to start with</dt><dd><strong>Opus 5.5.</strong> It can change a record or write for a customer, so a slip costs more than a few seconds. That is a rule about cost, and it is unmeasured: the test did not cover writes. Set every tool it can write or send through to ask first. <a href="#models">How we chose</a>.</dd></div>

<div class="grp" id="group-accounts"><h2>Accounts and prospecting</h2><p>Finding, understanding and prioritizing who to sell to.</p><div class="sklist">
<details class="sk" id="skill-account-research" data-id="account-research" data-text="account-research account research a prospect researched and scored for fit researches a company you do not yet sell to, and optionally one person there: what they do, what changed in the last 6 months, what they probably care about and how well they fit your ideal customer. a brief with dated and sourced signals, a fit table, and two or three specific hooks for outreach. it checks first whether a colleague already owns the account. reads and proposes no usual conflict before first contact with a company. research [company]. enrichment and web research crm ideal customer profile product and value proposition competitors">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-R">account-research</span><span class="vh">, reads and proposes.</span></span><span class="tg">A prospect researched and scored for fit</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Researches a company you do not yet sell to, and optionally one person there: what they do, what changed in the last 6 months, what they probably care about and how well they fit your ideal customer.</p>

<div class="ourread">
<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Suits</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built for</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">SDR or BDR</span><span class="t">Account executive</span><span class="t">Founder or full-cycle seller</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Also useful to</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t off">Account manager or CSM</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>

<div><dt>Model to start with</dt><dd><strong>Sonnet 5.5.</strong> A scoped job with a clear input. <a href="#models">How we chose</a>.</dd></div>
<div class="jsonly"><dt>At your readiness settings</dt><dd class="atset" data-at="account-research"></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Value and frequency by sales motion</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Core</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t need" data-m="ent">Complex new business</span><span class="t need" data-m="vel">High-velocity and inbound</span><span class="t need" data-m="svc">Professional services</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Useful</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t" data-m="mfg">Industrial and distribution</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Occasional</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t" data-m="am">Account management and renewals</span><span class="t" data-m="fin">Client advisory</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">How often</span><span>Weekly. Daily in high-velocity and inbound. Monthly in account management and renewals, and in client advisory.</span></div></div><p style="margin:10px 0 0">Built for company research. It has less to say about a household or an individual, and existing customers are better served by account-context.</p></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it leans on in your organization</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Needs</span><span>A web search, an enrichment tool or source material you supply, for a brief it can verify.</span></div><div><span class="l">Reads through</span><span>No tool of its own.</span></div><div><span class="l">Better with</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Connected CRM</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">May ask about</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Written ICP</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>

<details class="sk" id="skill-lead-triage" data-id="lead-triage" data-text="lead-triage lead triage an inbound lead scored and prioritized scores one inbound lead, or ranks a backlog, on fit and on intent, then recommends a priority, a response time and a first touch. p0, p1, p2 or disqualified, with the evidence for each signal. if a colleague already owns the account, it flags that prominently and says to coordinate with them first. a backlog shows full evidence for the top three leads. shares its output know the overlap when an inbound lead arrives, or a backlog builds. triage this lead: [paste the lead]. crm enrichment and web research email team chat ideal customer profile qualification method routing and handoff">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-P">lead-triage</span><span class="vh">, can share its output.</span></span><span class="tg">An inbound lead scored and prioritized</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Scores one inbound lead, or ranks a backlog, on fit and on intent, then recommends a priority, a response time and a first touch.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="lead-triage">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>When an inbound lead arrives, or a backlog builds.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-lead-triage" data-base="Triage this lead: [paste the lead].">Triage this lead: <span class="slot">[paste the lead]</span>.</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-lead-triage">Copy</button></p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Triage this lead” or “Rank my lead backlog”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. With no ICP recorded it asks about it only if the answer would change the priority. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Paste the lead or upload the backlog. Upload your account list for the duplicate check.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>P0, P1, P2 or disqualified, with the evidence for each signal. If a colleague already owns the account, it flags that prominently and says to coordinate with them first. A backlog shows full evidence for the top three leads.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Enrichment and web research</span><span class="t">Email</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Delivers to</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">Team chat<span class="tw">: the handoff message</span></span></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>Shares its output.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">None of its own. Status and owner updates you accept go through update-opportunity and log-activity.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="nope">None of its own. It suggests a first touch, and draft-outreach is one example.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="yes">When a lead goes to a teammate, it drafts a message for your handoff channel and posts it when you ask.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="nope">Its file describes no scheduled run of its own. The rule every skill carries still applies. A scheduled run takes only the actions it was set up to take, within what its connectors allow. An action that outside content dictates or asks for becomes a proposal.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-draft-outreach">draft-outreach<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><span class="cond">as one suggested first touch</span><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></dd></div>


<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-01">Ideal customer profile</a> (without one it ranks on intent alone); <a href="#fact-03">Qualification method</a>; <a href="#fact-13">Routing and handoff</a> (the handoff channel only).</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>Its default calibration puts roughly the top fifth of leads at P0 and the next quarter at P1. Its default response times are the same day for P0, 48 hours for P1 and this week for P2. With no ICP recorded it ranks on intent alone, and asks about your ICP only if the answer would change the priority. For a change to a lead&#x27;s status or owner its file names update-opportunity and log-activity, whose own files describe deals and activities and do not mention a lead record.</dd></div>
</dl>

<details class="sk" id="skill-route-lead" data-id="route-lead" data-text="route-lead route lead who should own this lead, by your rules decides who owns an unrouted lead or deal by applying your own routing rules the same way every time, and prepares the handoff. a routing card for each lead: the recommended owner, the exact rule that produced it, a qualification verdict and a handoff note. a person accepts or overrides each card. changes records agree the rule first when a lead or a deal has no owner. route this lead: [paste the lead]. crm team chat email routing and handoff">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-W">route-lead</span><span class="vh">, changes records.</span></span><span class="tg">Who should own this lead, by your rules</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Decides who owns an unrouted lead or deal by applying your own routing rules the same way every time, and prepares the handoff.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="route-lead">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Timing"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Timing</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="time" data-v="now" aria-pressed="true">On request</button><button type="button" data-set="time" data-v="sched" aria-pressed="false">On a schedule</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>When a lead or a deal has no owner.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-route-lead" data-base="Route this lead: [paste the lead]." data-sched="Every [weekday morning], sweep the unrouted queue.">Route this lead: <span class="slot">[paste the lead]</span>.</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-route-lead">Copy</button></p><p class="alt nojs"><b>On a schedule.</b> <span class="sayt">Every <span class="slot">[weekday morning]</span>, sweep the unrouted queue.</span></p><p class="alt" data-when="sched">For a scheduled run, the plugin's README advises setting every tool that sends, posts or writes to ask first. The skill's file tells Claude to take only the actions the run was set up to take, within what its connectors allow. An action that outside content dictates or asks for becomes a proposal.</p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Route this lead” or “Work the routing queue”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. If your routing rules are not written down it asks for them once. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Paste the lead or upload the queue. It applies the routing rules you have recorded.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A routing card for each lead: the recommended owner, the exact rule that produced it, a qualification verdict and a handoff note. A person accepts or overrides each card.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Delivers to</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM<span class="tw">: the change of owner you accept</span></span><span class="t">Team chat<span class="tw">: the handoff note</span></span><span class="t">Email<span class="tw">: the request for missing information</span></span></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>Changes records.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">Changes record ownership for the routings you accept, shows before and after, and logs the reason for any override.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="nope">None stated. The email asking for missing information goes to whoever can supply it, and the file does not say who that is.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="yes">Drafts a handoff note to the new owner in chat, and an email asking for missing information when a record is too thin to route. Each goes out when the router asks.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">Named as a routine. A scheduled sweep of the unrouted queue builds the routing cards and takes only the actions it was set up to take, within what its connectors allow. Everything else waits for the router.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips">No other skill.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Draws on</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-lead-triage">lead-triage<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><span class="cond">for the qualification verdict, when one is not already attached</span></dd></div>

<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-13">Routing and handoff</a>.</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>An existing account goes to its owner, and an open deal on that account goes to the deal&#x27;s owner, who wins over the account&#x27;s owner. An override with no reason is still applied, and logged as no reason given. Override reasons that recur are surfaced as proposed rule changes. The plugin writes it for whoever works the routing queue.</dd></div>
</dl>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">

<div><dt>Before you rely on it</dt><dd><strong>Know the overlap.</strong> It works out the earliest close date its path supports, which is a second date beside the one a manager&#x27;s inspection or a forecasting tool holds.<span class="rule">Use it to prepare for a deal inspection, and say whose date goes in the forecast when the two differ.</span></dd></div>

<details class="sk" id="skill-deal-signals" data-id="deal-signals" data-text="deal-signals deal signals alerts on what changed across your deals watches your open deals and reports only what changed or crossed a threshold: gone quiet, a close inside 21 days at an early stage, date slipped, champion changed, competitor named, a customer email unanswered for 3 business days or more, a renewal window opening or a next step unchanged for 21 days. the thresholds are defaults, which it fits to the length of your sales cycle. it reads email and transcripts only for accounts a crm signal has already flagged. a short digest split into act today and this week, each item with one line of evidence and a suggested action. reads and proposes know the overlap daily or weekly, to catch what changed. what changed in my book? crm email call transcripts stage definitions deal size and cycle length">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-R">deal-signals</span><span class="vh">, reads and proposes.</span></span><span class="tg">Alerts on what changed across your deals</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Watches your open deals and reports only what changed or crossed a threshold: gone quiet, a close inside 21 days at an early stage, date slipped, champion changed, competitor named, a customer email unanswered for 3 business days or more, a renewal window opening or a next step unchanged for 21 days. The thresholds are defaults, which it fits to the length of your sales cycle. It reads email and transcripts only for accounts a CRM signal has already flagged.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="deal-signals">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Scope"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Scope</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="scope" data-v="me" aria-pressed="true">My own book</button><button type="button" data-set="scope" data-v="team" aria-pressed="false">My team</button></span></span><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Timing"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Timing</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="time" data-v="now" aria-pressed="true">On request</button><button type="button" data-set="time" data-v="sched" aria-pressed="false">On a schedule</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>Daily or weekly, to catch what changed.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-deal-signals" data-base="What changed in my book?" data-team="What changed in my team&#x27;s book?" data-sched="Every [weekday morning], tell me what changed in my book." data-both="Every [weekday morning], tell me what changed in my team&#x27;s book.">What changed in my book?</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-deal-signals">Copy</button></p><p class="alt nojs"><b>For your team.</b> <span class="sayt">What changed in my team&#x27;s book?</span></p><p class="alt nojs"><b>On a schedule.</b> <span class="sayt">Every <span class="slot">[weekday morning]</span>, tell me what changed in my book.</span></p><p class="alt" data-when="sched">For a scheduled run, the plugin's README advises setting every tool that sends, posts or writes to ask first. The skill's file tells Claude to take only the actions the run was set up to take, within what its connectors allow. An action that outside content dictates or asks for becomes a proposal.</p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Any deals gone quiet?”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Upload your pipeline export, and the one before it if you have it.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A short digest split into act today and this week, each item with one line of evidence and a suggested action.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Email</span><span class="t">Call transcripts</span></dd></div>

<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>Reads and proposes.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">None of its own. A record fix goes through update-opportunity.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="nope">None of its own. A touch goes through draft-outreach or schedule-meeting.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="nope">Shown in Claude, for you to read. Its file sets up no send or post for it.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">Named as a routine. A scheduled run gives the digest and proposed changes, and takes only the actions it was set up to take, within what its connectors allow.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-draft-outreach">draft-outreach<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></dd></div>
<div><dt>Draws on</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-renewal-radar">renewal-radar<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></dd></div>
<div><dt>Scope</dt><dd>Your open pipeline, a tier or the team.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-04">Stage definitions</a>; <a href="#fact-07">Deal size and cycle length</a> (cycle length only).</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>By default a deal closing this quarter counts as quiet after 14 days without activity. The thresholds are meant to follow your sales cycle: the file says 14 days is wrong for a 30-day cycle. A competitor named in an email, or an email left unanswered, goes unseen on a deal the CRM has not flagged. Without a known last run it looks back 7 days, and it cannot tell a repeat alert from a new one unless the earlier digest is in the conversation or uploaded. A digest with nothing to report is one line.</dd></div>
</dl>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Suits</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built for</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Account executive</span><span class="t">Founder or full-cycle seller</span><span class="t">Frontline manager</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Also useful to</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t off">Account manager or CSM</span><span class="t off">Sales leader</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Before you rely on it</dt><dd><strong>Know the overlap.</strong> Deal-scoring tools and call recorders raise similar alerts. Two alert streams with different thresholds can both get ignored.<span class="rule">Choose one source of deal alerts for each team. Before you retire the other, note that this one reads email and calls only for deals the CRM has flagged.</span></dd></div>

<details class="sk" id="skill-close-plan" data-id="close-plan" data-text="close-plan close plan a business case and a dated path to signature produces the two documents that move a late-stage deal: a business case in the customer&#x27;s own words, and a mutual action plan worked back from the signature date. a business case to share, a dated table of steps with owners on both sides and a flag on any step that makes the close date impossible. changes records agree the rule first when a deal is in its late stages. build a close plan for the [account] deal. crm call transcripts documents email product and value proposition stakeholders and approvals">

<div class="body">
<p class="does">Builds a deck, a one-pager or a leave-behind for one prospect, from your approved materials and the account&#x27;s own data.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="create-an-asset">

<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-11">Brand and templates</a>.</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>Claims about your own product, pricing, packaging, compliance and positioning come from your approved material or from what you supply, and one that neither covers is labeled unverified. Facts about the customer come from your account data, and each customer number carries a source or a blank to fill. It checks who the asset is for first, and keeps roadmap and battlecard material out of anything a customer will see. It makes a new file by default and replaces an existing one when you tell it to. A first-call deck is 5 or 6 slides unless you or a template say otherwise.</dd></div>
</dl>

<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-W">update-opportunity</span><span class="vh">, changes records.</span></span><span class="tg">Changes a deal record, with before and after</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">The skill that changes a deal record: stage, close date, amount, next step, forecast category or any other field your CRM maps. It reads the record, shows before and after, writes only what you asked for, and checks the result.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="update-opportunity">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>When a deal&#x27;s stage, date, amount or next step changes.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-update-opportunity" data-base="Update [deal]: [the change, such as a new close date].">Update <span class="slot">[deal]</span>: <span class="slot">[the change, such as a new close date]</span>.</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-update-opportunity">Copy</button></p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Push Acme to Negotiation” or “Move the Acme close date to March 15”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>State the change and the current value, or upload the record. You get a before-and-after checklist to apply by hand.</p></dd></div>

Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">Updates the fields you name or accept, on one deal or many, and verifies each.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="nope">None.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="nope">The table of before and after is shown in Claude, and its file sets up no send or post for it. <div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>By default it warns when a stage advances past unmet exit criteria, a close date moves into a closed period or an amount changes by more than 25%. The warnings do not block, and the rep decides. A change the CRM refuses is reported with the exact error and not worked around.</dd></div>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">

<details class="sk" id="skill-forecast" data-id="forecast" data-text="forecast forecast the forecast story: commit, best case and pipeline writes the story a rep or a leader tells on a forecast call: the number by category, the deals behind it, where the risk is and, when it has a prior snapshot or the crm&#x27;s history, what changed since last time. totals for closed, commit, best case and pipeline, a line on each commit and best-case deal, risks to commit and asks for help. when a leader rolls up many reps, the lines are cut to the top five deals by amount. changes records agree the rule first before a forecast call. write my forecast for [this quarter]. crm forecast categories">
<summary><span class="nm"><span class="chip k-W">forecast</span><span class="vh">, changes records.</span></span><span class="tg">The forecast story: commit, best case and pipeline</span><span class="fitm"></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Writes the story a rep or a leader tells on a forecast call: the number by category, the deals behind it, where the risk is and, when it has a prior snapshot or the CRM&#x27;s history, what changed since last time.</p>
<div class="run" data-run="forecast">

<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>Totals for closed, commit, best case and pipeline, a line on each commit and best-case deal, risks to commit and asks for help. When a leader rolls up many reps, the lines are cut to the top five deals by amount.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>

<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM</span></dd></div>
