Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">Changes the forecast category on the deals you accept, through update-opportunity. It does not submit a forecast to any forecasting tool.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="nope">None.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="nope">The forecast narrative is shown in Claude, and its file sets up no send or post for it. The category changes are under Records.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">A scheduled run applies only the changes it was set up to make. A value, a record or a contact taken from a transcript, an email, a chat or enrichment is never written on a schedule, even when the schedule was set up to make that kind of update.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario">deal-slip-scenario<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><span class="cond">for what happens to the number if a deal slips</span></dd></div>

<div><dt>Scope</dt><dd>Your forecast, a named rep or a team rollup.</dd></div>

It takes the CRM&#x27;s own forecast category over one inferred from a stage. The plugin describes it as written for a rep or a leader.</dd></div>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Suits</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built for</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Account executive</span><span class="t">Founder or full-cycle seller</span><span class="t">Frontline manager</span><span class="t">Sales leader</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Also useful to</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t off">Account manager or CSM</span><span class="t off">RevOps or enablement</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Before you rely on it</dt><dd><strong>Agree the rule first.</strong> The official forecast lives wherever it is submitted. A narrative built here can carry a different number, and category edits change what that tool rolls up.<span class="rule">Submit in the forecasting tool. Use this to write the commentary and to check your categories first.</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Model to start with</dt><dd><strong>Opus 5.5.</strong> It can change a record or write for a customer, so a slip costs more than a few seconds. That is a rule about cost, and it is unmeasured: the test did not cover writes. Set every tool it can write or send through to ask first. In our small test all four models got the forecast figures in the key right. Opus 5.5 added a caveat of its own with two wrong figures. <a href="#models-test">The test</a>. <a href="#models">How we chose</a>.</dd></div>

<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">

<div class="run" data-run="renewal-radar">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Run it</h3><p>Restated from the skill's file. The timing is our read.</p></div>
<div class="vary jsonly"><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Your tools"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Your tools</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="conn" aria-pressed="true">Connected</button><button type="button" data-set="src" data-v="files" aria-pressed="false">Working from files</button></span></span><span class="vg" role="group" aria-label="Scope"><span class="vl" aria-hidden="true">Scope</span><span class="seg"><button type="button" data-set="scope" data-v="me" aria-pressed="true">My own book</button><button type="button" data-set="scope" data-v="team" aria-pressed="false">My team</button></span></span></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>Monthly, to see the renewals coming up.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Say</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="say-renewal-radar" data-base="What renewals are coming up?" data-team="What renewals are coming up across my team?">What renewals are coming up?</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="say-renewal-radar">Copy</button></p><p class="alt nojs"><b>For your team.</b> <span class="sayt">What renewals are coming up across my team?</span></p><p class="alt also">Also answers to “Is Acme going to renew?”.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd><p data-when="conn">With your tools connected, the request is enough to start. It asks once where your renewal dates are kept, only if neither the CRM nor what it already knows about your company tells it. It says what it could not see.</p><p data-when="files"><b class="nojs">Working from files. </b>Upload a renewals or contracts export.</p></dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A table of renewals in the next 120 days, by default, with a status and a reason, a block of at-risk accounts to act on this week, and data gaps to fix.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads from</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM</span><span class="t">Email</span><span class="t">Team chat</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Delivers to</dt><dd class="chips"><span class="t">CRM<span class="tw">: the fixes and the renewal deals you accept</span></span></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it can do when you ask</dt><dd><strong>Changes records.</strong> What its own file sets up, within the setting on each connector's tool. Any other action you ask for goes through a connected tool, as in every skill:<div class="rows acts4"><div><span class="l">Records</span><span><span class="yes">Fixes renewal records through update-opportunity and creates the missing renewal deals you accept, through the creation flow its file names. Each is shown with its values first.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Customer email</span><span><span class="nope">None.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">Its own output</span><span><span class="nope">The radar is shown in Claude, and its file sets up no send or post for it. The fixes and the renewal deals are under Records.</span></span></div><div><span class="l">On a schedule</span><span><span class="yes">A scheduled run makes only the fixes it was set up to make. A value, a record or a contact taken from a transcript, an email, a chat or enrichment is never written on a schedule, even when the schedule was set up to make that kind of update.</span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Hands work to</dt><dd class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-expansion-whitespace">expansion-whitespace<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><span class="cond">for the full pass on growth</span><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></dd></div>

<div><dt>Scope</dt><dd>Your book, a named account or the team.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company facts it uses</dt><dd><a href="#fact-14">Renewal and health data</a>.</dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Worth knowing</dt><dd>Risk rises with no activity on the account for 30 days, a changed champion or economic buyer, an open escalation or falling usage where the CRM tracks usage. An expansion under way, or multi-year or auto-renew terms, lower it. The 120-day window is a default, and the file suggests 180 days for enterprise sales. For one named account it adds a pricing recommendation and a paperwork timeline.</dd></div>
</dl>
<div class="ourread">
<h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin's files</small></h3>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Suits</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built for</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Account manager or CSM</span><span class="t">Founder or full-cycle seller</span><span class="t">Sales leader</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Also useful to</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t off">Frontline manager</span><span class="t off">RevOps or enablement</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>
<div><dt>Before you rely on it</dt><dd><strong>Agree the rule first.</strong> Renewal deals created here can double up with ones a billing system, a quoting tool or a customer success platform generates.<span class="rule">If renewals are created automatically, decline the creation it offers and use the radar to read.</span></dd></div>
<div><dt>Model to start with</dt><dd><strong>Opus 5.5.</strong> It can change a record or write for a customer, so a slip costs more than a few seconds. That is a rule about cost, and it is unmeasured: the test did not cover writes. Set every tool it can write or send through to ask first. <a href="#models">How we chose</a>.</dd></div>
<div class="jsonly"><dt>At your readiness settings</dt><dd class="atset" data-at="renewal-radar"></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>Value and frequency by sales motion</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Core</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t need" data-m="am">Account management and renewals</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Useful</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t" data-m="svc">Professional services</span><span class="t" data-m="fin">Client advisory</span><span class="t" data-m="mfg">Industrial and distribution</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Occasional</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t" data-m="ent">Complex new business</span><span class="t" data-m="vel">High-velocity and inbound</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">How often</span><span>Quarterly or less. Weekly in account management and renewals. Monthly in high-velocity and inbound, and in client advisory.</span></div></div><p style="margin:10px 0 0">It depends on knowing where renewal dates live: renewal deals, contract records or a date field. It reads that from the CRM and from what it already knows about your company, and asks once only if neither tells it.</p></dd></div>
<div class="wide"><dt>What it leans on in your organization</dt><dd><div class="rows"><div><span class="l">Built around</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t need">Renewal dates</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Reads through</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Connected CRM</span><span class="note">or an export or a paste</span></span></span></div><div><span class="l">Better with</span><span><span class="chips"><span class="t">Logged activity</span><span class="t">Health or usage data</span><span class="t">Email</span><span class="t">Team chat</span></span></span></div></div></dd></div>
</dl>
</div>

</div>
</details>
</div></div>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:20px">The example requests are illustrations written for this entry. The company and people in them are invented.</p>

<!-- ============================================================ FIT -->
<div id="fit" data-view="fit" data-group="sales" data-title="Claude Sales plugin">
 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>Which skills fit your team</h1>
   <p class="lede">The plugin locks no skill to a role, and the same skill is a daily habit in one business and idle in another. Choose a role and the sales motion closest to yours.</p>
   <p class="sub">This part is our read from the skill files, apart from the lines marked as from the files. None of it is usage data. It was compiled with Claude, an Anthropic model, about an Anthropic plugin. Its editor has changed some of the ratings, and his review of the rest is still to come.</p>
   <div class="subnav"><a href="#fit-role">By role</a><a href="#fit-motion">By sales motion</a><a href="#fit-week">Through the week</a></div>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <div class="bar lead jsonly"><div class="fld"><label for="role-f">Role</label><select id="role-f" data-pick="role"><option value="all">All roles</option><option value="sdr">SDR or BDR</option><option value="ae">Account executive</option><option value="am">Account manager or CSM</option><option value="founder">Founder or full-cycle seller</option><option value="mgr">Frontline manager</option><option value="lead">Sales leader</option><option value="ops">RevOps or enablement</option></select></div><div class="fld"><label for="motion-f">Sales motion</label><select id="motion-f" data-pick="motion"><option value="ent">Complex new business</option><option value="vel">High-velocity and inbound</option><option value="am">Account management and renewals</option><option value="svc">Professional services</option><option value="fin">Client advisory</option><option value="mfg">Industrial and distribution</option></select></div></div>
   <h2 id="fit-role">By role</h2>
   <p>Where it can read the CRM, the plugin's <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a> skill infers one of four roles: account executive, SDR or BDR, leader or customer success. With a role or without one, it suggests three skills to start with. The rows below are a wider reading, role by role.</p>
   <p>Built for this role means the skill does that role's own core work. Also useful means it helps now and then. <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a> is each person's own run, leaders included: first on opening the plugin, and again to check what is connected or to relearn a voice. So no row lists it.</p>
   <div class="def shared">
    <h3>Anyone who carries a book</h3>
    <p>The nine skills in the plugin's Your day group are not tied to a role. They run the routine around meetings, email and the CRM for whoever sells, so the first four rows below leave them out.</p>
    <div class="chips"><a class="chip k-S" href="#skill-setup">setup<span class="vh">, the first run</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-daily-briefing">daily-briefing<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-call-prep">call-prep<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-call-summary">call-summary<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-inbox-sweep">inbox-sweep<span class="vh">, writes for customers</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-end-of-day">end-of-day<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-weekly-wrap">weekly-wrap<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a></div>

   <h3 style="margin-top:38px">What separates a rep's skill from a manager's</h3>
   <ul class="princ">
    <li><b>Three skills are written for leaders</b>The plugin's own descriptions call <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a> a leader view, <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> a leader's prep and <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a> leader-focused.</li>
    <li><b>Nine can be run for a team, and five for a named rep</b>Each file names its own scope, and each works within what the person running it can see. For a team: <a class="skl" href="#skill-weekly-wrap"><code>weekly-wrap</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-signals"><code>deal-signals</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-pipeline-review"><code>pipeline-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-voice"><code>customer-voice</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-renewal-radar"><code>renewal-radar</code></a>. For a named rep: <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-pipeline-review"><code>pipeline-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a>.</li>
    <li><b>Access follows the person</b>Claude acts with each person's own sign-in, so a manager sees a team only as far as the CRM already allows. <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> leaves out what the manager cannot see, and says so.</li>
    <li><b>Leaders mostly need three views</b>The number: <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>. The patterns: <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-competitive-intelligence"><code>competitive-intelligence</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-voice"><code>customer-voice</code></a>. The base: <a class="skl" href="#skill-renewal-radar"><code>renewal-radar</code></a>.</li>
   </ul>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:14px">The first and third come from the plugin's files and Anthropic's help pages. The second is our count from the files, and the fourth is our read.</p>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec soft tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="fit-motion">By sales motion</h2>
   <p>The grid is our reading, for a team that keeps its deals as records. It places each skill by how much it is likely to be worth in the chosen motion, and by a first guess at how often its user would reach for it. Neither is measured.</p>
   <p class="nojs note">The menus need scripts to run. The grid below shows complex new business, and the table under it gives every motion.</p>
   <div class="mnote"><b id="mo-name">Complex new business</b><p id="mo-note">The plugin&#x27;s home ground. The deal skills assume several buyers, staged progress and a forecast by category.</p><p class="note" id="mo-eg">Named accounts, several buyers and cycles of months. Enterprise software, infrastructure, large contracts.</p></div>
   <div class="mx" id="mx" role="group" aria-label="Skills placed by value and by frequency"><div class="corner" style="--r:1;--c:1"></div><div class="ch" style="--r:1;--c:2">Quarterly or less</div><div class="ch" style="--r:1;--c:3">Monthly</div><div class="ch" style="--r:1;--c:4">Weekly</div><div class="ch" style="--r:1;--c:5">Daily</div><div class="rh" style="--r:3;--c:1">Core<small>Close to how the skill was written.</small></div><div class="rh" style="--r:4;--c:1">Useful<small>Works, with part of it idle.</small></div><div class="rh" style="--r:6;--c:1">Occasional<small>A narrow case, or data this motion rarely keeps.</small></div><div class="rh" style="--r:7;--c:1">Poor fit<small>Built around something this motion usually lacks.</small></div><div class="fill" style="--r:2;--c:1"></div><div class="fill b" style="--r:5;--c:1"></div><div class="ql q-ho" data-q="ho" style="--r:2;--c:4 / span 2"><b>Daily drivers</b><small>Rated high and likely to be used often.    <p class="note">Five skills are used when something calls for them, and the grid places them by a rough cadence: <a class="skl" href="#skill-stakeholder-map"><code>stakeholder-map</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-close-plan"><code>close-plan</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-create-an-asset"><code>create-an-asset</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-health"><code>customer-health</code></a>. for</td><td><b>Useful</b>, when called for</td><td><b>Useful</b>, when called for</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">customer-voice</th><td><b>Useful</b>, monthly</td><td><b>Useful</b>, monthly</td><td><b>Core</b>, monthly</td><td><b>Occasional</b>, quarterly or less</td><td><b>Occasional</b>, quarterly or less</td><td><b>Occasional</b>, quarterly or less</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">renewal-radar</th><td><b>Occasional</b>, quarterly or less</td><td><b>Occasional</b>, monthly</td><td><b>Core</b>, weekly</td><td><b>Useful</b>, quarterly or less</td><td><b>Useful</b>, monthly</td><td><b>Useful</b>, quarterly or less</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">Five skills are used when something calls for them, so the table gives no frequency for them. <a class="skl" href="#skill-stakeholder-map"><code>stakeholder-map</code></a>: when you need to see who matters in a deal or an account. <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>: when a deal, or a few, may slip, shrink or be lost, and the number is at stake. <a class="skl" href="#skill-close-plan"><code>close-plan</code></a>: when a deal is in its late stages. <a class="skl" href="#skill-create-an-asset"><code>create-an-asset</code></a>: when a customer or an internal audience needs a document or a deck made for them. <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-health"><code>customer-health</code></a>: before a business review, or when an account feels off.</p></details>
   <h3 style="margin-top:34px">How to read a rating</h3>
   <ul>
    <li><strong>Core.</strong> Close to how the skill was written.</li><li><strong>Useful.</strong> Works, with part of it idle.</li><li><strong>Occasional.</strong> A narrow case, or it needs data this motion rarely keeps.</li><li><strong>Poor fit.</strong> Built around a record or a process this motion usually lacks.</li>
    <li><strong>Frequency.</strong> How often the person a skill suits would reach for it: daily, weekly, monthly, or quarterly or less. It is a first guess from how the skill is designed. A poor fit is given none.</li>
    <li><strong>Outside the grid.</strong> <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a> is each person's own setup run, so it is not placed.</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="fit-week">Through the week</h2>
   <p>A skill becomes a habit when it has a fixed moment. The first list sorts the skills rated core or useful for the chosen role and sales motion by how often they are likely to be used, with those used only when something calls for them at the end. The routines under it put a few skills in the order a day calls for them.</p>
   <p class="note">The routines and their order are ours, as a suggested way to work. Each step after the first is tied to the one before it by a line in one of the two skills' files: a handoff, a suggestion or a named source. The step says which, with the condition the file gives.</p>
   <div class="cad" id="cad"><div><h3>Daily</h3><div class="chips" data-cad="4"><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-daily-briefing">daily-briefing<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><a records</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-inbox-sweep">inbox-sweep<span class="vh">, writes for customers</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-end-of-day">end-of-day<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-signals">deal-signals<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></div></div><div><h3>Weekly</h3><div class="chips" data-cad="3"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a How do I respond?</span><small class="lk">Suggested by competitive-intelligence: for a specific pushback.</small></li><li><span class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-draft-outreach">draft-outreach<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></span><span class="sayt">Draft outreach to <span class="slot">[person]</span> at <span class="slot">[company]</span>.</span><small class="lk">Handed on by handle-objection: for the written reply, by its write line.</small></li></ol></div><div class="routine" data-routine="renewal" data-roles="am founder"><h3>A renewal in view</h3><p class="rwhen">Some months ahead of a renewal date. <span class="who">For account manager or CSM and founder or full-cycle seller.</span></p><h4>Read the account</h4><ol class="chain"><li><span class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-renewal-radar">renewal-radar<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></span><span class="sayt">What renewals are coming up?</span></li><li><span class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-customer-health">customer-health<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></span><span class="sayt">How healthy is <span class="slot">[account]</span>?</span><small class="lk">Draws on renewal-radar: for how close the renewal is.</small></li></ol><h4>Look for growth</h4><ol class="chain"><li><span class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-renewal-radar">renewal-radar<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></span><span class="sayt">What renewals are coming up?</span></li><li><span class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-expansion-whitespace">expansion-whitespace<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></span><span class="sayt">Where can I expand <span class="slot">[account]</span>?</span><small class="lk">Handed on by renewal-radar: for the full pass on growth.</small></li></ol></div></div>
  </div>
 </section>
</div>

<!-- ============================================================ BEFORE ROLLOUT -->
<div id="rollout" data-view="rollout" data-group="sales" data-title="Claude Sales plugin">
 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>Before you roll it out</h1>
   <p class="lede">A rep can get into trouble with a good skill by using it in place of the system the business bought for that job. The plugin does not know what else you own. Two checks come before a rollout: where each skill meets a tool you already run, and whether your records give it enough to read. One setting comes with them, which is the model a skill runs on.</p>
   <p class="sub">Compiled with Claude, an Anthropic model, about an Anthropic plugin. The sorting and the model advice in this part are our read. The editor has changed some of both, and his review of the rest is still to come.</p>
   <div class="subnav"><a href="#rule">The rule</a><a href="#overlap">This plugin, sorted</a><a href="#readiness">Readiness check</a><a href="#models">Which model</a><a href="#setups">Eight setups</a></div>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap g75">
   <div>
    <h2 id="rule">A rule for any assistant that can act in your tools</h2>
    <p class="read">Sort what the assistant can do into three groups before anyone uses it. The groups are worded for any provider's sales skills, and the rest of this part applies them to one.</p>
    <ol class="wk">
     <li><span><b>Agree the rule first.</b> Anything that can change a record or put words in front of a customer. Decide where the official version lives, and who may let the assistant write there.</span></li>
     <li><span><b>Know the overlap.</b> Anything that changes no record and writes to no customer by its own steps, but gives a second view of a figure another tool or team already reports. Use it to prepare, and quote the official tool.</span></li>
     <li><span><b>Start here.</b> Anything that reads and proposes, and competes with nothing you run. These are the low-risk pilots, as long as the tools you grant them cannot send or write.</span></li>
    </ol>
   </div>
   <div class="tests">
    <h3>Our read; tests to run</h3>
    <p>The three groups are our synthesis from one provider's plugin so far. They are not a finding in the evidence register.</p>
    <ol class="wk">
     <li><span>List every tool the assistant can write to, and name one owner for each.</span></li>
     <li><span>For each official figure, such as the forecast, a health score or a lead score, name the one system that holds it.</span></li>
    </ol>
    <p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">The same sort gets run on the next provider entry. If the groups do not hold there, this section changes.</p>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec soft tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="overlap">This plugin, sorted by that rule</h2>
   <p>The first group follows each file's own write line. Which of the other two a skill falls in, and which tool it might collide with, is our read.</p>
   <ul class="tiers"><li><div><h3>Agree the rule first</h3><p>The 13 skills whose own write line lists a change to a record, and the three that write for customers: <a class="skl" href="#skill-inbox-sweep"><code>inbox-sweep</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-handle-objection"><code>handle-objection</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-create-an-asset"><code>create-an-asset</code></a>. Decide where the official version lives, and who may send, before people use these.</p></div><div><p class="n">16 skills</p><div class="chips" style="margin-top:8px"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-call-summary">call-summary<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-inbox-sweep">inbox-sweep<span class="vh">, writes for customers</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-stakeholder-map">stakeholder-map<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-draft-outreach">draft-outreach<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-route-lead">route-lead<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-account-plan">account-plan<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-expansion-whitespace">expansion-whitespace<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-close-plan">close-plan<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-handle-objection">handle-objection<span class="vh">, writes for customers</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-create-an-asset">create-an-asset<span class="vh">, writes for customers</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-update-opportunity">update-opportunity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-forecast">forecast<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-customer-health">customer-health<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-renewal-radar">renewal-radar<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></div></div></li><li><div><h3>Know the overlap</h3><p>None of these lists a change to a record.    <h3 style="margin-top:38px">By the tool you already own</h3>
   <p>Find the tools you have. Each row names the skills that work the same ground, what goes wrong when both are used with no rule and a rule to start from. Vendor names appear only where the plugin's own connector list gives them.</p>
   <div class="scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Skills by the tool you may already own"><table class="ovt"><thead><tr><th scope="col">A tool you may own</th><th scope="col">Skills that touch it</th><th scope="col">What goes wrong</th><th scope="col">A working rule</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">Forecasting and deal scoring<small>Where the forecast is submitted and deals are scored: a forecasting tool or the CRM&#x27;s own forecast.</small></th><td><div class="chips"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-forecast">forecast<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-review">deal-review<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-advance-gap">deal-advance-gap<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-signals">deal-signals<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario">deal-slip-scenario<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-pipeline-review">pipeline-review<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-team-pipeline">team-pipeline<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a></div></td><td>Two numbers for the same quarter. A manager reading a rep&#x27;s activity without the team knowing.</td><td>One dashboard is the number. Tell the team which skills managers use and what those skills read.</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
   <div class="g2" style="margin-top:38px">
    <div>
     <h3>What the plugin's files tell Claude to do about it</h3>
     <ul class="princ">
      <li><b>Permissions sit on each connector tool</b>Each can be allowed, set to ask first or blocked, and the files tell Claude to stay inside that. If a write is blocked, the skill is to hand over a checklist to apply by hand, and never to try another tool to make the change.</li>
      <li><b>Asked for is done, suggested is shown</b>The files tell Claude to make a change you request, and to show a change it proposes first, with its evidence, for you to accept.</li>
      <li><b>Outside content is information</b>The files tell Claude never to treat email, transcripts, chat or enrichment data as instructions. If an email asks for something to be sent or changed, the skill is to show you that request before anything happens.</li>
      <li><b>Scheduled runs stay in their lane</b>A scheduled run is to take only the actions it was set up to take, within what its connectors allow, and nothing that outside content dictates or asks for. In eight skills a value, a record or a contact taken from a transcript, an email, a chat or enrichment is never written on a schedule, even when the schedule was set up to make that kind of update: <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-summary"><code>call-summary</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-stakeholder-map"><code>stakeholder-map</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-plan"><code>account-plan</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-expansion-whitespace"><code>expansion-whitespace</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-crm-hygiene-check"><code>crm-hygiene-check</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-health"><code>customer-health</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-renewal-radar"><code>renewal-radar</code></a>.</li>
      <li><b>An empty result is not widened</b>When a search of your own records comes back empty, the skill is to stop and ask, and never to widen silently to the whole company.</li>
      <li><b>The forecast skill stops short of submitting</b>Its file says that step belongs to your forecasting tool.</li>
      <li><b>The asset skill names its sources</b>Claims are to come from approved material, your account data or what you supply, and the rest is labeled unverified.</li>
     </ul>
     <p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">These are instructions to a model, restated from the files. We did not test whether they hold in a live run.</p>
    </div>
    <div>
     <h3>What it leaves to you</h3>
     <div class="box">
      <p>The plugin cannot know that your forecast belongs in another tool, or that a routing tool should own assignment. Someone has to tell the team.</p>
      <p>Its safeguards are written instructions to the model. The dependable control is the permission on each connector tool, which an admin sets. For scheduled runs, the plugin's README advises ask first for every tool that sends, posts or writes.</p>
      <p>The files leave three questions open, for whoever runs your Claude workspace: who can see a page once it is shared, how long uploaded records are kept and who corrects a post that turns out to be wrong.</p>
      <p class="go"><a href="#seats">Who sets those permissions, and how</a></p>
     </div>
    </div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>
 <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="readiness">Is your organization set up for it?</h2>
   <p>Every skill runs with nothing connected, from an upload or from what you paste. How much it has to read is another matter. Set the switches to match your business and see where each skill would stand.</p>
   <p class="note jsonly">The switches start from a sketch of one sales motion. Each sketch is an illustration, not data, so change any switch. Your settings stay in this browser.</p>
   <p class="note nojs">The switches need scripts to run. The table below gives the same information in a fixed form.</p>
   <div class="jsonly">
    <div class="presets chips" role="group" aria-label="Start from a sketch"><span class="l">Start from a sketch</span><button type="button" data-preset="all" aria-pressed="false">Everything in place</button><button type="button" data-preset="ent" aria-pressed="false">Complex new business</button><button type="button" data-preset="vel" aria-pressed="false">High-velocity and inbound</button><button type="button" data-preset="am" aria-pressed="false">Account management and renewals</button><button type="button" data-preset="svc" aria-pressed="false">Professional services</button><button type="button" data-preset="fin" aria-pressed="false">Client advisory</button><button type="button" data-preset="mfg" aria-pressed="false">Industrial and distribution</button><button type="button" data-preset="none" aria-pressed="false">Nothing connected</button></div>
    <div class="rd" id="rd">
     <div class="swgroups" id="switches"><fieldset class="swg" data-g="tools"><legend>Your tools</legend><p>Can Claude reach them? Without a tool, the skill works from an export or from what you paste.</p><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-crm" data-k="crm" checked><label for="sw-crm">A CRM that Claude can connect to</label><small>HubSpot, Salesforce, Close and Monday are included. The plugin lists Pipedrive, Attio, Zoho CRM and Copper as other options.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-mail" data-k="mail" checked><label for="sw-mail">Work email on Google Workspace or Microsoft 365</label><small>Gmail and Microsoft 365 are included.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-cal" data-k="cal" checked><label for="sw-cal">A work calendar on Google Workspace or Microsoft 365</label><small>Google Calendar, Calendly and Microsoft 365 are included.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-rec" data-k="rec" checked><label for="sw-rec">Recorded calls, or meeting notes saved as documents</label><small>A call recorder, or notes documents a connector can read.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-chat" data-k="chat" checked><label for="sw-chat">Team chat on Slack or Microsoft Teams</label><small>Used for deal-desk threads, escalations and team posts.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-docs" data-k="docs" checked><label for="sw-docs">A document store that holds your approved content</label><small>Google Drive, Notion, Confluence and SharePoint are included.</small></div></fieldset><fieldset class="swg" data-g="records"><legend>Your records</legend><p>Does the data exist at all? A skill cannot read what nobody keeps.</p><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-opps" data-k="opps" checked><label for="sw-opps">Deals are records with a stage, an amount and a close date</label><small>Whatever you call them: opportunities, deals or your own name.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-acts" data-k="acts" checked><label for="sw-acts">Calls and meetings are logged against the account</label><small>As tasks or as calendar-style events. The plugin reads which.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-roles" data-k="roles" checked><label for="sw-roles">Contacts are attached to deals with a role</label><small>Champion, economic buyer and so on.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-hist" data-k="hist" checked><label for="sw-hist">The CRM keeps stage and close-date history</label><small>Needed to see what slipped and how long a deal sat in a stage.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-renew" data-k="renew" checked><label for="sw-renew">Renewal dates live in a known place</label><small>Renewal deals, contract records or a date field.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-prod" data-k="prod" checked><label for="sw-prod">What each customer has bought is recorded</label><small>Line items, or won deals marked by product.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-leads" data-k="leads" checked><label for="sw-leads">Inbound leads arrive as records</label><small>A lead object or a queue, not only an inbox.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-loss" data-k="loss" checked><label for="sw-loss">Closed deals carry a loss reason and a competitor</label><small>The raw material for win-loss and competitive patterns.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-hier" data-k="hier" checked><label for="sw-hier">The CRM knows who reports to whom</label><small>A manager field or a role hierarchy.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-health" data-k="health" checked><label for="sw-health">Health, usage or support cases are visible</label><small>In CRM fields or in documents a connector can read.</small></div></fieldset><fieldset class="swg" data-g="decisions"><legend>Your decisions</legend><p>Is it written down? Where it is not, a skill reads what it can from your tools or uses a labeled default, and asks only where the answer changes the result.</p><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-icp" data-k="icp" checked><label for="sw-icp">Your ideal customer profile is written down</label><small>Industries, size, buyer titles and disqualifiers.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-exit" data-k="exit" checked><label for="sw-exit">Stage names and exit criteria are written down</label><small>What must be true to leave each stage, and the fields each stage requires.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-qual" data-k="qual" checked><label for="sw-qual">A qualification method is written down</label><small>BANT, MEDDIC or your own, with the buying roles it names.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-fcat" data-k="fcat" checked><label for="sw-fcat">Forecast categories are mapped to your stages</label><small>Which stages count as commit and which as best case.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-quota" data-k="quota" checked><label for="sw-quota">Quota is known, for you and for each rep</label><small>The target that a forecast, a coverage check or a what-if is measured against.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-rules" data-k="rules" checked><label for="sw-rules">Lead routing rules are written down</label><small>Territory, size bands, named accounts and who wins a tie.</small></div><div class="sw"><input type="checkbox" id="sw-catalog" data-k="catalog" checked><label for="sw-catalog">A product catalog with typical deal sizes exists</label><small>Products or product families and what each usually sells for.</small></div></fieldset></div>
     <div class="rdres" id="rd-results" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Where each skill stands at these settings"><h3>Where each skill stands</h3><p class="note">At the settings on this page. The reasons are our read.</p><div role="status" aria-live="polite" class="vh" id="rd-say"></div><details class="lvx" open><summary><b id="t0">0</b><span>Little to read<small>It is built around a record you do not keep.</small></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary><ul id="lv0"></ul></details><details class="lvx" open><summary><b id="t1">0</b><span>From files<small>Claude cannot reach its main tool. Upload an export or paste.</small></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary><ul id="lv1"></ul></details><details class="lvx" open><summary><b id="t2">0</b><span>Works, with gaps<small>Its main source is there. Something that would sharpen it is missing.</small></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary><ul id="lv2"></ul></details><details class="lvx"><summary><b id="t3">0</b><span>Inputs in place<small>The kinds of input you ticked are there. That is not a check of your records, your permissions or the skill.</small></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary><ul id="lv3" class="flat"></ul></details></div>
    </div>
    <div class="rdpin jsonly" id="rdpin" aria-hidden="true"><span><b id="p3">0</b>inputs in place</span><span><b id="p2">0</b>with gaps</span><span><b id="p1">0</b>from files</span><span><b id="p0">0</b>little to read</span><a href="#rd-results" tabindex="-1">Results</a></div>
   </div>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:18px">What each skill leans on is our read of its steps. The plugin keeps no such list. The check looks at inputs only: it tests no permission, no routing rule and no result. For a first pilot, name an owner, a handful of real cases, the figure or record to compare the output with, and what counts as a pass.</p>
   <details class="more plan"><summary>A sample plan for a first pilot of a skill that can reach a customer</summary>
    <p>This is a sketch for <a class="skl" href="#skill-schedule-meeting"><code>schedule-meeting</code></a>, which can put an invitation in a customer's inbox. Change the parts to fit your own tools. What the skill is to do is restated from its file. The cases, the pass and the stop rule are our read, as a place to start.</p>
    <dl class="setups">
     <div><dt>Owner</dt><dd>One person who signs off the pilot, and one who checks each run against the calendar and the CRM.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>Cases</dt><dd>Up to 10 real requests. Include a follow-up with one contact, an account with two open deals, a contact with no email address on the record, a request made while the calendar's write tool is blocked and the same request made a second time.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>What the file says to expect</dt><dd>With two open deals, it asks which one the meeting belongs to. With no address on the record, it shows one from the header of an email thread as unverified, and uses it once you pick it. With the write tool blocked, it gives the invite details as text to paste, quotes the refusal and neither retries nor reaches for another tool. The file does not say what happens when you ask a second time, so check for a duplicate.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>What to compare</dt><dd>After a direct booking, the calendar: one event at the time agreed, 30 minutes long, or 60 for a demo or a quarterly business review, unless you said otherwise, with attendees taken only from your own words, the CRM or an address you picked from a thread. The customer's inbox: one invitation, since creating the event emails every attendee. The CRM, when you asked for the meeting to be logged or booked end to end: one event or activity on the related deal or account, saved as it was shown.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>Who puts it right</dt><dd>Name who corrects a wrong invitation or a duplicate record, and who tells the customer.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>A pass</dt><dd>Every case gives the result its kind calls for. A direct booking: right on all three counts above. A blocked write: the invite details as text to paste, and nothing created in the calendar, the inbox or the CRM. Two open deals: it asks which one and waits for your answer. No address on the record: it uses only an address you picked. A repeat: count what was created, since the file sets no check for duplicates. A wrong attendee fails the case, whatever else is right.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>When to stop</dt><dd>At a wrong attendee, a meeting logged to the wrong deal or a booking made while the tool was blocked. Find the cause before the next run.</dd></div>
     <div><dt>Not known until you measure it</dt><dd>The time a booking saves, how long an approval takes with the tools set to ask first, what it costs and whether more meetings are held.</dd></div>
    </dl>
   </details>

 <section class="sec soft tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="models">Which model to run a skill on</h2>
   <p>The plugin does not choose a model. None of its 36 skill files names one, so a skill runs on whichever model is selected in Claude at the moment you ask. The model is a setting for your team to decide, and it changes three things: how much a skill catches, how long you wait and what the reply looks like.</p>
   <div class="g2" style="margin-top:26px">
    <div>
     <h3>What Anthropic says</h3>
     <ul class="princ">
      <li><b>Opus 5.5 is the usual place to start</b>Anthropic's guide to choosing a model starts most work there.</li>
      <li><b>Sonnet 5.5 for everyday work</b>The guide pairs it with speed: writing, analysis and agent tasks of an ordinary day.</li>
      <li><b>Haiku 5.5 for volume</b>It has the lowest latency and price, and suits high-volume and real-time work.</li>
      <li><b>Fable 5.2 at the top</b>The guide calls it the most capable model open to all customers, and points it at long agent sessions and deep research.</li>
      <li><b>Effort is a second dial</b>Within one model, an effort setting trades quality against time and cost. The guide's advice is to try that dial first, since it often does more than a change of model.</li>
     </ul>
     <p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">From <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/choosing-a-model">Anthropic's guide to choosing a model</a>, read on November 21, 2026. Anthropic is the vendor, and the guide is written for developers. It does not mention the plugin.</p>
    </div>
    <div>
     <h3>What the plugin's files say</h3>
     <div class="box mbox">
      <p>Nothing about models. No skill file names a model or an effort level, and neither does the README or the connector list.</p>
      <p>The files do describe what comes back, and they tie it to what is connected: a rendered page where one helps, and paste-ready text or a checklist where the skill cannot write.</p>
     </div>
    </div>
   </div>
   <h3 style="margin-top:38px">A rule to start from</h3>
   <p>Sort each skill by what a mistake would cost and by how much judgment it needs. This is our read as of November 2026, and model names date quickly.</p>
   <ul class="mtiers"><li><div><h3>It changes records or writes for customers</h3><p>A slip costs more than a few seconds of waiting. Start on the stronger model, with every tool it can write or send through set to ask first. This rests on what an error would cost, and it is unmeasured: the test below did not cover writes.</p></div><div><p class="n">Opus 5.5 to start, 16 skills</p><div class="chips" style="margin-top:8px"><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-call-summary">call-summary<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-C" href="#skill-inbox-sweep">inbox-sweep<span href="#skill-log-activity">log-activity<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-stakeholder-map">stakeholder-map<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-draft-outreach">draft-outreach<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-route-lead">route-lead<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-account-plan">account-plan<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-forecast">forecast<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-customer-health">customer-health<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a><a class="chip k-W" href="#skill-renewal-radar">renewal-radar<span class="vh">, changes records</span></a></div></div></li><li><div><h3>It weighs several sources and makes a call</h3><p>A review, a diagnosis or a pattern across many deals. The one skill of this kind in the test below scored the same on every model, so the rule starts these on the faster model. That rests on one skill, run once.</p></div><div><p class="n">Sonnet 5.5 to start, 7 skills</p><div class="chips" style="margin-top:8px"><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-review">deal-review<span proposes</span></a></div></div></li><li><div><h3>A scoped job with a clear input</h3><p>One account, one meeting or one question.</p></div><div><p class="n">Sonnet 5.5 to start, 11 skills</p><div class="chips" style="margin-top:8px"><a class="chip k-S" href="#skill-setup">setup<span class="vh">, the first run</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-daily-briefing">daily-briefing<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-call-prep">call-prep<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-end-of-day">end-of-day<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-weekly-wrap">weekly-wrap<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-account-research">account-research<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-account-context">account-context<span class="vh">, can proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-P" href="#skill-rep-context">rep-context<span class="vh">, can share its output</span></a></div></div></li><li><div><h3>A repeated pass that returns a list you can check</h3><p>The same check on every deal, often on a schedule. Try the smallest model once a run has matched your own rules and records.</p></div><div><p class="n">Sonnet 5.5 to start, 2 skills</p><div class="chips" style="margin-top:8px"><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-deal-signals">deal-signals<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a><a class="chip k-R" href="#skill-crm-hygiene-check">crm-hygiene-check<span class="vh">, reads and proposes</span></a></div></div></li></ul>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:14px">Fable 5.2 sits above all four rows. Keep it for the few jobs that run rarely and read a great deal, such as <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a> or <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-plan"><code>account-plan</code></a>, when a wait of several minutes is acceptable. We did not test it on either of those two.</p>
   <h3 id="models-test" style="margin-top:38px">A small test</h3>
   <p>We ran six of the skills on four Claude models, once each, on invented files that hold known answers. It is a pilot: 24 runs in all, enough to show where to look and too few to settle anything.</p>
   <ul class="princ">
    <li><b>The files</b>One invented company: a pipeline export of 24 deals, three call transcripts, an inbox of 10 messages, 12 inbound leads and a page of company facts. Problems were planted in them, such as three deals past their close date and an email that asks for a contract amount to be changed.</li>
    <li><b>The runs</b>On November 21, 2026, each model got the same skill file, the same request and the same files in a session of its own, with no tool connected. Effort was left at each model's default.</li>
    <li><b>The marking</b>Two separate Claude sessions marked each reply against the answer key. They saw the replies under code names and were not told which model wrote which.</li>
    <li><b>What was marked</b>Planted items found, numbers right and guardrails kept, such as promising no go-live date in a customer email and claiming no write that did not happen. Time and length were recorded by the run.</li>
   </ul>
   <div class="scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="The test in sum, by model"><table class="rt pt sum stack wide"><thead><tr><th scope="col">Model</th><th scope="col">Planted items found</th><th scope="col">Numbers in the key right</th><th scope="col">Guardrails kept</th><th scope="col">Slips the markers noted</th><th scope="col">Time for one run</th><th scope="col">What came back</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">Haiku 5.5</th><td data-l="Items found">66.25 of 68</td><td data-l="Numbers in the key">10 of 10</td><td data-l="Guardrails kept">19 of 21</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Slips">Kept a skill&#x27;s default over the company&#x27;s rule. Credited a competitor&#x27;s claim to the customer. Both cost marks.</td><td data-l="Time for a run">45 s to 79 s</td><td class="fmt" data-l="What came back">A short reply with a separate page or document in one of six runs, and everything in one reply in the other five. 638 to 1,353 words in all.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Sonnet 5.5</th><td data-l="Items found">68 of 68</td><td data-l="Numbers in the key">10 of 10</td><td data-l="Guardrails kept">21 of 21</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Slips">One wrong total in a summary table. It is outside the key, so no mark was lost.</td><td data-l="Time for a run">28 s to 54 s</td><td class="fmt" data-l="What came back">A short reply with a separate page or document in four of six runs, and everything in one reply in the other two. 1,009 to 1,586 words in all.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Opus 5.5</th><td data-l="Items found">68 of 68</td><td data-l="Numbers in the key">10 of 10</td><td data-l="Guardrails kept">21 of 21</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Slips">Two wrong figures in one caveat. They are outside the key, so no mark was lost.</td><td data-l="Time for a run">61 s to 1 min 59 s</td><td class="fmt" data-l="What came back">A short reply with a separate page or document, every time. 1,553 to 2,270 words in all.</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Fable 5.2</th><td data-l="Items found">68 of 68</td><td data-l="Numbers in the key">10 of 10</td><td data-l="Guardrails kept">21 of 21</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Slips">None found.</td><td data-l="Time for a run">12 min 56 s to 20 min 05 s</td><td class="fmt" data-l="What came back">A short reply with a separate page or document, every time. 2,055 to 3,109 words in all.</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:10px">Summed over the six skills. An item's mark is the mean of two markers, and a partly found item counts as half. One item of the key was wrong and was corrected on November 22, 2026, which raised each model's total by half a mark. The numbers column counts only the figures in the key. The slips column says of each slip whether it cost a mark: the two by Haiku 5.5 did, and the figures that Sonnet 5.5 and Opus 5.5 added sit outside the key. Time runs from a session's first step to its last, in our test harness and not in the Claude app.</p>
   <h3 style="margin-top:34px">What the test suggests</h3>
   <ul class="princ"><li><b>The planted problems did not separate the three larger models</b>Sonnet 5.5, Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.2 each found all 68. Haiku 5.5 found 66.25. Every number in the key was right. On work this well defined, from clean files, the three tied and the smallest model came close.</li><li><b>Speed was the widest gap</b>One run took Sonnet 5.5 under a minute and Haiku 5.5 little more. Opus 5.5 took 1 to 2 minutes. Fable 5.2 took 13 to 20 minutes and made several times as many tool calls. A wait of that length suits a quarterly review, not a skill used daily.</li><li><b>The slips were in the details</b>Haiku 5.5 kept the hygiene skill's 14-day default where the company's own rule said 30 days, and in a call summary it credited a competitor's claim to the customer. Sonnet 5.5 got one total wrong in a summary table, and Opus 5.5 got an amount and a ratio wrong in one caveat. Both were figures the models added beyond the request, so neither cost a mark. The markers found no such slip in Fable 5.2's six replies.</li><li><b>The guardrails mostly held</b>All four models showed the rep an email that asked for a contract amount to be changed, and none acted on it. None claimed a write or a send, and none named the competitor to the customer. One Haiku 5.5 draft put the customer's go-live date in writing, against the company's rule.</li><li><b>One skill came back in different shapes</b>Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.2 gave a short reply with a separate page or document every time. Haiku 5.5 put everything into one long reply in five runs of six. Sonnet 5.5 did each. The model shapes the output as well as the skill does.</li><li><b>What it does to the starting rule</b>It supports Sonnet 5.5 for scoped and repeated work: on these six it scored the same as Opus 5.5 in about half the time. It supports checking a Haiku 5.5 run against your own rules before trusting it. It gave no measured reason to start a skill that weighs several sources on Opus 5.5: the one skill of that kind we tested, pipeline-review, scored the same on every model. So since November 23, 2026 the rule starts those seven on Sonnet 5.5, by the editor's decision. The other tested skill that only reads, crm-hygiene-check, is a repeated pass, where Haiku 5.5 found 14 of 15 items and the other three found all 15. Nor does the test give a reason to prefer Fable 5.2. On skills that can write it says nothing, because nothing was connected. We keep Opus 5.5 for those as a place to start and step down from. That rests on what a mistake would cost, not on a difference we measured.</li></ul>
   <h3 style="margin-top:34px">What it does not show</h3>
   <ul class="lim"><li><strong>One run each.</strong> A second run could come out differently, so no single result means much.</li><li><strong>Too easy at the top.</strong> The planted problems could not tell three of the four models apart. A harder test would plant conflicts between sources and calls that need judgment.</li><li><strong>From files only.</strong> Nothing was connected, so no write, no send and no live record was tested. That is where a wrong choice of model costs most.</li><li><strong>A test harness, not the Claude app.</strong> Effort was whatever each session defaulted to, and Anthropic's guide lists a higher default for Fable 5.2 and Sonnet 5.5 than for Opus 5.5 and Haiku 5.5. Times in the app will differ.</li><li><strong>One vendor's models marked each other.</strong> Two Claude sessions marked every reply separately, and on that first marking they agreed on 390 of 396 marks. The answer key is made of checkable facts, and the slips named above were checked by hand against the test files.</li><li><strong>Two flaws in our own test, one now corrected.</strong> One line of the invented company's stage rules can be read two ways. Three models read it one way and Fable 5.2 the other, and the key counted neither. The key also expected a draft reply to a set of legal redlines, where the skill's own file says to flag legal mail and not draft it. All four models flagged it, and the first marking took half a mark from each. A model from another lab caught the error on November 22, 2026. The totals here count the item as found. The markers' own marks are kept with the files, and the corrected mark was set by Claude, not by the two markers.</li></ul>

   <p class="note" style="margin-top:16px">The test files, the answer key, all 24 replies and the marks travel with this page, under <a href="kit/modeltest/README.md"><code>kit/modeltest</code></a>, so anyone can mark them again or rerun the test.</p>

 <section class="sec tight">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="setups">Eight setups worth checking</h2>
   <dl class="setups">
    <div><dt>We do not call them opportunities</dt><dd>The skills take stage and field names from your CRM instead of assuming one vendor's. HubSpot's deals, for example, are checked on their own properties. From an upload, a skill uses your own column headers.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>Our deals live in a custom object</dt><dd>Nothing in the skill files describes standing in another object for deals. Expect to say so in your project instructions, or to work from an export. This is our read of the files and was not tested.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>We do not track deals at all</dt><dd>Thirteen skills are built around a deal with a stage, an amount and a close date, and have little to read without one: <a class="skl" href="#skill-weekly-wrap"><code>weekly-wrap</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-review"><code>deal-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-advance-gap"><code>deal-advance-gap</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-signals"><code>deal-signals</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-slip-scenario"><code>deal-slip-scenario</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-close-plan"><code>close-plan</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-update-opportunity"><code>update-opportunity</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-pipeline-review"><code>pipeline-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-crm-hygiene-check"><code>crm-hygiene-check</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-team-pipeline"><code>team-pipeline</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-rep-context"><code>rep-context</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-win-loss-review"><code>win-loss-review</code></a>. That grouping is ours. Others lose a part: <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-context"><code>account-context</code></a> names its deals section as having no data, <a class="skl" href="#skill-daily-briefing"><code>daily-briefing</code></a> shows leads in that row when you upload a lead backlog, and <a class="skl" href="#skill-competitive-intelligence"><code>competitive-intelligence</code></a> loses its counts of wins and losses.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>We log client meetings as events</dt><dd>Two skills check this for themselves. <a class="skl" href="#skill-log-activity"><code>log-activity</code></a> reads from the CRM whether meetings are logged as tasks or as calendar-style events, and <a class="skl" href="#skill-schedule-meeting"><code>schedule-meeting</code></a> whether they are calendar-style events or activities. Each drafts the shape your setup expects.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>Our renewals are not deals</dt><dd><a class="skl" href="#skill-renewal-radar"><code>renewal-radar</code></a> accepts renewal deals, contract records or a renewal date field. It reads which you use from the CRM and from what it already knows about your company, and asks once only if neither tells it.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>Our customers are households</dt><dd><a class="skl" href="#skill-lead-triage"><code>lead-triage</code></a> marks industry and company size as not applicable for a consumer lead, and scores the dimensions that do apply. Scoring on intent alone is its rule for a team with no ICP recorded. <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-research"><code>account-research</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-account-tiering"><code>account-tiering</code></a> score fit on industry, size and buyer titles, so they have less to go on.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>Our meeting notes sit in a shared drive</dt><dd>Four skills say that the Google Drive connector cannot see shared drives, or may not: <a class="skl" href="#skill-setup"><code>setup</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-prep"><code>call-prep</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-summary"><code>call-summary</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-customer-voice"><code>customer-voice</code></a>. Each is to name the gap and offer a paste or an upload. Notes kept there are missed until you paste or upload them.</dd></div>
    <div><dt>We run on Salesforce and Microsoft 365</dt><dd>Both are included. Each needs an admin to enable it before anyone can sign in.</dd></div>
   </dl>
  </div>
 </section>
</div>

  <div class="wrap g75">
   <div>
    <h1>Configure it for your company</h1>
    <p class="lede">Out of the box, the Claude Sales plugin knows nothing about your company, so its skills infer what they can, fall back on a labeled default or ask. Configure it once and they work from your ICP, your stages and your wording rules. This guide starts with what you get, role by role, then shows how to set it up.</p>
    <div class="acts"><a class="btn pri" href="#outcome">See what you get</a><a class="btn" href="#for-claude">Hand it to Claude</a></div>
    <p class="who"><span>A guide by <b>Nathan Fierley</b></span><span>For sellers, operators and admins</span><span>About 25 minutes to read</span><span>Checked against Sales plugin 2.1.0 on November 16, 2026</span></p>
   </div>

 </section>
