<details class="gsk" id="gpt-find-key-internal-sources">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>find-key-internal-sources</code></span><span class="tg">Who knows, and what to read</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Points a seller to the colleague, document or channel that the evidence says is the right place to get a customer&#x27;s question answered.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>When it is unclear who inside the company knows, who owns or approves the answer, which document is authoritative, or where to escalate.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Ask</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="gask-find-key-internal-sources">@Sales who owns the answer on data residency, and what should I read first?</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="gask-find-key-internal-sources">Copy</button></p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>A specific question, topic or problem from a customer, or a support task. Optional: the account, the kind of route wanted, a quick or deep search, privacy scope, the number of results, a document type and known names or aliases.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>Ranked experts, documents and channels, a first message the seller can copy, and the gaps in coverage. A quick search returns up to three of each. A deep one returns five to eight where the evidence supports them.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads</dt><dd>Knowledge bases and file stores, for pages that are kept up to date, who owns them, FAQs, runbooks, field guides and recorded decisions. Internal messaging, for what a channel is about, who is active in it and how issues get escalated. The CRM, for the account or deal and whatever is blocking it.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>Nothing. It only reads: no post, no assignment and no change to any system. A later option is a routing note to reuse. Where that would be saved is not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>The suggested first message is drafted in the chat and is not posted. No approval step for saving a routing note is stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It trusts documented ownership over a mention in chat, and owning an account in the CRM does not make someone the owner of an answer. It searches public channels unless the user asks for private ones and has access. It invents no people or links and escalates nothing by itself.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>One topic, question or support task, with or without an account.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Supplied documents, messages and what is already known. Where support is thin it names the smallest missing source and assigns no owner.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Product names, team names, abbreviations, old names, wiki hubs and internal process terms that the user gives or the sources show. Its own guidance says company-specific additions belong outside the plugin&#x27;s package.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd>Not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>GTM job <small>Our read</small></dt><dd>Account research and call preparation</dd></div>
</dl>

<div class="ggrp"><h3 id="gpt-g-meetings">Meetings and calls <small>Before a meeting, after a call and what customers said.</small></h3>
<details class="gsk" id="gpt-prepare-for-meeting">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>prepare-for-meeting</code></span><span class="tg">Prepare for a meeting</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span><span class="t">Can be scheduled</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Prepares one commercial meeting, lines up a day of them or builds a customer presentation out of the preparation.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>A coming meeting with a customer, a prospect or a partner, a renewal or opportunity meeting, or an important internal one. Also a same-day call queue, a first call, an executive briefing or a workshop. Not for neutral research interviews with nothing commercial in them.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Ask</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="gask-prepare-for-meeting">@Sales prepare me for tomorrow&#x27;s meeting with <span class="slot">[account]</span></span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="gask-prepare-for-meeting">Copy</button></p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>Which meeting, or which date, and the kind of output when that is unclear. The calendar is required only when the meeting has to be found. Optional: the invitation, a link, the account, an attendee, the topic, a goal, a cut-off date for evidence, source documents, a template and a format.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>One brief: date, attendees, summary, goal, open questions, a timed agenda, a recommended stance and gaps. For a day: briefs in time order with watch-outs and suggested closes. A customer deck of 6 to 9 slides when asked.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads</dt><dd>The calendar for which meeting it is, when, the agenda and who is coming. Earlier call transcripts for what was decided and promised. Email for what the customer has asked. Internal messages, notes, strategy documents, assets and templates for the team&#x27;s own view. The CRM for the account, its stakeholders and the deal or renewal in question. A data provider where that adds something.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>It can put the brief into a meeting note you already keep or start a fresh document to read beforehand, offer to attach that to the invitation, draft a short update for chat or email, and build the deck from the brief once you have reviewed it. It can set up a schedule.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>An existing file is updated only on request. Sending, sharing and attaching need authorization. No schedule until the user agrees.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It does not invent attendance, roles or checks of sources, and does not write scripts for facilitating the meeting. It respects a cut-off date. A recurring run may not attach notes, send messages or write to the CRM.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>One meeting, or one seller&#x27;s qualifying meetings on a date. Running it over a named seller&#x27;s calendar as a manager is not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Daily preparation that only reads, in the seller&#x27;s time zone. The instructions also name a scheduled summary and action list after a meeting.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>An invitation or meeting details that you supply, with any notes. It must then say that the meeting came from the user and was not confirmed in the calendar.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>What is known of the account and the deal, your strategy and documents, and the sections, layout and branding of a template you supply.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd><a class="skl" href="#gpt-sales-presentations"><code>sales-presentations</code></a> for the customer deck.</dd></div>
<div><dt>GTM job <small>Our read</small></dt><dd>Account research and call preparation</dd></div>

<details class="gsk" id="gpt-follow-up-after-call">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>follow-up-after-call</code></span><span class="tg">Follow up after a call</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span><span class="t">Can be scheduled</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Works from the record of a finished call to set out what was decided, who owes what and the follow-up to send, or a readout for the customer.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>Once a call is over: with a customer, a prospect or a partner, or an internal call that matters to a deal. It covers discovery, demos, reviews of a pilot or an evaluation, and qualification.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Ask</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="gask-follow-up-after-call">@Sales here are my notes from today&#x27;s call with <span class="slot">[account]</span>. What are the next steps, and draft the follow-up</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="gask-follow-up-after-call">Copy</button></p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>The kind of source and the evidence itself: a transcript, notes or a recording summary. The call has to be identifiable. Optional: the account, the date, the contact, an internal channel, the opportunity, a template and a format.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A call summary, next steps for the customer and the seller with owners and dates, three subject lines and an email of 150 to 220 words, one sentence for the CRM&#x27;s next-step field, a draft for the internal team and the status of each action. Or one focused draft, a document or a readout deck of 6 to 10 slides.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads</dt><dd>The call itself: a transcript, a recording whose text can be fetched or notes. The calendar for when it was and who attended. The CRM for the account, the deal, the contact and who owns them. Email, in case the call or a promised follow-up is recorded there. Internal messages for the agreed place to post a recap. Your files for templates and assets.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>By default the answer stays in the chat, and the CRM sentence is there to copy and paste. On request it updates a document or delivers a new one. <div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>The exact draft and where it would go are reviewed before any approval. The first pass creates no email draft, sends nothing, posts nothing and writes nothing to the CRM. No schedule until the user agrees.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It does not rebuild the conversation from account records, invent recipients, owners, dates, prices or commitments, or write to a customer about an internal-only call without a basis.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>One completed call and its account or opportunity. Use by a manager for a named seller is not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>When the user wants follow-up to continue, or the full package records a firm commitment, decision date or meeting still to come, it can offer to run again for that call or account.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Pasted or uploaded notes, the text of a transcript, a transcript exported from a recording tool or a message thread that documents the call. With too little to go on it lists the calls it might be, or returns an outline plainly marked as incomplete.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>A supplied document template with its branding, how accounts map to opportunities and the confirmed conventions for internal channels.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd><a class="skl" href="#gpt-sales-presentations"><code>sales-presentations</code></a> for the customer readout.</dd></div>

<details class="gsk" id="gpt-find-customer-quotes">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>find-customer-quotes</code></span><span class="tg">Find what customers said</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Pulls customers&#x27; or prospects&#x27; exact words on a theme and shows the evidence for the words and for who said them.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>Voice-of-customer research, testing a theme, objections, product friction, evidence for a use case or quotations from named calls.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Ask</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="gask-find-customer-quotes">@Sales find customer quotes about onboarding time from last quarter&#x27;s calls</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="gask-find-customer-quotes">Copy</button></p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>A theme, an objection, a product area, a use case, a storyline or named calls, with evidence that keeps people&#x27;s direct speech and who was speaking. Optional: a company, a segment, dates, calls and the number of quotes. The default target is five for each theme.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>Quotes grouped by theme, with the number found against the number wanted. Each quote names the speaker or company where known and gives a confidence score, a relevance score, the supporting evidence and its source. Gaps are listed and JSON is available on request. Anything said by a non-customer is labelled apart.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads</dt><dd>Meeting transcripts for the words, who spoke, who attended, the date and a link. The CRM only to identify a company, its customer status or a filter. Pasted or exported transcripts and notes, provided they keep the original wording.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>Nothing at first. The user can later ask for chosen quotes to be packaged.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>Posting, sending, packaging or saving quotes each wait for a later request from the user.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It does not polish, splice, paraphrase or invent a quotation. It wants confidence of at least 0.8 that the speaker is a customer or prospect, and relevance of 0.75 by default. It returns fewer quotes, or none, when the evidence is thin. It gives no legal or compliance approval.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>Any number of themes, across the calls, accounts or segments you name.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>The text of a transcript, an export from a recording tool that includes text or call notes that preserve what was said and by whom. With none of these it asks for the smallest usable source.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Not stated. A company&#x27;s CRM record, status and segment can filter the search.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd>A business case, a competitive brief, meeting preparation or an evidence appendix can use the quotes. It can itself start from a recent call follow-up.</dd></div>
<div><dt>GTM job <small>Our read</small></dt><dd>Coaching and enablement</dd></div>

<div class="body">
<p class="does">Lays out who is involved in a single deal, what has to be decided, what could go wrong and what to do next. It can also plan a first attempt to sell an offer.</p>
<div class="gcard">

<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>Something that identifies the deal: the account, the opportunity, an initiative or your notes. And the decision or step you want help with. For a first sale it is enough to give the offer, the buyer in mind, the pilot or evaluation and the objections heard so far.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A deal snapshot, a deal map, a map of the buying committee, a register of procurement risks, next actions in priority order, gaps in the evidence and notes on what was inferred. Any one part on request.</dd></div>

A data provider or public information only to check a point already raised.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>Nothing at first. Later options: a plan of actions agreed with the buyer, or a tracker, messages to stakeholders drafted for review, and opportunity updates worded for the CRM and drafted for review.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>A CRM update, a message, a recap or any further document each wait for a later request.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It keeps other deals at the same account out of the picture. It does not make up a buying committee, a stage, a price, a date, a security sign-off or a promise, and it marks roles and risks it has inferred as inferences.</dd></div>

<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Deal details that you supply, with the gaps marked. A first-sale plan needs no CRM, named customer, price or earlier discovery call.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Account plans, CRM fields and any deal context supplied. No required sales method is stated.</dd></div>

<details class="gsk" id="gpt-build-business-case">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>build-business-case</code></span><span class="tg">Build a business case</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Builds the reasoning and evidence for a customer&#x27;s investment decision, and puts numbers on value where the inputs support them.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>
<dl class="runkv">

<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>A customer, an account or a generic scenario, with a workflow, an initiative, a pain, a target metric or a decision. A case for a named customer needs authoritative account and commercial context. Optional: the buyer, industry, constraints, current tools, the solution, notes, metrics, usage, pricing, an earlier case, a template and the kind of output.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A summary for executives, what the customer is trying to do and what stands in the way, the workflows and use cases that matter most, where value should come from, the metrics and assumptions, the return logic, what sets the offer apart, proof and caveats. It can be a structural case, a low, base and high model fit for finance, one focused output, or a decision deck of 8 to 12 slides.</dd></div>
</dl>

<dl class="kv">

Workbooks, proposals and templates you supply.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>Only the output asked for, unshared: a decision document or a customer deck when one is requested. A suggested next step is a value-model table laid out for a spreadsheet.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>A request for a document or a deck authorizes that one item only. Otherwise the content is reviewed first, and saving or sharing needs an explicit request. It posts and sends nothing, leaves the CRM and your original template untouched, and creates no file you did not ask for.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It does not invent returns, prices, buyers, approval or evidence that a customer confirmed. Public or analogous proof is kept apart from the customer&#x27;s own facts, and unsupported assumptions stay visible.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>A single customer, initiative or buying decision, or a generic scenario. Use by a manager for a named seller is not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Notes, metrics, links, exports or a generic scenario. Without credible numbers it returns a structural case and names the inputs a finance review would need.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Commercial evidence for the account, proposal terms, customer metrics, proof points and templates.</dd></div>

<details class="gsk" id="gpt-build-competitive-brief">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>build-competitive-brief</code></span><span class="tg">Build a competitive brief</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span><span class="t">Can be scheduled</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Compares competitors from cited sources and advises the seller on what to say, what to check, what needs proof and what not to claim.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>

<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A web page and a summary in the chat. The page sets out the context, the seller&#x27;s own baseline, the landscape, a profile of each competitor, what a comparison matrix shows, how to respond, which claims to avoid and the sources. </dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">

Other research and a data provider to fill gaps.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>By default a self-contained HTML brief, written to the place asked for, the workspace or a temporary folder. A new, unshared Google Slides deck when asked. It can offer a weekly digest on a schedule.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>The default web page needs no extra approval. It asks before overwriting a file the user can see. No schedule until the user agrees.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>Without evidence it does not say a competitor is in a deal. It makes up no prices or capabilities, and a story from the field stays a story until confirmed. The matrix covers five competitors by default and eight at most.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>A single competitor or several, a product or a market, or a single account or deal. Use by a manager for a named seller is not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>A weekly competitive digest that only reads, for the same competitors, product, category or account, after a check for an existing one.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Nothing is required. It works from supplied material and suitable public evidence, and names gaps and weaker evidence.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Your own baseline, positioning and proof, battlecards you already have, the segments you target, objections you know of and a presentation template.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd>Meeting preparation as a possible next step, and Google Slides authoring when a deck is asked for.</dd></div>

<div class="ggrp"><h3 id="gpt-g-pipeline">Pipeline and forecast <small>Standing views of a book, a team and the forecast.</small></h3>
<details class="gsk" id="gpt-seller-account-dashboard">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>seller-account-dashboard</code></span><span class="tg">A seller&#x27;s account dashboard</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Sellers</span><span class="t">Can be scheduled</span><span class="t">For a named seller</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">
<p class="does">Builds or refreshes a seller&#x27;s interactive dashboard of accounts and pipeline, or returns a ranked list of accounts to work.</p>
<div class="gcard">
<div class="runhead"><h3>Use it</h3><p>As ChatGPT reported the skill's instructions. The example request is ours and was not run.</p></div>
<dl class="runkv">
<div><dt>When</dt><dd>A seller wants a home for their accounts, a working view of the pipeline, a ranking of accounts or the week&#x27;s priorities. Not for the fictional demo or for finding new accounts.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Ask</dt><dd><p class="sayline"><span class="sayt" id="gask-seller-account-dashboard">@Sales build my account dashboard and rank this week&#x27;s priorities</span><button class="btn sm jsonly" type="button" data-copy="gask-seller-account-dashboard">Copy</button></p></dd></div>
<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>For a filled dashboard: the seller or an authorized stand-in, the scope and every account the seller owns, including those with no open opportunity. The goal and a design style when unclear. An empty placeholder needs no CRM. Optional: territory, named accounts, ranking criteria, sales motion, capacity and extra views.</dd></div>
<div><dt>You get</dt><dd>A link to a working dashboard with Home, Accounts and Pipeline views, a brief summary, the gaps that matter and a single next step. A ranking alone returns lists to focus on, to monitor and to leave, with the sources and details of the run.</dd></div>
</dl>
</div>
<dl class="kv">
<div><dt>Reads</dt><dd>The CRM for who owns which accounts, their deals, contacts, stage, value and recent activity. The calendar for meetings. Call transcripts for what customers decided or objected to. Email for what was asked, answered and promised. Internal messages for changes and blockers. Your files for account plans, territories, lists and rules on which accounts to leave alone. A data provider for fit and contacts.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>A dashboard, or the ranking, kept as a local project that the user owns. It can refresh the matching dashboard, publish it as a private site and set a refresh schedule. Unless the user declines, the shared dashboard routine also generates a private design image.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>Agreement before any hosting, publishing, outside sharing or change of host. A second local dashboard needs an explicit choice when a matching unpublished one exists. No schedule without explicit approval.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>Nothing on the dashboard is made up: no account, priority, score, value, reply or meeting. A broad dashboard keeps the whole book. It does not treat the demo&#x27;s data as a customer&#x27;s, duplicate outreach or widen access, and it acts in the CRM or sends outreach only with separate authorization.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>One seller&#x27;s book, a stated territory or a named set of accounts. A manager can ask for the dashboard of a named seller they are authorized to see.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Once the dashboard is hosted privately, one refresh that only reads: weekday mornings is the example given. A ranking with a reusable scope can rerun weekly. It checks active and paused schedules for a duplicate.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Authoritative records or an export, within the scope asked for. An empty placeholder needs no CRM. A supplied list with no link gets its ranking in the chat.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Who owns what, territories, target lists, account plans, which accounts to leave alone, how to rank, the sales motion, a seller&#x27;s capacity, what the dashboard is for and design choices saved earlier.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd>ChatGPT Sites for hosting, once authorized.</dd></div>
<div><dt>GTM job <small>Our read</small></dt><dd>Pipeline review and forecasting</dd></div>

<details class="gsk" id="gpt-review-forecast">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>review-forecast</code></span><span class="tg">Review a forecast</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Managers and leaders</span><span class="t">Can be scheduled</span><span class="t">For a named seller</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>
<div class="body">

<dl class="kv">

Email for signs of engagement and for procurement. Internal messages for whatever is stuck with the team, legal or the deal desk.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>Nothing at first. On request it drafts notes for a manager, text worded for the CRM, a spreadsheet layout or follow-up on a risk. A weekly review on a schedule, after approval.</dd></div>

<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It claims no past movement without something to compare against and does not let chat override facts the CRM owns. It changes no category, amount, close date or record by itself.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>One seller or book, a team, a period, a report, a pipeline view or chosen deals. A manager can limit it to the deals of a named owner.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>A weekly rerun that only reads, for the same scope and conventions, after a check for a duplicate and the user&#x27;s approval.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>A pipeline export, a forecast table, a snapshot or a linked authoritative report. Anecdotes alone are not enough. It reviews risk only when the evidence cannot support a change of posture.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Team and book definitions, the reporting period, stage gates, category conventions, guidance from a manager or revenue operations, and the amount basis, such as ARR, ACV, TCV, a weighted amount or the CRM&#x27;s main amount.</dd></div>

<div class="ggrp"><h3 id="gpt-g-coaching">Coaching <small>A rep&#x27;s calls, against peers and over time.</small></h3>
<details class="gsk" id="gpt-get-rep-call-feedback">
<summary><span class="nm"><code>get-rep-call-feedback</code></span><span class="tg">Coach a rep on their calls</span><span class="fitm"><span class="t">Managers and leaders</span><span class="t">For a named seller</span></span><span class="pm" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary>

<div><dt>Give it</dt><dd>A rep it can identify or calls you supply, evidence close to a transcript, and a coaching focus or a basis for comparison. A benchmark needs peer examples it can identify or a comparison set that has been confirmed.</dd></div>

<dl class="kv">

<div><dt>Can create or change</dt><dd>Nothing at first. Later options: a short note from manager to rep, or for self-coaching, drafted for review, and a coaching document to reuse.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Asks first</dt><dd>Later documents come after review. The skill itself creates no scorecard, sends no feedback, posts nothing and saves no coaching file.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Will not</dt><dd>It does not rank reps, create formal scorecards, read personality or performance into thin evidence, or show raw connector IDs. It quotes at most 25 words from one source.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Whose work it covers</dt><dd>One named seller, with a bounded set of peers. A manager can name the rep and ask for coaching from manager to rep.</dd></div>
<div><dt>On a schedule</dt><dd>Not stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>With no app connected</dt><dd>Pasted or exported material close to a transcript, or detailed call notes. When a comparison cannot be supported it gives a limited memo and names the gaps.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Earlier coaching and a manager&#x27;s examples can be read. No required company method or scorecard is stated.</dd></div>

<details class="gsk" id="gpt-review-rep-call-trends">

<div><dt>Company context it uses</dt><dd>Coaching context from a manager can be used. The plugin brings its own rubric. No required company rubric is stated.</dd></div>
<div><dt>Before and after</dt><dd>It offers a bounded comparison with peer examples.</dd></div>

 <section class="sec soft">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="gpt-apps">What it works with</h2>
   <p class="lead2">The installed copy declares 46 optional integrations, each a vendor's product. It does not record which of them an account has connected. Connecting an app, and what it may then reach, is settled with that app's provider.</p>
   <div class="scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Optional integrations the installed plugin declares, by category"><table class="rt dt stack wide"><thead><tr><th scope="col">Category, as the plugin stores it</th><th scope="col">Entries</th><th scope="col">Named in the plugin's list</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">Internal Messaging</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">2</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Microsoft Teams, Slack</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Meeting Transcripts</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">6</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Fireflies, Granola, Otter.ai, Zoom <span class="more-n">and 2 stored as codes</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row">CRM</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">8</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Attio, Close, HubSpot, Pipedrive, Salesforce, ServiceNow, Zoho <span class="more-n">and 1 stored as a code</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Sales Intelligence</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">10</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Actively, Apollo, Common Room, HG Insights, Meticulate, Rox, ZoomInfo <span class="more-n">and 3 stored as codes</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Email</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">4</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Gmail, Outlook Email, Outreach <span class="more-n">and 1 stored as a code</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Knowledge &amp; Files</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">10</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Canva, Figma, Google Drive, Notion, SharePoint <span class="more-n">and 5 stored as codes</span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Calendar</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">2</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Google Calendar, Outlook Calendar</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Scheduling</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">1</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Calendly</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Work Management</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">1</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">Monday.com</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Document Signing</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Entries">3</td><td class="fmt" data-l="Named">DocuSign <span class="more-n">and 2 stored as codes</span></td></tr></tbody></table></div>
   <p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">13 of the 46 are stored as codes and not as names, and one of them sits in two categories. The plugin's README prints 40 names. Nine of those match no plain identifier: Agentforce Sales, Backstory, Clay, Gamma, Gong, Highspot, Salesloft, Seismic and Zoom Revenue Accelerator. Its files tie only Clay to a code, so the others are not placed in a category here.</p>
   <p class="note">Three counts do not agree. OpenAI's page shows eight apps (Fireflies, Gong, Granola, Otter.ai, Slack, Teams, Zoom and Zoom Revenue Accelerator) and says there are 33 more, which makes 41. The README names 40. The installed copy declares 46. We could not reconcile them.</p>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="gpt-setup">Setup, company context and admin controls</h2>
   <div class="g2 mt">
    <div class="def"><h3>Install and connect</h3><p>From OpenAI's <a href="https://help.openai.com/en/articles/20001707-getting-started-with-chatgpt-for-sales">help page</a>: open the Sales listing and install the plugin. Under "Works with", install the partner plugins you need, connect each with your own work account and review the permissions it asks for.</p><p>From the installed copy: a workflow asks for a source only when it needs one. If the user declines, it asks for the smallest export, excerpt or file that would do, and stops only when a first result cannot be supported.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>First run</h3><p>The plugin's README gives a first prompt: <span class="sayt">@Sales Help me get started</span>. The routing skill answers it with an introduction and offers the <a class="chip" href="#gpt-demo-exec-and-seller-dash">demo-exec-and-seller-dash</a>. No separate skill for onboarding or company setup is stated.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>Company context</h3><p>No central settings screen and no company-wide intake is stated. Each workflow takes its context from records you give it or from sources it is allowed to read. For finding companies that means your ICP and territories. For a forecast, your stages and how you count amounts. For ranking accounts, your account plans and the rule for which accounts to leave alone. For research, the proof and messaging you have approved. For documents and decks, your templates.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>Customizing</h3><p>Dashboards, templates, reference inputs and some demo material can be customized, and a dashboard keeps its customizations when it is refreshed. A company's own connector can stand in for a source category. No way to edit, copy or extend the 18 skills is stated, and the plugin's manifest marks the package as proprietary.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>Admin controls</h3><p>From OpenAI's help page: in a Business or Enterprise workspace an admin opens the Admin Console, then Plugins, and sets the Sales plugin and partner plugins to available for installation or installed for the workspace. Admins also review each app's access, allowed actions and provider permissions. The installed copy states no control over single skills, over partner apps inside Sales or over single actions.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>An always-on agent</h3><p>OpenAI's <a href="https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-for-sales/">announcement</a> describes dot, an agent in ChatGPT that is always on and can use the same plugins. The installed copy describes schedules workflow by workflow and no single agent that runs them all.</p></div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec soft">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="gpt-rollout">Before rollout</h2>
   <div class="g2 mt">
    <div class="gsplit">
     <h3>From the plugin's own text, as reported</h3>
     <ul class="lim">
      <li>A request that only reads can still leave a file behind. <a class="chip" href="#gpt-seller-account-dashboard">seller-account-dashboard</a>, <a class="chip" href="#gpt-sales-leadership-dashboard">sales-leadership-dashboard</a> and <a class="chip" href="#gpt-build-competitive-brief">build-competitive-brief</a> do so as their normal result. A user who wants the competitive brief in the chat alone can ask for that.</li>
      <li>Reading can still cost money. <a class="chip" href="#gpt-enrich-company-and-contact-data">enrich-company-and-contact-data</a> asks for approval before any search, export or enrichment that uses a data provider's credits, and before it touches personal contact data.</li>
      <li>Different sources answer different questions. The CRM settles ownership, opportunity fields and the state of the forecast. Transcripts settle what a customer said and what was promised on a call. The calendar settles the details of an invitation.</li>
      <li>Publishing a dashboard and scheduling its refresh are two approvals, not one.</li>
      <li>The demo is fiction. It does not show that any provider is connected or can be written to.</li>
     </ul>
    </div>
    <div class="ourread flat gsplit">
     <h3>Our read <small>Not from the plugin</small></h3>
     <ul class="lim">
      <li><b>Agree where the official number lives.</b> The two dashboards and <a class="chip" href="#gpt-review-forecast">review-forecast</a> give a second view of figures that a CRM or a forecasting tool already reports.</li>
      <li><b>Decide who may spend provider credits</b> before <a class="chip" href="#gpt-enrich-company-and-contact-data">enrich-company-and-contact-data</a> is in wide use.</li>
      <li><b>Check your own rules for reviewing recorded calls</b> before managers use <a class="chip" href="#gpt-get-rep-call-feedback">get-rep-call-feedback</a> and <a class="chip" href="#gpt-review-rep-call-trends">review-rep-call-trends</a> on named reps.</li>
      <li><b>Keep a person on anything a customer will see.</b> Decks, business cases and follow-up emails are drafts until someone reviews them.</li>
     </ul>
    </div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="side-by-side">Beside the Claude Sales plugin</h2>
   <p>These tables set facts side by side. They do not say which plugin is better, and no skill of either was run for them.</p>
   <div class="scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="The two sales plugins, fact by fact"><table class="rt dt stack wide"><thead><tr><th scope="col">Fact</th><th scope="col">Claude Sales plugin</th><th scope="col">ChatGPT Sales plugin</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><th scope="row">Built by</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">Anthropic</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">OpenAI</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Version read</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">2.1.0</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">1.1.7</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Skills</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">36</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">18: one that routes requests and 17 workflows</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">The plugin's files</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">Public, under the Apache 2.0 license. Read by us</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">No public copy. Reported to us by ChatGPT from an installed copy</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Runs in</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">Claude</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">ChatGPT Work and Codex</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">How a skill is called</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">When a request matches it, or directly by its name</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">By asking @Sales. The routing skill picks the workflow</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Tools it names</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">23 servers set up in advance, in 10 categories</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">46 optional integrations in 10 categories</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Company setup</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">A first-run skill, and company facts written down once</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">No setup skill or company-wide settings are stated. An admin of a Business or Enterprise workspace can install it for everyone</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">Results the provider reports</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">None with a number</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">One customer's estimate in the announcement</td></tr><tr><th scope="row">On this site</th><td class="fmt" data-l="Claude">A full entry, with our read of fit, overlap and value</td><td class="fmt" data-l="ChatGPT">Every skill as ChatGPT reported it. Nothing is rated</td></tr></tbody></table></div>

<div id="salesforce-in-claude" data-view="salesforce-in-claude" data-group="sf" data-title="Salesforce in Claude">
 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>Salesforce in Claude, from Salesforce</h1>
   <p class="lede">This plugin from Salesforce, the CRM vendor, brings a seller's Salesforce accounts, opportunities and pipeline into Claude, with 37 skills for sales work. This entry sets out what Salesforce and Anthropic have published about it.</p>
   <div class="acts"><a class="btn pri" href="#sf-skills">Browse the 37 skills</a><a class="btn" href="#sf-source">Why only public pages</a></div>
   <p class="who"><span><b>Preview.</b> From public pages only. It awaits its editor's review.</span><span>In beta since September 15, 2026, read on November 25, 2026</span><span>For revenue leaders and operators</span></p>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="sf-source">Why only public pages</h2>
   <p class="lead2">Salesforce in Claude is in a beta that Salesforce approves organization by organization. Its <a href="https://www.salesforce.com/en-us/wp-content/uploads/sites/4/documents/legal/Agreements/beta-agreements/Beta-Services-Agreement.pdf">beta agreement</a> makes the beta and the information Salesforce discloses about it Salesforce's confidential information, so this page uses only what Salesforce and Anthropic have published.</p>
   <div class="g3 mt">
    <div class="def"><h3>What this entry uses</h3><p>Salesforce's help pages on the plugin, its Claudeforce page and its beta request form, the Claude Help Center's two articles, Anthropic's announcement and the list of skills in Anthropic's public Sales plugin, all read on November 25, 2026. <a href="#sf-sources">The sources</a> are listed at the end of the page.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>What it does not use</h3><p>No skill file was read, nothing was installed for this entry and no skill was run. Nothing on the page comes from the beta itself.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>What that leaves open</h3><p>What each skill's instructions say, which tools it calls and how well it works. Where the published pages disagree, the entry says so.</p></div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec soft">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="sf-glance">At a glance</h2>
   <dl class="glance"><div><dt>Provider and offering</dt><dd><p>Salesforce, a vendor. The plugin is the first product of <a href="https://www.salesforce.com/claudeforce/">Claudeforce</a>, a partnership between Salesforce and Anthropic. Anthropic calls it a plugin built with Salesforce. In beta since September 15, 2026.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Category</dt><dd><p>CRM vendor</p></dd></div><div><dt>In one sentence</dt><dd><p>A plugin for Claude with 37 sales skills, a first-run skill and connectors to Salesforce and Slack, for researching accounts, preparing and following up meetings, working deals, reviewing pipeline and keeping Salesforce up to date.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Go-to-market (GTM) jobs it covers</dt><dd><ul class="jobs"><li><span>Lead qualification</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-lead-triage">lead-triage</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-assign-target-to-sdr">assign-target-to-sdr</a></span></li><li><span>Account research and call preparation</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-account-context">account-context</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-research-prospect">research-prospect</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-call-prep">call-prep</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-query-activity-history">query-activity-history</a></span></li><li><span>Outreach and follow-up</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-draft-outreach">draft-outreach</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-call-follow-up">call-follow-up</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-summarize-conversation-transcript">summarize-conversation-transcript</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-inbox-sweep">inbox-sweep</a></span></li><li><span>Deal strategy and reviews</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-stakeholder-map">stakeholder-map</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-deal-review">deal-review</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-deal-advance-gap">deal-advance-gap</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-deal-signals">deal-signals</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-handle-objection">handle-objection</a></span></li><li><span>Proposals</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-close-plan">close-plan</a></span></li><li><span>Pipeline review and forecasting</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-deal-slip-scenario">deal-slip-scenario</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-pipeline-review">pipeline-review</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-team-pipeline">team-pipeline</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-forecast-narrative">forecast-narrative</a></span></li><li><span>CRM updates</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-update-opportunity">update-opportunity</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-sfdc-hygiene-check">sfdc-hygiene-check</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-log-activity">log-activity</a></span></li><li><span>Account planning and expansion</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-account-plan">account-plan</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-account-tiering">account-tiering</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-expansion-whitespace">expansion-whitespace</a></span></li><li><span>Renewals</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-customer-health">customer-health</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-renewal-radar">renewal-radar</a></span></li><li><span>Coaching and enablement</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-win-loss-review">win-loss-review</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-customer-voice">customer-voice</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-rep-context">rep-context</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-find-internal-answer">find-internal-answer</a></span></li><li><span>Daily planning</span><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-weekly-wrap">weekly-wrap</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-calendar-events">calendar-events</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-daily-briefing">daily-briefing</a></span></li></ul><p class="note" style="margin-top:10px">Placed by us from Salesforce's one-line descriptions. Under CRM updates, <a class="chip" href="#sfk-sfdc-hygiene-check">sfdc-hygiene-check</a> finds what is wrong and changes nothing. No skill of its own for onboarding and adoption and support. Outside the list: <a class="chip" href="#sfk-voice-profile">voice-profile</a>.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Systems it works with</dt><dd><p>Salesforce and Slack. Claude reaches Salesforce through Salesforce's hosted MCP server. The Claude Help Center says both connectors come with the plugin, and Salesforce says connecting Slack is optional for the first run. Anthropic says the skills can read and write Slack deal channels and account-team threads.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Who can get it</dt><dd><p>Organizations that Salesforce approves through a <a href="https://agentexchange.salesforce.com/sales-cloud-in-claude-beta-access">beta request on AgentExchange</a>. Salesforce's pages list its Enterprise, Performance, Unlimited and Developer Editions. On the Claude side the sources differ, as <a href="#sf-open">set out below</a>. Salesforce says companies that reach Claude only through a cloud provider, such as AWS Bedrock or Google Cloud Vertex AI, cannot use it for now. The Claude Help Center says it works in chat and Cowork, on the web and desktop. No price is stated.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Controls</dt><dd><p>Claude sees only what the signed-in user can see in Salesforce. An admin decides with one tool setting whether skills may create and update records: with the user's approval, without it or not at all. No skill deletes a record.</p></dd></div><div><dt>What the provider reports</dt><dd><p>No measured result. Anthropic's announcement names GitLab, Siemens and Legora as customers that have deployed the plugin, and says 14,000 Salesforce sellers use it. Its customer quotes report time saved on meeting preparation, with no study behind them.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Evidence in the register</dt><dd><p>None yet.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Not public or not known</dt><dd><p>The skill files are not public. Not known: what each skill's instructions say, which skills change records in practice and how any of them performs.</p></dd></div><div><dt>On this site</dt><dd><p>This entry. It rates nothing.</p></dd></div><div><dt>Last checked</dt><dd><p>November 25, 2026: Salesforce's help pages, its Claudeforce page and its beta request form, the Claude Help Center, Anthropic's announcement and the skills folder of Anthropic's Sales plugin, by Claude, then against the same pages by OpenAI's Codex.</p></dd></div></dl>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="sf-skills">The 37 skills</h2>
   <p class="lead2">Claude picks a skill from what the user asks, and a user can also call one by typing / and its ID. Salesforce groups the skills by the work they do, and this page keeps its groups.</p>
   <ul class="sftally">
    <li><b>6 of 37</b> are marked by Salesforce as able to create and update records, if the admin allows it.</li>
    <li><b>None</b> deletes a record. Salesforce says deletion stays in Salesforce.</li>
    <li><b>Configure</b> is offered on first use, a skill outside Salesforce's list of 37.</li>
   </ul>
   <p class="note">What each skill does is restated from Salesforce's one-line description. The GTM job beside it is our read.</p>
   <div class="sfgrp"><h3 id="sf-g-account-intelligence-and-research">Account intelligence and research <small>6 skills</small></h3><div class="scroll" tabindex="0" role="region" aria-label="Account  <section class="sec soft">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="sf-setup">Setup, first run and admin controls</h2>
   <div class="g2 mt">
    <div class="def"><h3>Request access</h3><p>A Salesforce admin asks for the beta through the <a href="https://agentexchange.salesforce.com/sales-cloud-in-claude-beta-access">request form on AgentExchange</a>. The <a href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/16952184-set-up-salesforce-in-claude-for-your-organization">Claude Help Center</a> says approval brings an email with the setup steps.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>In Salesforce</h3><p>The admin turns on Salesforce's hosted MCP server once for the org. Setup calls it the Headless360 (H360) MCP Server. An External Client App with Claude's callback address gives the key and secret that Claude needs, and <a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.set_up_salesforce_in_claude.htm&amp;type=5">Salesforce's setup pages</a> have the values. A permission set limits who may connect, once that app admits only users an admin has approved. Salesforce recommends one, and without it anyone in the org can. It warns that letting users authorize themselves skips the set.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>In Claude</h3><p>An Owner or Primary Owner of the Claude organization enters that key and secret for the Salesforce connector. They then choose, for everyone or for set groups, whether members get the plugin installed or may install it themselves. The Claude Help Center adds a third choice, required. Each user signs in with their own Salesforce account.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>Record changes</h3><p>One tool setting in Claude, for what Salesforce calls the Dispatch API, decides whether skills may create and update records. Needs Approval, which Salesforce recommends, has the user approve each change. Always Allowed makes changes without asking. Deny keeps the plugin read only. <a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.set_tool_restrictions.htm&amp;type=5">Salesforce says</a> the tools are unrestricted until an admin sets them.</p><p>A save follows the same rules for required and read-only fields as in Lightning Experience. Records must use page layouts or Dynamic Forms. Objects whose actions are overridden with Lightning web components or Visualforce are not supported, unless the user has the Customize Application permission and the Builder user type. <a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.salesforce_in_claude_record_crud.htm&amp;type=5">No record can be deleted</a>.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>First run</h3><p><a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.configure_salesforce_in_claude.htm&amp;type=5">Salesforce calls the first-run skill Configure</a> and prompts the user to run it on first use. It sets up a recurring daily task called Salesforce Updates and gathers the user's role, recently viewed records, recent activity and open opportunities on the accounts they own. On request it builds a live view of the user's book. Anthropic's announcement and the Claude Help Center call it a setup skill that tailors the plugin to the user's role and book of business.</p></div>
    <div class="def"><h3>Data</h3><p>Claude sees only the records and fields the user can see in Salesforce, under the org's profiles, permission sets and sharing rules. <a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.security_permissions_and_data_handling.htm&amp;type=5">Salesforce says</a> that once data reaches Anthropic, the customer's contract with Anthropic governs it and that Anthropic may not use Salesforce data or business logic reached through the connection without permission. Anthropic says it does not train its models on Team and Enterprise data by default.</p></div>
   </div>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="sf-compare">Beside the Claude Sales plugin</h2>
   <p>Salesforce's list shares many skill IDs with Anthropic's own Sales plugin, and Salesforce's Claudeforce page says Salesforce and Anthropic built the skills. A shared ID may still mean different instructions. Salesforce's skill files are not public, and nothing here compares them.</p>
   <ul class="sftally">
    <li><b>27</b> IDs are the same in both.</li>
    <li><b>10</b> are only in Salesforce's list.</li>
    <li><b>Nine</b> are only in Anthropic's plugin.</li>
   </ul>
   <dl class="sfonly">
    <div><dt>Only in Salesforce's list</dt><dd><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#sfk-assign-target-to-sdr">assign-target-to-sdr</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-calendar-events">calendar-events</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-call-follow-up">call-follow-up</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-find-internal-answer">find-internal-answer</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-forecast-narrative">forecast-narrative</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-query-activity-history">query-activity-history</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-research-prospect">research-prospect</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-sfdc-hygiene-check">sfdc-hygiene-check</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-summarize-conversation-transcript">summarize-conversation-transcript</a><a class="chip" href="#sfk-voice-profile">voice-profile</a></span></dd></div>
    <div><dt>Only in Anthropic's plugin</dt><dd><span class="chips"><a class="chip" href="#skill-account-research">account-research</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-call-summary">call-summary</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-competitive-intelligence">competitive-intelligence</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-create-an-asset">create-an-asset</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-crm-hygiene-check">crm-hygiene-check</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-end-of-day">end-of-day</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-forecast">forecast</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-route-lead">route-lead</a><a class="chip" href="#skill-setup">setup</a></span></dd></div>
   </dl>
   <p>Record changes are marked differently, so the two counts are not the same measure: Salesforce's mark covers Salesforce records, and the Claude Sales entry's covers any record system a skill writes to. Salesforce marks four of the 27 shared skills as able to create and update records: <a class="chip" href="#sfk-account-plan">account-plan</a>, <a class="chip" href="#sfk-log-activity">log-activity</a>, <a class="chip" href="#sfk-schedule-meeting">schedule-meeting</a> and <a class="chip" href="#sfk-update-opportunity">update-opportunity</a>. The Claude Sales entry, reading Anthropic's files, marks 10 of the 27 as changing records. Anthropic's announcement also describes call preparation that adds contacts, and a deal review that, when the seller asks it to keep going, fills in qualification fields and contact roles and saves them once approved. Salesforce marks none of <a class="chip" href="#sfk-call-prep">call-prep</a>, <a class="chip" href="#sfk-deal-review">deal-review</a> and <a class="chip" href="#sfk-close-plan">close-plan</a>. Which skill makes those changes is not public.</p>
   <h3 class="mt" id="sf-by-job">By GTM job</h3>
   <p>The site's 13 jobs, with the skills of each plugin placed under them. Placement is our read, and a shared ID sits under the same job in both columns.</p>

   <p class="note" style="margin-top:12px">The Salesforce column is from Salesforce's skills reference, read on November 25, 2026. The Claude column is from version 2.1.0 of Anthropic's Sales plugin, read in its public files. <a class="chip" href="#sfk-voice-profile">voice-profile</a> in Salesforce's list and <a class="chip" href="#skill-setup">setup</a> in Anthropic's fall outside the 13 jobs, and those are not in the table.</p>
   <h3 class="mt" id="sf-copilot">Salesforce's build for Microsoft 365 Copilot</h3>
   <p>Salesforce documents the same kind of plugin for Microsoft 365 Copilot, in beta and needing a Microsoft 365 Copilot license. Its <a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.m365copilot_sales_skills_ref.htm&amp;type=5">skills reference</a> lists 38 skills, 35 of them with the same IDs as the list for Claude. Only for Copilot: <code>executive-movement</code>, <code>salesforce-hygiene-check</code> and <code>event-invite-list</code>. Only for Claude: <code>update-opportunity</code> and <code>sfdc-hygiene-check</code>. Salesforce marks five of the 38 as able to create and update records.</p>
  </div>
 </section>

 <section class="sec soft">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h2 id="sf-open">Where the public pages disagree</h2>
   <ul class="lim">
    <li><strong>The Claude plan.</strong> <a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.set_up_salesforce_in_claude.htm&amp;type=5">Salesforce's setup page</a> asks for a direct Anthropic subscription: Claude Enterprise, Claude Team or Claude Console. The box at the top of its pages on setting up and using the plugin says Claude Enterprise. The <a href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/16952184-set-up-salesforce-in-claude-for-your-organization">Claude Help Center</a> and <a href="https://claude.com/resources/articles/salesforce-in-claude">Anthropic's announcement</a> say all paid plans.</li>
    <li><strong>The beta.</strong> <a href="https://www.salesforce.com/claudeforce/">Salesforce's Claudeforce page</a> says select pilot customers have the plugin today and an open beta is planned for September 2026. Anthropic announced the beta on September 15, 2026. The Claude Help Center says Salesforce approves organizations through its beta sign-up.</li>
    <li><strong>The Salesforce edition.</strong> Salesforce's pages list Enterprise, Performance, Unlimited and Developer Editions. The Claude Help Center says the latest Sales Cloud enterprise edition.</li>
    <li><strong>Who built the skills.</strong> The Claude Help Center says Salesforce, Anthropic says it built the plugin with Salesforce and Salesforce's Claudeforce page says both companies built the skills.</li>
    <li><strong>Approval of record changes.</strong> Anthropic and the Claude Help Center say Claude asks the seller before each change by default. Salesforce says the tools are unrestricted until an admin sets them, and that users create and update records only once an admin sets the Dispatch API to Needs Approval or Always Allowed. The pages do not say how the two levels combine.</li>
    <li><strong>The first-run skill.</strong> Salesforce calls it Configure and prompts the user to run it on first use. The Claude Help Center calls it a setup skill that runs automatically the first time the plugin opens, and Anthropic says it creates an artifact for the user's role and book.</li>
    <li><strong>How the plugin reaches members.</strong> Both let the admin pick groups. Salesforce lists two choices for them, installed by default and available to install. The Claude Help Center adds a third, required.</li>
    <li><strong>A workflow with no skill.</strong> The Claude Help Center gives QBR decks as an example, and no skill in Salesforce's list names one.</li>
   </ul>
   <h3 class="mt" id="sf-sources">Sources</h3>
   <p>All read on November 25, 2026.</p>

<!-- ============================================================ METHOD -->
<div id="method" data-view="method" data-group="page" data-title="Method and sources">
 <section class="open">
  <div class="wrap">
   <h1>Method, limits and sources</h1>
   <p class="lede">The Claude Sales plugin entry was compiled from the plugin's own files, and the ratings in it are a first reading, which its editor has begun to change. The shorter plugin pages are described at the end.</p>
  </div>
 </section>
 <div class="wrap" style="padding-block:clamp(32px,4.5vw,56px) clamp(48px,6vw,88px)">
  <div class="art">
   <h2 style="margin-top:0">How it was put together</h2>
   <ul>
    <li>All 36 skill files, the README and the connector notes of Sales plugin 2.1.0 were read from an installed copy on November 21, 2026. A public copy sits in Anthropic's knowledge-work-plugins repository, under the Apache 2.0 license.</li>
    <li>The tools a skill uses are taken by script from the table in its file. Whether a tool is read, or is somewhere the skill delivers to, was set by hand from that table's own words, and from the file's write line or a step where the table lists only a read. What a skill can change, send or post was written by hand from its file, and a script holds it to the file's own write line. Each skill that another one names was sorted by hand: one it hands work to, one it draws on or one it only mentions. The card leaves out the last kind. A script checks that each name shown is in the file. The build fails when the dataset and the files disagree.</li>
    <li>What the build checks is narrow: that a skill named on a card is in that skill's file, that a tool's direction is one the table allows and that the four facts agree with the file's write line. It checks no number, no condition and no scope. Those rest on reading, and that is where all three rounds of outside checks found most of what they disputed.</li>
    <li>Descriptions are restated in this entry's words. For the plugin's exact wording, open the skill file.</li>
    <li>Each run card restates the skill's own trigger phrases, the inputs it names and what it accepts when no tool is connected. A request for a wider scope or for a schedule appears only where the skill's file takes one. The routines and their order are ours, and each link in one is checked against the file that states it.</li>
    <li>On November 22, 2026 two models from other labs checked the entry's numbered claims against the plugin's files, in two rounds: an OpenAI model (GPT-6-based Codex, by its own account) and xAI's Grok Build. In the first round they disputed 78 of 398 claims between them. In the second they disputed 105 of the 595 claims of the corrected entry, much of it wording that the first corrections had added. Each round also held six false claims planted as a test, and each model caught all six both times. On November 23, 2026 the OpenAI model checked the 170 statements that had changed since, with five false ones planted among them, and caught all five. It disputed 52 of the entry's own. The most common fault, on 11 cards, was a line that read as a limit on what a skill can do, where every skill's file says an action you ask for goes through a connected tool. Claude checked each dispute against the file and made the corrections, and the editor decided what to accept. What they found and what changed is set out in <a href="kit/CROSSLAB.md"><code>kit/CROSSLAB.md</code></a>. The text as it now stands has had no check from outside.</li>
    <li>The configuration half is Nathan Fierley's guide, checked against the same plugin version on November 16, 2026. Its words are kept, apart from five small edits: three serial commas and one numeral changed to meet the site's style rules, and one sentence about buttons that this page replaced with a menu. Its sales leader is split here into a frontline manager and a leader. On November 22, 2026, 12 corrections were made here after the checks by two other labs' models, such as the number of skills written for leaders. Three of them were worded again on November 23, 2026, after the third check. Each change is listed in <a href="kit/CONFIGURE-EDITS.md"><code>kit/CONFIGURE-EDITS.md</code></a>. The guide's own published copy does not carry them yet.</li>
   </ul>

   <h2>Fact and our read</h2>
   <p>Three kinds of statement sit side by side in this entry. They are marked apart wherever they meet.</p>
   <ul>
    <li><strong>From the plugin's files.</strong> What each skill does, what it reads, what it can change, send or post and which skill it hands work to. The plugin is open source, so each statement can be checked against the skill files.</li>
    <li><strong>Our count from the files.</strong> Counts and groupings that we made from what the files say: the four markings of a skill's name, who takes a team or a named rep and which skills are named as routines. Each gives its rule, and none is a sentence in a file.</li>
    <li><strong>Our read.</strong> Which role a skill suits, where it overlaps a tool you may own, how much it is worth and how often it is used in a sales motion, when in the week it fits, the eight routines and their order, which model to start it on and what it leans on in your organization. The reasons are shown. None of it is usage data.</li>
   </ul>
   <p>Our read is a first reading by Claude, the AI model made by Anthropic, for Nathan Fierley to edit. He has changed parts of it, as the credits say. Anthropic also makes the plugin, so weigh the ratings with that in mind.</p>
   <p>The ratings place this plugin's skills against roles and sales motions. They do not score the provider, and they are kept out of the evidence register.</p>

   <h2>What it does not tell you</h2>
   <ul>
    <li><strong>How the skills perform in your business.</strong> One small test ran six skills from invented files, and it is described below. Nothing was run against a live CRM, and no time saved or deals won are claimed.</li>
    <li><strong>How often anyone uses a skill.</strong> The frequency ratings are estimates from how each skill is designed. Five skills are given an occasion in place of a frequency. Seven are named as routines in the plugin's files: <a class="skl" href="#skill-daily-briefing"><code>daily-briefing</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-end-of-day"><code>end-of-day</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-weekly-wrap"><code>weekly-wrap</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-route-lead"><code>route-lead</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-deal-signals"><code>deal-signals</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-competitive-intelligence"><code>competitive-intelligence</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-crm-hygiene-check"><code>crm-hygiene-check</code></a>.</li>
    <li><strong>How it compares.</strong> Only Anthropic's Sales plugin is covered in depth. The <a href="#chatgpt-sales">ChatGPT for Sales</a> and <a href="#salesforce-in-claude">Salesforce in Claude</a> pages set out what OpenAI and Salesforce make public about their own, and none is rated against another.</li>
    <li><strong>What changes next.</strong> Skills are added and reworded between versions. Check the version in this entry against yours.</li>
   </ul>

   <h2 id="model-test-method">How the model test was run</h2>
   <ul>
    <li><strong>What ran.</strong> Six skills, each on four Claude models, once: <a class="skl" href="#skill-pipeline-review"><code>pipeline-review</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-crm-hygiene-check"><code>crm-hygiene-check</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-forecast"><code>forecast</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-call-summary"><code>call-summary</code></a>, <a class="skl" href="#skill-inbox-sweep"><code>inbox-sweep</code></a> and <a class="skl" href="#skill-lead-triage"><code>lead-triage</code></a>. Each run was a fresh session that read the skill's file and followed it for one request, working only from the test files.</li>
    <li><strong>Where.</strong> In the agent harness this entry was built in, on November 21, 2026. That is not the Claude app, and nothing was connected. Effort was left at each model's default, which differs between models.</li>
    <li><strong>The answer key.</strong> The pipeline figures in the key were computed by script from the test file. The planted items in the call, the inbox and the leads were written with the files. One of those items was wrong: the key expected a draft reply to legal redlines, where the skill's file says to flag legal mail and not draft it. It was corrected on November 22, 2026, after a model from another lab caught it. Claude set the corrected mark on reading each reply again. The two markers did not mark it.</li>
    <li><strong>The marking.</strong> Two separate Claude sessions, one on Opus 5.5 and one on Sonnet 5.5, marked the four replies for each skill against the key, under code names. On that first marking they agreed on 390 of 396 marks. One guardrail mark was set on a second reading, and the reason is recorded with the files. So Anthropic models marked Anthropic models, on an Anthropic plugin. The key is made of facts that can be checked, and every reply and mark is published with this page.</li>
    <li><strong>What it is not.</strong> One run for each skill and model cannot separate a model's ability from chance. The test covers the from-files path only. It is our own test, and it stays out of the evidence register.</li>
   </ul>

   <h2 id="other-listings">How the other plugin pages were put together</h2>
   <ul>
    <li>Anthropic's plugins were read from a copy of its <a href="https://github.com/anthropics/knowledge-work-plugins">public repository</a> at the commit of November 19, 2026. Each plugin's version and each skill's name and description were taken by script, and the build checks every name and count on these pages against them.</li>
    <li>A skill list restates each skill's own description. The steps inside the skill files were read only for the Sales plugin.</li>
    <li>The ChatGPT for Sales entry rests on a report, because the plugin's files are not public. On November 22, 2026 the site's editor installed the plugin in his own ChatGPT account and gave ChatGPT <a href="kit/chatgpt/inventory-prompt.md">a fixed prompt</a>, kept in this page's files, that asked for a read-only inventory of the plugin's own text. ChatGPT reported version 1.1.7 and the full instructions of 18 skills. Claude checked the report against two facts the prompt had held back, against itself and against OpenAI's <a href="https://openai.com/business/plugins/sales/">plugin page</a>, <a href="https://help.openai.com/en/articles/20001707-getting-started-with-chatgpt-for-sales">help page</a>, <a href="https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-for-sales/">announcement</a> and <a href="https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/plugins">plugins guide</a>, opened on November 22, 2026. The entry restates the report in this site's words and quotes the plugin once. No workflow was run, and nothing in the entry is rated.</li>
    <li>What ChatGPT marked as its own inference is not given as fact: the plan and mode of the session, the order of the skills and the names behind integrations stored as codes.</li>
    <li>The Salesforce in Claude entry rests on public pages only. The plugin is in a beta whose agreement makes the beta and the information Salesforce discloses about it Salesforce's confidential information. Claude read Salesforce's help pages on the plugin, its Claudeforce page and beta request form, the Claude Help Center, Anthropic's announcement and the list of skills in Anthropic's public Sales plugin on November 25, 2026. The same day OpenAI's Codex, based on GPT-6, checked every line against the same pages. The entry took its 18 corrections. Each skill's line restates Salesforce's one-line description in this site's words, and the build holds the wording apart from Salesforce's. The groups and the mark on skills that can change records are Salesforce's. The GTM jobs are our read, and a skill ID shared with Anthropic's Sales plugin sits under the same job. No skill file was read, nothing was installed and nothing on that page is rated.</li>
    <li>Which skills count as leader skills, and who may reach for each, is our read. The note on Google rests on one search and on its <a href="https://workspaceupdates.googleblog.com/2026/09/skills-gemini-app-workspace.html">Workspace Updates</a> post about skills. The note on Microsoft rests on its <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dynamics365/sales/use-copilot-cowork-sales-skills">documentation of the sales skills in Copilot Cowork</a> and on its <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/dynamics-365/blog/business-leader/2026/11/17/microsoft-ignite-2026-introducing-dynamics-365-in-copilot/">post of November 17, 2026</a>, both read on November 23, 2026. How the 18 skills divide between its two plugins is our count from the table in that documentation.</li>
    <li>Nothing on those pages is rated, and none of their skills was run.</li>
   </ul>

   <h2>Rules this entry follows</h2>
   <ul>
    <li><strong>A listing is never paid for.</strong> Providers cannot pay to be listed, moved or described differently, and a listing is not an endorsement.</li>
    <li><strong>The provider's statements are dated.</strong> What this entry says about the product comes from the provider's own files and pages, with the date each was read.</li>
    <li><strong>Named systems only.</strong> Vendor names appear only where the plugin's own connector list gives them. Every other tool is named as a category.</li>
    <li><strong>The conflict is stated.</strong> This entry was compiled with Claude, an Anthropic model, and it covers an Anthropic product. On November 22, 2026 models from two other labs checked it against the plugin's files in two rounds, and on November 23, 2026 one of them checked what had changed since. Claude made the corrections after each. Before it is published it still needs its editor's review, and the latest corrections have had no check from outside.</li>
    <li><strong>One template.</strong> <a href="#chatgpt-sales">ChatGPT for Sales</a> from OpenAI, also an AI lab and a vendor, and <a href="#salesforce-in-claude">Salesforce in Claude</a> from Salesforce, a CRM vendor, are set out on the same 12 fields.</li>
   </ul>

   <h2 id="sources">Sources</h2>
