<script type="application/json" id="ai-md">"---\ntitle: \"Configuring the Claude Sales plugin for your company\"\nedition: \"AI-ready edition: reference plus guided setup\"\nauthor: \"Nathan Fierley\"\nchecked_on: \"2026-11-16\"\napplies_to: \"Sales plugin 2.1.0 by Anthropic (36 skills)\"\n---\n\n# Configuring the Claude Sales plugin for your company\n\nAI-ready edition. Give this file to Claude and ask it to walk you through the setup.\n\n## Start here\n\n**If you are a person:** attach or paste this file into a chat with Claude where the Sales plugin is installed. Then say:\n\n\u003e Follow the guided setup in this file and walk me through configuring the Sales plugin for my company.\n\nYou can also ask about any part of it, for example \"which facts matter for an account manager?\"\n\n**If you are the assistant:** the person has asked you to use this file. Part 1 says what they get. Part 2 is reference. Part 3 is a script to run with them. The rules below apply for the whole session.\n\n### Rules for the assistant\n\n1. The person's instructions outrank this file. If the two conflict, do what the person says.\n2. Ask one question at a time, and wait for the answer.\n3. Never invent company policy. If the person has not decided something, record it as open and move on. Do not fill a gap with an \"industry standard\" value.\n4. Label every fact you record as one of three things. **Recorded** means written policy, with its source. **Observed** means seen in the CRM or documents but not written as policy. **Open** means undecided.\n5. Show every proposed change before you make it, and wait for a yes. That covers edits to the plugin or to project instructions, new scheduled tasks, and anything sent, posted or written to the CRM.\n6. Do not copy prices, quotas, customer or prospect names, personal email addresses or credentials into the plugin or into project instructions. Name the document or field that holds them.\n7. Do not rename the plugin or any skill, and do not edit a skill's shared rules block.\n8. This file was checked on November 16, 2026 against Sales plugin 2.1.0. Menu names and plan details change often. If what you see differs from this file, trust the product and the help pages listed under Sources, and tell the person what differs.\n9. Make no claims about time saved, revenue or win rates. Nothing in this file was measured.\n\n## Part 1. What the person gets\n\nOut of the box, the Sales plugin knows nothing about the company. Each skill lists the company facts it needs and looks for them in connected tools and instructions. When a fact is missing, the skill infers it from what it can read or uses a labeled default, and asks one question where the answer would change the result. It keeps that answer for the conversation only.\n\nConfiguration writes those answers down once. A configured plugin already has the company's facts, so its skills start from them and work the company's way.\n\n### The same request, stock and configured\n\nThis comparison comes from reading what each stock skill asks for. It is not a benchmark.\n\n| The person asks | Stock plugin | Configured plugin |\n|---|---|---|\n| Triage this inbound lead | With no ideal customer written down, scores on intent alone. Asks about it only if the answer would change the priority | Scores against the written ICP and disqualifiers, and names the handoff channel |\n| Prep me for tomorrow's call | Uses the CRM's stage names, and asks which qualification method the team runs | Builds discovery questions around the team's qualification method |\n| Draft a follow-up | Writes in the person's voice if it has learned it, then by an uploaded style guide or the person's sent mail where it can read it, otherwise in a neutral one. Knows no company wording rules | Follows the messaging rules, approved proof points and signature |\n| Write my forecast | Uses the CRM's own forecast category where it has one. Otherwise infers the categories from the stages, and asks which stages count as commit only if the answer depends on it | Uses the company's mapping of stages to forecast categories, where the CRM has no forecast category of its own |\n| Which renewals are coming up? | Reads where renewal dates live from the CRM and from what it already knows about the company, and asks once only if neither tells it | Reads the field the company named |\n\n### Benefits, by design\n\n- **Fewer repeated questions.** A stock answer lasts one conversation. A configured fact is there every time, for every skill that needs it.\n- **The same rules for everyone.** Every seller's skills use one ICP, one set of stage definitions and one set of wording rules.\n- **Guardrails that travel.** A rule such as \"no pricing by email\" is given to every draft, whoever asks for it. It is an instruction to the model. A tool permission can block every send or hold each one for a yes. It cannot check what an email says, so a rule about content still needs a person's review.\n- **Honest gaps.** Where the company has not decided something, the configuration can say so. The skill then reports the gap instead of inventing a rule.\n\n### Outcomes by role\n\nOne company configuration serves every role. Roles differ in which skills they use, and so in which facts matter most. Fact IDs refer to section 2.4.\n\n| Role | What changes once the plugin is configured | Facts to get right first |\n|---|---|---|\n| SDR or BDR | Inbound leads are scored against the written ICP and disqualifiers and matched to an owner by the company's rules. Once the person accepts the routing and asks for the post, the handoff goes to the named channel with the note the company specified | F01, F13, F02 |\n| Account executive | The deal skills check each deal against the company's stage exit criteria and qualification method, and forecasts use one category mapping | F04, F03, F12, F08, F09, F05 |\n| Account manager or CSM | Renewal and health skills read the fields the company named, so upcoming renewals come from data instead of a question | F14, F15 |\n| Sales leader | Forecasts use one mapping of stages to categories, team pipeline reviews use one coverage target and win-loss reviews use one loss-reason field | F05, F06, F16, F17 |\n| Revenue operations or enablement | One reference page holds every fact, its source document and the open decisions | All of them, plus connectors, permissions and distribution |\n| Founder or full-cycle seller | The facts they repeat most often are answered once, in every skill that needs them | F01, F02, F10 |\n\n### Seats: who changes what\n\nRoles decide which skills and facts matter. Seats decide who is allowed to change them. On a plan of their own, a person holds all three seats and nothing here conflicts. On a Team or Enterprise plan the seats are usually different people.\n\nIn one line each: admins decide what each tool may do; operators write the company facts down once; sellers use the company build and add their voice.\n\n| Seat | Who it is | Controls | Cannot change |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Admin | An Owner or Primary Owner of the Claude organization. On Enterprise, also anyone whose custom role manages the organization's libraries. The role labeled Admin in the members list does not open the plugin settings | Which connectors the organization has, tool permissions for everyone, each plugin's availability (not available, available to install, installed by default or required, and by group on Enterprise), and what members may create, share and publish | The sales facts, which belong to the operator |\n| Operator | Whoever owns the sales process and its tools: revenue operations, sales operations, enablement, or a sales leader or founder doing that job | The company facts and skill behavior, the reference page, the open decisions, and each new version of the build | Connectors, tool permissions and who receives the plugin, which belong to the admin |\n| Seller (individual contributor) | A person using Claude on the company's plan | Their own sign-in to each tool, their voice, signature and role, their own project instructions, their scheduled routines, and their own plugins where the organization allows members to add them | A plugin the organization manages or one a colleague shared with them, a plugin marked required, tool permissions set for the organization, and which connectors the organization has (they can ask for one where requests are turned on) |\n\nWhere the seats overlap:\n\n- **Company facts.** A seller can write them in project instructions, and the operator writes them into the plugin. If both exist, a skill can see two answers, and no documented rule says which wins. Once the company build exists, sellers keep only personal facts in their own instructions.\n- **The plugin itself.** Members cannot edit a plugin the organization manages. A seller's own customized copy is a separate plugin with the same name, and the docs give no rule for two same-named plugins in the app. Keep one Sales plugin per person. A good private copy should be offered to the operator and then retired.\n- **Claude Code.** There the rule is documented: when a plugin from any other source has the same name as one synced from the Claude account, the other copy loads and the synced copy is reported as not loaded. A seller who installed the stock plugin from the marketplace will get it in place of the company's synced build until they remove their own copy.\n- **What a tool may do.** The organization's tool permissions win, and members cannot loosen them. If a write tool is blocked, the skill keeps working and hands the person a checklist to apply by hand.\n- **What data a skill sees.** The admin enables a connector, and each person signs in with their own account. Claude acts with that person's access, so the same skill returns different records for different people. Fix access in the CRM, not in the plugin.\n- **Wording.** The operator's wording rules and a seller's learned voice both shape a draft. Say in the build that company wording rules outrank personal habit, and keep voice out of the shared plugin.\n- **Scheduled routines.** Each seller creates their own, and each run acts within the organization's permissions. Keep sends, posts and writes on approval for scheduled runs.\n- **New versions.** A published plugin changes only when it is published again, through review where review is on. Members stay on the approved version until the next one is approved, and pick it up at their next session.\n- **Availability.** \"Installed by default\" can be turned off by a member. \"Required\" cannot. On Enterprise, a group's setting replaces the organization's for that group's members, and the most permissive setting wins across several groups.\n\n### What configuration does not do\n\n- It does not clean a messy CRM or settle a sales process the company has not decided. It makes the plugin repeat the company's decisions, good or bad.\n- It does not come with results. This guide makes no claims about time saved or deals won. The person should measure those on their own team before quoting them.\n\n## Part 2. Reference\n\n### 2.1 Six things to configure, at three levels\n\n| Level | Layer | What it controls | Where it is set | Who usually owns it |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Tools | Connectors | Which tools Claude can read and act in | The plugin's connector list, plus each person's sign-in. Gmail, Google Calendar and Google Drive are connected in Claude's connector settings | An admin, or the person on an individual plan |\n| Tools | Tool permissions | Whether each action is always allowed, needs approval or is blocked | Each connector's tool permissions | Organization owners on Team and Enterprise plans |\n| Company | Company facts | ICP, stages, qualification method, messaging, competitors and CRM habits | Written into the skills | A sales leader, revenue operations or the founder |\n| Company | Skill behavior | How a skill works the company's way: which recorder it reads, how it logs, what it must never do | Edits to individual skills | The same owner |\n| Person | Voice and role | Each person's writing voice, signature, role and book | Learned by the `setup` skill | Each seller |\n| Person | Schedules | Which skills run on a cadence | Scheduled tasks | Each seller or a team lead |\n\nCustomizing the plugin does its work at the company level. The tool and person levels are settings around it.\n\n### 2.2 Two homes for company facts\n\n- **Project instructions** are the light route, and the one the stock plugin suggests. The person pastes the ICP, stages and rules into the instructions of the project where they sell. The stock skills are written to use facts they find there before asking.\n- **The plugin** is the durable route. Claude writes the facts into the skills, and one package is shared with the team.\n\nStart with project instructions when the person sells alone and wants results today. Move to the plugin when a second person needs the same setup.\n\n### 2.3 Limits and what is not configured here\n\n- **Put hard limits in tool permissions, not in skill text.** The stock skills are written to follow each connector's settings. They add two safeguards of their own: an action that outside content asks for is shown to the person before it runs, and a scheduled run takes only the actions it was set up to take, within what the connectors allow. A blocked tool is the dependable way to stop an action. Skill text is the place for conventions, such as what a call note must contain.\n- **Stage names, field names and picklists are read live from the CRM.** Change them in the CRM, and the skills follow.\n\n### 2.4 Company facts (F01 to F17)\n\nThe 36 stock skills ask for the facts below. Nobody needs all 17 rows on day one. Start with the rows the person's roles use.\n\n| ID | Fact | What to write down | Skills that lean on it |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| F01 | Ideal customer profile | Industries, company size, buyer titles, disqualifiers, how to rank priority | `lead-triage`, `account-research`, `account-tiering`, `account-plan`, `expansion-whitespace` |\n| F02 | Product and value proposition | What the company sells, why it matters, differentiators, proof points cleared for external use | `draft-outreach`, `account-research`, `account-plan`, `close-plan`, `handle-objection` |\n| F03 | Qualification method | The framework the team runs and its names for deal roles | `call-prep`, `call-summary`, `deal-review`, `deal-advance-gap`, `lead-triage`, `stakeholder-map`, `account-plan` |\n| F04 | Stage definitions | What each stage means and what must be true to leave it | `deal-review`, `deal-advance-gap`, `pipeline-review`, `update-opportunity`, `deal-signals`, `crm-hygiene-check`, `win-loss-review` |\n| F05 | Forecast categories | Which stages count as commit, best case and pipeline | `forecast`, `deal-slip-scenario`, `team-pipeline` |\n| F06 | Quota and coverage | Where quota is recorded and the coverage target | `deal-slip-scenario`, `team-pipeline` |\n| F07 | Deal size and cycle length | Typical deal size, size bands, average cycle, any alert thresholds | `pipeline-review`, `deal-signals`, `win-loss-review`, `expansion-whitespace` |\n| F08 | Competitors | Who the team meets, their pitch, their gaps and the company's wedge | `competitive-intelligence`, `handle-objection`, `account-research`, `draft-outreach` |\n| F09 | Common objections | The objections the team hears and the answers it stands behind | `handle-objection`, `deal-review` |\n| F10 | Voice and signature | Tone, greeting and sign-off habits, signature block | `draft-outreach`, `inbox-sweep`, `call-summary`, `schedule-meeting` |\n| F11 | Brand and templates | Deck structure, palette, where approved content lives | `create-an-asset` |\n| F12 | Stakeholders and approvals | Who must be involved to close, and the internal approval chain with real turnaround times | `close-plan`, `deal-advance-gap`, `stakeholder-map` |\n| F13 | Routing and handoff | Territory map, size bands, named accounts, who wins ownership, the handoff channel | `route-lead`, `lead-triage` |\n| F14 | Renewal and health data | Where renewal dates live, health and adoption fields, where success metrics live | `renewal-radar`, `customer-health` |\n| F15 | Product catalog | Products or SKUs, typical deal size per product, usage fields | `expansion-whitespace` |\n| F16 | CRM habits | Required activity fields, how meetings are logged, where competitor and loss reason are recorded | `log-activity`, `schedule-meeting`, `win-loss-review`, `competitive-intelligence` |\n| F17 | Team channels | Which chat channels each team uses | `rep-context`, `weekly-wrap` |\n\nThree rules for what goes in:\n\n- **Write decisions, not aspirations.** If the team does not follow a stage definition, the plugin will enforce a fiction.\n- **Point to anything that changes often.** For prices and quotas, name the document or field that holds the current figure.\n- **Leave out anything sensitive.** A shared plugin should hold no customer names, personal email addresses or credentials.\n\n### 2.5 Roles and the skills they use most\n\nThis map is a suggestion drawn from the skill descriptions. The plugin itself assigns nothing, and no skill is locked to a role. Before any configuration, the `setup` skill already infers a role from the CRM (AE, BDR or SDR, leader, or customer success), builds a role-aware dashboard and recommends three skills. Three skills, `team-pipeline`, `rep-context` and `win-loss-review`, are written for leaders.\n\n| Role | Skills they will use most | Configuration tip |\n|---|---|---|\n| SDR or BDR | `lead-triage`, `route-lead`, `account-research`, `draft-outreach`, `schedule-meeting`, `log-activity` | Define a qualified lead, who receives it and what the handoff note must contain |\n| Account executive | `call-prep`, `call-summary`, `stakeholder-map`, `deal-review`, `deal-advance-gap`, `close-plan`, `handle-objection`, `update-opportunity`, `forecast` | Write exit criteria for each stage. Four skills check deals against them directly: `deal-review`, `deal-advance-gap`, `crm-hygiene-check` and `update-opportunity`. Others use them too |\n| Account manager or CSM | `customer-health`, `renewal-radar`, `expansion-whitespace`, `customer-voice`, `account-plan`, `account-context` | Name the renewal field first. The renewal skill calls that mapping \"the whole game\" |\n| Sales leader | `team-pipeline`, `rep-context`, `pipeline-review`, `forecast`, `win-loss-review`, `deal-slip-scenario` | Fix the category mapping and coverage target once, so every forecast reads the same way |\n| Revenue operations or enablement | `setup`, `crm-hygiene-check`, and spot checks of the rest | Own the reference page: every fact, its source document and the open decisions |\n| Founder or full-cycle seller | The daily skills from every row | Start with the five facts repeated most often |\n\nWhat a fact looks like written down. These lines are invented for a fictional company. They show the level of detail that works: one decision, stated plainly.\n\n- **SDR or BDR:** A lead is qualified when a director or above confirms the problem and agrees to a call. Hand off in the inbound channel with company, contact, trigger and next step.\n- **Account executive:** A deal leaves Discovery only when the economic buyer is named and the next meeting is booked.\n- **Account manager:** Renewal date is the Contract End Date field on the company record. Flag any renewal inside 120 days with no meeting booked.\n- **Sales leader:** Commit means Contract Sent. Best case means Proposal and Negotiation. The coverage target is three times quota.\n\nWhere configuration differs by role:\n\n- **Emphasis.** Every role shares the same facts, but each stresses different ones. Configure first the rows for the roles the company actually has.\n- **Handoffs.** Configuration pays most where work changes hands. Lead to AE needs routing rules and a handoff note. AE to account manager needs renewal dates and products recorded at close.\n- **The personal layer.** Voice, signature, book and territory belong to one person. Let `setup` learn them, and keep them in that person's own instructions, not the shared plugin.\n- **Access.** Owners on Team and Enterprise plans set tool permissions for the whole organization. Claude also acts with each person's own access in the CRM, so CRM roles still apply.\n\nOne build or several: keep one build unless two teams truly sell differently. Separate builds drift apart, and each is another plugin to update. If two segments run different stages or messaging, split by segment, not by job title. Enterprise admins can then set which groups get which plugin.\n\n### 2.6 Tips from one real configuration\n\nBefore starting:\n\n- Point Claude at documents, not memory. It can then quote them and link back to them.\n- Name one owner, who reviews the changes and answers the open questions.\n- Say what to ignore: test records, sample data and internal accounts in the CRM. Otherwise they show up in pipeline reviews.\n\nWhile configuring:\n\n- Give each skill only the facts it uses. A call-prep skill does not need routing rules.\n- Keep one reference page inside the plugin: every fact, a link to each source document, and the open decisions.\n- Label how sure each fact is: recorded, observed or open.\n- Never let the configuration invent policy. Keep a proposed rule on the open list until the owner approves it.\n- Encode wording rules: banned words, required phrases and claims that cannot be made. They then apply to every draft.\n- Separate what exists from what is planned, so no draft sells the roadmap as built.\n- Replace vendor-specific passages. A few stock passages describe one vendor's mechanics, such as a CRM audit query.\n- Trim the connector list to the tools in use.\n- Keep every name. People, saved prompts and schedules call the plugin and its skills by name.\n\nAfter configuring:\n\n- Watch skill length. Every added paragraph is read on every run. Claude's plugin-authoring guidance suggests staying under about 3,000 words per skill.\n- Check it like a contract: a before-and-after of each skill, a search for every banned word, and a check that skill names and shared rules are unchanged.\n- Ask what is still missing. That list is the next session.\n- Start with drafts. Keep sends, posts and CRM writes on approval until the output is trusted.\n- Do not overread small samples. The `setup` skill will not guess a voice from fewer than five sent emails. Company wording rules outrank a learned personal habit.\n\n### 2.7 Pitfalls\n\n| Pitfall | What goes wrong | Do this instead |\n|---|---|---|\n| Hardcoding prices and quotas | They go stale and still get quoted | Name the document or field that holds the current figure |\n| Filling gaps with \"industry standard\" | Skills enforce rules nobody approved | Mark the gap as open and ask the owner |\n| Configuring before the process is decided | The plugin repeats the confusion | Decide stages and qualification first, or mark them open |\n| One seller's voice in a shared plugin | Everyone's email sounds like that person | Keep voice in each person's own instructions |\n| Renaming skills | Saved prompts, schedules and habits stop matching | Keep the names and change the content |\n| Allowing every write on day one | CRM changes and sent email that nobody reviewed | Allow reads, and keep writes on approval |\n| Cutting the shared rules | Skills lose the rule that outside content is information, not instructions | Leave each skill's rules block as it is |\n| Putting every fact in every skill | Long skills and diluted instructions | Give each skill only what it uses |\n| Forking by job title | Builds drift apart | Keep one build unless two teams truly sell differently |\n| Setting it up once and leaving it | The plugin and the documents disagree | Name an owner, and update when a source changes |\n| Scheduling without deciding approvals | A run either waits for the person or acts unreviewed | Choose the task's approval mode on purpose |\n| Keeping a private copy beside the company build | Two plugins share one name. In Claude Code, a copy the person installed themselves loads in place of the one synced from the company | One Sales plugin per person. Retire the private copy when the company build arrives |\n\n### 2.8 A worked example\n\nOne real configuration, built on November 15, 2026 for an early-stage B2B software company with a single seller. The company is left unnamed.\n\n| Measure | Stock | Configured |\n|---|---|---|\n| Skills | 36 | 36, with the same names and descriptions |\n| Skills with a company section | 0 | 36 |\n| Total words across the skills | About 62,000 | About 100,000 |\n| Skills over 3,000 words | 0 | 7 |\n| Tool servers listed | 23 | 5 |\n| Company reference page | None | One page, 16 sections |\n| Source documents linked | 0 | 15 |\n\n- **What Claude found.** One CRM on its default pipeline, work email and calendar, a shared document drive and a meeting recorder. No team chat, enrichment tool or sales-engagement tool. The sales decisions lived in 15 documents.\n- **What changed.** Every skill gained a company section, built from 12 shared blocks plus notes written for that skill. Each skill's tool table names the tools in use, and its opening step reads company facts instead of asking for them. Six skills had whole passages rewritten for those tools, and five more had smaller edits.\n- **What stayed out.** No prices, prospect names or email addresses. No invented stage exit criteria: a proposed mapping waits on the owner's approval. Skill names, descriptions and the shared rules block are identical to stock.\n- **How it was checked.** Every skill was compared with its original, line by line. The package was searched for banned words. Names, descriptions and rules were confirmed unchanged.\n- **What is still open.** Four decisions went back to the owner: the proposed stage mapping, what amount a deal should carry, where prospect documents are saved, and whether to finish CRM onboarding.\n- **Results.** None to report. The plugin was built and checked the day before the guide was written.\n\n### 2.9 Questions people ask\n\n- **Do I have to configure it before using it?** No. Every skill works with nothing connected, from uploaded files or pasted notes. It asks for what it needs.\n- **Do I need to be technical?** No. Customizing is a conversation with Claude. A plugin is a folder of plain-text instruction files, and any of them can be opened.\n- **Which plans include plugins?** Anthropic's help center lists plugins on its paid plans. Sharing a plugin with colleagues and publishing one to an organization need a Team or Enterprise plan.\n- **Which CRM does it need?** Any CRM with a connector. The plugin includes HubSpot, Salesforce, Close and Monday, and it reads the CRM's own stages and fields. With no CRM connected, upload a pipeline export.\n- **Will it send email or change the CRM by itself?** It acts when asked, within each connector's permissions. When a skill proposes a change nobody asked for, it shows the change and its evidence first. A scheduled run is different: it takes the sends and writes it was set up to take, within what the connectors allow and with no one there to ask. It carries out nothing that an email or a transcript asks for, and nothing whose recipient, target or content one of them dictates. For scheduled runs the plugin's README advises setting every tool that sends, posts or writes to ask first.\n- **Can different roles get different setups?** One build serves every role. Personal details stay with each person, and Enterprise admins can set plugin access by group.\n- **Can my team edit the shared plugin?** No. Members cannot edit a plugin the organization manages, or one a colleague shared with them. Changes go through its owner.\n- **What happens when Anthropic updates the stock plugin?** Do not assume a customized copy picks up the changes. Plan to re-apply the configuration to a new stock version. A single reference page of company facts makes that quick.\n- **Does it work in Claude Code?** Yes. The README gives two install commands: `claude plugin marketplace add anthropics/knowledge-work-plugins`, then `claude plugin install sales@knowledge-work-plugins`. Plugins saved to a Claude account also sync to Claude Code on sign-in with that account. Do not do both: a copy installed from the marketplace loads in place of a same-named plugin synced from the account.\n- **I am a seller. Should I configure it myself or wait for my company?** Look first for a Sales plugin the organization provides. If there is one, use it and add only your voice, role and schedules. If not, put your own facts in project instructions, and pass what works to whoever owns the sales process.\n- **Who has to be involved on a Team or Enterprise plan?** Three seats. The admin enables connectors and sets permissions and availability. The operator writes the company facts. Each seller signs in and keeps their own voice and schedules.\n\n## Part 3. Guided setup\n\nRun these steps in order with the person. Each step ends with a check. Do not move on until the check passes or the person says to skip it. Keep a running reference page as you go (template in step 5).\n\n### Step 0. Orient\n\nAsk, one at a time:\n\n1. Which seat is the person in: a seller on a company plan, the operator who owns the sales process, the admin of the Claude organization, or all three on a plan of their own? Section \"Seats: who changes what\" in Part 1 defines each.\n2. Which roles will use the plugin: SDR or BDR, account executive, account manager or CSM, sales leader, revenue operations, founder or full-cycle seller?\n3. Is this for one person or a team?\n4. Which tools do they sell with: CRM, email, calendar, meeting recorder, team chat, documents?\n\nThen route by seat:\n\n- **Seller on a company plan.** First have them open Plugins and look for a Sales plugin their organization already provides. If there is one, do steps 1 and 2 with it, then help with their own schedules in step 8. Do not customize a second copy. Collect the questions the skills still ask, and help them send that list to whoever owns the sales process. If there is none, do steps 1 to 4 and write the facts into their project instructions (step 5, route A). Customizing their own copy is fine where the organization lets members add plugins, as a draft to offer the operator.\n- **Operator.** Run every step. Before step 5, ask who the admin is, whether publishing to the organization needs review, and how the build will reach people.\n- **Admin.** Do step 1 for the organization's connectors, then steps 7 and 9. Leave the sales facts to the operator.\n- **All three on one plan.** Run every step in order.\n\nThen tell them the plan in a few lines:\n\n- Which fact rows to cover first, from \"Outcomes by role\" in Part 1.\n- Which home fits: project instructions for one person who wants results today, the plugin once a second person needs the same setup.\n- Who else must be involved: an admin for Salesforce or Microsoft 365 connectors, an owner for organization-wide permissions, the plugin's owner if the organization already distributes a managed copy.\n\n**Check:** the person agrees with the seat, the scope, the home and the owner of the configuration.\n\n### Step 1. Install and connect\n\n1. Confirm the Sales plugin is installed. It is added from \"Plugins\" in the left sidebar. Some organizations still see the older name, \"Customize\".\n2. Connect the tools in this order: CRM, then email, then calendar. That is the order the plugin's `setup` skill follows.\n3. Say what you can and cannot see from this chat. If a tool the person named is not connected, tell them and continue with what is there.\n\n**Check:** the plugin is installed and at least the CRM is connected, or the person has chosen to work from an uploaded pipeline export.\n\n### Step 2. Run setup\n\n1. Have the person say \"set me up\", or run the `setup` skill if they ask you to.\n2. Setup shows what is connected, infers the person's role from the CRM, builds a starter dashboard, learns their writing voice from sent mail and recommends three skills.\n3. Setup saves nothing by itself. Offer the short voice summary for the person to keep in their own project instructions. Voice is personal, so it stays out of the shared plugin.\n\n**Check:** the person has seen their role, their voice summary and the three recommended skills.\n\n### Step 3. Gather the sources of truth\n\n1. Ask where each decision is written down: ICP, messaging, stage definitions, qualification method, brand guide. Ask for a link or an attachment for each.\n2. Ask what to ignore in the CRM: test records, sample data, internal accounts.\n3. Read the sources. List what each one covers and what none of them covers.\n\n**Check:** a list of sources with links, a list of things to ignore, and a first list of gaps.\n\n### Step 4. Collect the facts\n\nFor each fact row in scope, in the order from step 0:\n\n1. Look in the sources and the connected tools first.\n2. Show what you found, with its source, in one or two plain sentences.\n3. Ask the person to confirm, correct or mark it open.\n4. Record the fact with its label: recorded, observed or open. For anything that changes often, record where the current figure lives instead of the figure.\n\nWrite each fact as one decision, stated plainly. The example lines in section 2.5 show the level of detail.\n\n**Check:** every in-scope fact is recorded with a label and a source, or is on the open list with an owner.\n\n### Step 5. Write it down in the chosen home\n\n**Route A, project instructions.** Draft a compact block and show it to the person before they paste it into the project where they sell:\n\n```\nSales facts for [company]. Owner: [name]. Updated: [date].\n\nIdeal customer: [industries, size, buyer titles]\nDisqualifiers: [list]\nStages and exit criteria: [stage: what must be true to leave it]\nQualification method: [name, and our names for deal roles]\nForecast categories: [which stages count as commit, best case, pipeline]\nWording rules: [banned words, required phrases, claims we cannot make]\nWhere current figures live: [prices: document; quota: field]\nIgnore in the CRM: [test records, internal accounts]\nOpen decisions: [decision: owner]\n```\n\n**Route B, the plugin.** The person opens the installed plugin, selects \"Customize\", then \"Let's go\". Or they ask you to customize it in this chat. A prompt to start from:\n\n```\nCustomize the Sales plugin for my company.\nOur sources of truth are the documents in [folder or links].\nUse only what those documents and our connected tools say.\nWhere something is undecided, list it as an open question. Do not fill it in.\nDo not copy prices, customer names or email addresses into the plugin.\nDo not rename the plugin or any skill.\nWhen you finish, show what changed in each skill and what you could not find.\n```\n\nWhile customizing, follow the tips in section 2.6: give each skill only the facts it uses, keep one reference page inside the plugin, and keep every name. One session does not have to cover everything: the person can come back, say what to add, and save again. If the organization already distributes the Sales plugin, members cannot edit that managed copy. Its owner should run the customization.\n\nAfter saving, have the person check that one Sales plugin is turned on. If the stock one is still listed beside the customized copy, they turn the stock one off.\n\nReference page template:\n\n```\nCompany reference page. Owner: [name]. Updated: [date].\n\nFor each fact: ID, the fact as written, label (recorded, observed or open), source link.\nOpen decisions: decision, owner, what is waiting on it.\nIgnore list: records and accounts the skills should skip.\nChange log: date, what changed, which source changed.\n```\n\n**Check:** the person has read the block or the changed skills and said yes.\n\n### Step 6. Review it like a contract\n\n1. Show a before-and-after of each skill the person will use every day.\n2. Search the result for every banned word.\n3. Confirm that the plugin name, the skill names, their descriptions and each shared rules block are unchanged.\n4. Report any skill that now runs past about 3,000 words, and offer to trim it.\n5. List what you could not find. That list is the next session.\n\nThe person then selects \"Save plugin\".\n\n**Check:** the person has saved the plugin, or has pasted the block into project instructions, and has the list of what is still missing.\n\n### Step 7. Set what each tool may do\n\nTool permissions are set by the person, or by an organization owner on Team and Enterprise plans. Walk them through it:\n\n1. Each connector tool can be set to \"Always allow\", \"Needs approval\" or \"Blocked\".\n2. A sound start is reads allowed, with sends, posts and CRM writes on approval.\n3. Anything that must never happen gets \"Blocked\". A blocked tool is a hard limit. A sentence in a skill is not.\n4. Members cannot loosen a permission the organization has set. If the person is a seller, tell them what is blocked for them. When a write tool is blocked, the skill keeps working and hands over a checklist to apply by hand.\n\n**Check:** the person has set permissions, or knows who will.\n\n### Step 8. Schedule the routines\n\n1. The `setup` skill offers four routines: a daily briefing, a CRM hygiene check, an end-of-day close-out and a weekly wrap. They are created from \"Scheduled\", then \"New task\".\n2. Ask which ones the person wants, and at what times.\n3. Keep send, post and write tools on approval for scheduled runs, as the plugin's README advises. By design, a scheduled run takes only the actions it was set up to take. Anything else it finds becomes a proposal in its output.\n4. Have the person choose each task's approval mode on purpose.\n\n**Check:** the chosen routines exist, each with a decided approval mode. Zero routines is a fine answer.\n\n### Step 9. Share and maintain\n\nSkip this step for one person using project instructions.\n\n1. Choose a route to roll it out. Anthropic's tutorial lists three: send the plugin file, host it in a Git repository set up as a marketplace, or have an admin add it for the organization.\n2. Try it with a few sellers first. The operator shares the plugin with specific people, where the organization allows sharing.\n3. Publish it to the organization. The organization's publishing policy is one of three: requires review, open, or off. Under review, nothing reaches other members until a reviewer approves it.\n4. The admin sets its availability: not available, available to install, installed by default or required. \"Installed by default\" can be turned off by a member, and \"required\" cannot. Enterprise admins can vary that by group.\n5. The admin adds the plugin's connectors in organization settings. Making a plugin available does not add its connectors, and each member still signs in with their own account.\n6. Decide whether the stock Sales plugin stays visible beside the configured one. Hiding it avoids two plugins with one name.\n7. Agree how it stays current. When a source document changes, the owner tells Claude what changed and saves the plugin again. A plugin shared directly updates for its recipients. One published to the organization must be published again.\n8. Agree what happens on a new stock version: re-apply the configuration from the reference page.\n9. Ask sellers to retire any private copy, and to remove company facts from their own project instructions.\n\n**Check:** the team has the plugin, and one named person owns updates.\n\n### Step 10. Close out\n\nGive the person a short summary:\n\n- The facts recorded, each with its label and source.\n- The open decisions, each with an owner.\n- What changed: skills edited, permissions set, routines scheduled.\n- What to do in the first week: start with drafts, keep writes on approval, and note every question a skill still asks. Each of those questions is a fact to add next time.\n\n### If the person is short on time\n\n1. Connect the CRM and email.\n2. Run setup.\n3. Paste the ICP and stage definitions into the project instructions.\n4. Customize the plugin later, when the setup needs to be shared.\n\n## Sources\n\nChecked on November 16, 2026. Menu names and plan details change often, so treat the linked pages as the authority.\n\n- Sales plugin 2.1.0 by Anthropic: its README, connector notes and 36 skill files, read from the installed plugin. A public copy is in the knowledge-work-plugins repository: https://github.com/anthropics/knowledge-work-plugins\n- Use plugins in Claude, Claude Help Center: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13837440-use-plugins-in-claude\n- How to customize plugins in Claude Cowork, Anthropic tutorial: https://academy.claude.com/tutorials/how-to-customize-plugins-in-cowork\n- Manage plugins for your organization, Claude Help Center: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13837433-manage-plugins-for-your-organization\n- Manage plugins, skills, and connectors from one Plugins page, Claude Help Center: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/17740565-manage-plugins-skills-and-connectors-from-one-plugins-page\n- Use connectors to extend Claude's capabilities, Claude Help Center: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11176164\n- Manage plugins for your organization, Claude docs (availability, policy, publishing review, group access): https://claude.com/docs/plugins/admin\n- Plugin loading reference, Claude Code docs (synced plugins and name conflicts): https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins/loading\n- Schedule recurring tasks in Claude Cowork, Claude Help Center: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/13854387-schedule-recurring-tasks-in-claude-cowork\n- How can I create and manage projects?, Claude Help Center: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/9519177\n- Claude's built-in plugin authoring guidance: the 3,000-word suggestion for a skill's main file.\n- One configuration of the Sales plugin, built on November 15, 2026: the tips, the worked example and its counts.\n"</script>
<script>
(function () {
  'use strict';
  var D = JSON.parse(document.getElementById('data').textContent);
  var byId = {};
  D.skills.forEach(function (s) { byId[s.id] = s; });
  var swLabel = {}, swGroup = {}, roleById = {}, motionById = {};
  D.switches.forEach(function (s) { swLabel[s.k] = s.short; swGroup[s.k] = s.group; });
  D.roles.forEach(function (r) { roleById[r.k] = r; });
  D.motions.forEach(function (m) { motionById[m.k] = m; });

  function $(sel, root) { return (root || document).querySelector(sel); }
  function $$(sel, root) { return Array.prototype.slice.call((root || document).querySelectorAll(sel)); }
  function esc(t) { return String(t).replace(/[&<>"]/g, function (c) { return { '&': '&amp;', '<': '&lt;', '>': '&gt;', '"': '&quot;' }[c]; }); }
  // a, b and c. A serial comma only when an item itself contains "and" or "or".
  function join(list, word) {
    word = word || 'and';
    if (list.length < 2) { return list.join(''); }
    var compound = list.some(function (t) { return / (and|or) /.test(t); });
    return list.slice(0, -1).join(', ') + (compound ? ', ' : ' ') + word + ' ' + list[list.length - 1];
  }
  var WORDS = ['No', 'One', 'Two', 'Three', 'Four', 'Five', 'Six', 'Seven', 'Eight', 'Nine', 'Ten', 'Eleven', 'Twelve', 'Thirteen', 'Fourteen', 'Fifteen', 'Sixteen', 'Seventeen'];
  function focusQuiet(el) { try { el.focus({ preventScroll: true }); } catch (e) { el.focus(); } }
  function jump(el) { try { el.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'instant', block: 'start' }); } catch (e) { el.scrollIntoView(); } }

  // Remembered in this browser only: role, sales motion, readiness switches and ticked facts. The page works without it.
  var store = {
    get: function (k) { try { return JSON.parse(window.localStorage.getItem('mg.' + k)); } catch (e) { return null; } },
    set: function (k, v) { try { window.localStorage.setItem('mg.' + k, JSON.stringify(v)); } catch (e) { /* storage is optional */ } }
  };

  var state = { role: 'all', motion: 'ent', kinds: {}, q: '' };

  function kind(s) { return 'k-' + s.cls; }
  // The marking in words too, for anyone who cannot see outline and fill.
  var KIND_WORDS = { 'k-R': 'reads and proposes', 'k-P': 'can share its output', 'k-C': 'writes for customers', 'k-W': 'changes records', 'k-S': 'the first run' };
  function chip(s, dim) {
    return '<a class="chip ' + kind(s) + (dim ? ' dim' : '') + '" href="#skill-' + s.id + '">' + s.id + '<span class="vh">, ' + KIND_WORDS[kind(s)] + (dim ? ', outside the chosen role' : '') + '</span></a>';
  }

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- views */
  // With scripts, a view is found by its name and gives up its id. The browser then has nothing of its own to
  // scroll to when the address names a view, so a view always opens at its top. Without scripts the ids stay,
  // and the same links jump down one long page.
  var views = $$('[data-view]');
  var viewByName = {};
  views.forEach(function (v) { viewByName[v.getAttribute('data-view')] = v; v.removeAttribute('id'); });
  var tabLinks = $$('.tabs .tl:not([data-nav="all"]) a');
  var restoreY = null;   // set by the browser's Back: the place to return to instead of the link's target
  var pendingBack = null;   // set by a click on a link: where the reader was, for "Back to where you were"
  var navEl = $('#nav'), hereEl = $('#here');

  // The one navigation row follows the page: on the front door it lists the page's parts, inside a plugin
  // it names the plugin, offers the way back and shows that plugin's sections.
  function show(view, el, user) {
    views.forEach(function (v) { v.classList.toggle('on', v === view); });
    var name = view.getAttribute('data-view');
    navEl.setAttribute('data-group', view.getAttribute('data-group') || 'page');
    hereEl.textContent = view.getAttribute('data-title') || '';
    tabLinks.forEach(function (a) {
      if (a.getAttribute('href') === '#' + name) {
        a.setAttribute('aria-current', 'page');
        var list = a.closest('.tl');
        if (list) { list.scrollLeft = Math.max(0, a.offsetLeft - 24); }
      } else {
        a.removeAttribute('aria-current');
      }
    });
    if (restoreY !== null) { var y = restoreY; restoreY = null; window.scrollTo(0, y); } else if (el === view) { window.scrollTo(0, 0); } else { jump(el); }
    if (pendingBack) { offerBack(pendingBack, view); pendingBack = null; }
    if (user) {
      var target = el === view ? $('h1', view) : (el.tagName === 'DETAILS' ? $('summary', el) : el);
      if (target) {
        if (target.tagName !== 'SUMMARY' && !target.hasAttribute('tabindex')) { target.tabIndex = -1; }
        focusQuiet(target);
      }
    }
  }

  function route(user) {
    var id = '';
    try { id = decodeURIComponent(window.location.hash.slice(1)); } catch (e) { id = ''; }
    var el = id ? (viewByName[id] || document.getElementById(id)) : null;
    if (!el) { show(views[0], views[0], user); return null; }
    var view = el.closest('[data-view]');
    if (!view) { return null; }
    if (el.classList.contains('sk')) {
      if (el.hidden) { clearFilters(); }
      el.open = true;
    } else if (el.classList.contains('gsk')) {
      el.open = true;
    }
    show(view, el, user);
    return el === view ? null : el;
  }

  window.addEventListener('hashchange', function () { route(true); });
  // A link to the address already showing fires no hashchange, so go there by hand.
  document.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
    var a = e.target.closest ? e.target.closest('a[href^="#"]') : null;
    if (a && !e.defaultPrevented && a.getAttribute('href') === window.location.hash) { route(true); }
  });

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- a look at a skill, and the way back */
  // A chip names a skill whose row or card may sit far down this view or in another one. A click shows that
  // skill's own line where the reader is, with a link to the full card. A jump a link does make can be undone:
  // "Back to where you were", or the browser's Back, returns to the same place on the page. Without scripts the
  // chips stay plain links.
  var host = (typeof PLG !== 'undefined' && PLG) ? PLG : document.body;
  var KICK = { sk: 'Claude Sales plugin', gsk: 'ChatGPT for Sales', sfk: 'Salesforce in Claude' };
  function narrow() { return window.matchMedia('(max-width: 719px)').matches; }
  function txt(el) { return el ? el.textContent.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim() : ''; }
  function cap(t) { return t ? t.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + t.slice(1) : t; }

  function peekData(id) {
    var t = document.getElementById(id);
    if (!t) { return null; }
    if (t.tagName === 'TR' && id.indexOf('sfk-') === 0) {
      var cells = $$('td[data-l]', t), d = { kick: KICK.sfk, code: txt($('th code', t)), name: txt($('.sfn', t)), does: '', facts: [], go: 'Show it in the list' };
      cells.forEach(function (c) {
        var l = c.getAttribute('data-l');
        if (l === 'What it does') { d.does = txt(c); } else { d.facts.push([l, txt(c)]); }
      });
      return d;
    }
    if (t.classList.contains('sk')) {
      var ch = $('summary .chip', t), k = ch ? (ch.className.match(/k-[A-Z]/) || [''])[0] : '';
      return { kick: KICK.sk, code: t.getAttribute('data-id') || id.replace(/^skill-/, ''), name: txt($('summary .tg', t)), does: txt($('.body .does', t)),
        facts: KIND_WORDS[k] ? [['Marked', cap(KIND_WORDS[k])]] : [], go: 'Open the full card' };
    }
    if (t.classList.contains('gsk')) {
      var fit = txt($('summary .fitm', t));
      return { kick: KICK.gsk, code: txt($('summary code', t)), name: txt($('summary .tg', t)), does: txt($('.body .does', t)),
        facts: fit ? [['For', fit]] : [], go: 'Open the full card' };
    }
    return null;
  }

  var peekEl = null, peekBg = null, peekFrom = null;
  function buildPeek() {
    peekBg = document.createElement('div'); peekBg.className = 'pk-bg'; peekBg.hidden = true;
    peekEl = document.createElement('div'); peekEl.className = 'pk'; peekEl.hidden = true;
    peekEl.setAttribute('role', 'dialog'); peekEl.setAttribute('aria-labelledby', 'pk-t');
    host.appendChild(peekBg); host.appendChild(peekEl);
    peekBg.addEventListener('click', function () { closePeek(true); });
    peekEl.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
      if (e.target.closest('[data-pk-x]')) { closePeek(true); }
      else if (e.target.closest('[data-pk-go]')) { closePeek(false); }
    });
  }
  function placePeek() {
    if (!peekEl || peekEl.hidden) { return; }
    if (narrow()) { peekEl.style.left = ''; peekEl.style.top = ''; peekEl.style.width = ''; return; }
    var r = peekFrom.getBoundingClientRect();
    if (r.bottom < 0 || r.top > window.innerHeight) { closePeek(false); return; }
    var w = Math.min(440, window.innerWidth - 32), h, top;
    peekEl.style.width = w + 'px';
    h = peekEl.offsetHeight;
    top = r.bottom + 8;
    if (top + h > window.innerHeight - 12 && r.top - 8 - h >= 64) { top = r.top - 8 - h; }
    top = Math.max(64, Math.min(top, window.innerHeight - h - 12));
    peekEl.style.left = Math.min(Math.max(16, r.left), window.innerWidth - w - 16) + 'px';
    peekEl.style.top = top + 'px';
  }
  function openPeek(a, d) {
    if (!peekEl) { buildPeek(); }
    peekFrom = a;
    var facts = d.facts.length ? '<dl class="pk-f">' + d.facts.map(function (f) { return '<dt>' + esc(f[0]) + '</dt><dd>' + esc(f[1]) + '</dd>'; }).join('') + '</dl>' : '';
    peekEl.innerHTML = '<p class="pk-k">' + esc(d.kick) + '</p>' +
      '<h2 class="pk-t" id="pk-t" tabindex="-1"><code>' + esc(d.code) + '</code>' + (d.name ? '<span>' + esc(d.name) + '</span>' : '') + '</h2>' +
      (d.does ? '<p class="pk-d">' + esc(d.does) + '</p>' : '') + facts +
      '<div class="pk-a"><a class="btn sm pri" href="' + esc(a.getAttribute('href')) + '" data-pk-go>' + esc(d.go) + '</a><button class="btn sm" type="button" data-pk-x>Close</button></div>';
    peekEl.hidden = false;
    peekBg.hidden = !narrow();
    placePeek();
    focusQuiet($('#pk-t', peekEl));
  }
  function closePeek(refocus) {
    if (!peekEl || peekEl.hidden) { return; }
    peekEl.hidden = true; peekBg.hidden = true;
    if (refocus && peekFrom && peekFrom.isConnected) { focusQuiet(peekFrom); }
  }

  var backEl = null, backFrom = null;
  function hideBack() { if (backEl) { backEl.hidden = true; } backFrom = null; }
  function offerBack(from, view) {
    var far = from.view !== view || Math.abs(window.scrollY - from.y) > window.innerHeight * 0.75;
    if (!far) { hideBack(); return; }
    if (!backEl) {
      backEl = document.createElement('button');
      backEl.type = 'button'; backEl.className = 'bk'; backEl.hidden = true;
      backEl.textContent = 'Back to where you were';
      host.appendChild(backEl);
      backEl.addEventListener('click', function () { hideBack(); window.history.back(); });
    }
    backFrom = { y: from.y, view: from.view };
    backEl.hidden = false;
  }
  window.addEventListener('scroll', function () {
    if (backFrom && backFrom.view.classList.contains('on') && Math.abs(window.scrollY - backFrom.y) < 160) { hideBack(); }
    if (peekEl && !peekEl.hidden) { window.requestAnimationFrame(placePeek); }
  }, { passive: true });
  window.addEventListener('resize', function () { if (peekEl && !peekEl.hidden) { peekBg.hidden = !narrow(); placePeek(); } });

  // The browser's Back returns to the place the reader left, not to the top of the section the address names.
  window.addEventListener('popstate', function (e) {
    restoreY = e.state && typeof e.state.mgY === 'number' ? e.state.mgY : null;
    hideBack(); closePeek(false);
    // An address that does not change fires no hashchange, so restore here as well.
    window.setTimeout(function () { if (restoreY !== null) { window.scrollTo(0, restoreY); restoreY = null; } }, 0);
  });

  document.addEventListener('keydown', function (e) {
    if (e.key === 'Escape' && peekEl && !peekEl.hidden) { closePeek(true); }
  });
  document.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
    var a = e.target.closest ? e.target.closest('a[href^="#"]') : null;
    if (peekEl && !peekEl.hidden && !e.target.closest('.pk, .pk-bg') && a !== peekFrom) { closePeek(false); }
    if (!a || e.defaultPrevented || e.button !== 0 || e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey || e.shiftKey || e.altKey) { return; }
    var href = a.getAttribute('href'), id = href.slice(1);
    if (a.classList.contains('chip') && /^(skill|sfk|gpt)-/.test(id) && !a.closest('.map, .pk')) {
      var d = peekData(id);
      if (d) {
        e.preventDefault();
        if (peekEl && !peekEl.hidden && peekFrom === a) { closePeek(true); } else { openPeek(a, d); }
        return;
      }
    }
    if (href === window.location.hash || href === '#' || a.closest('.tabs, .mast')) { return; }
    try { window.history.replaceState({ mgY: window.scrollY }, '', window.location.href); } catch (err) { /* the place is a convenience */ }
    var cur = views.filter(function (v) { return v.classList.contains('on'); })[0] || views[0];
    var mark = pendingBack = { y: window.scrollY, view: cur };
    // A link whose jump some other handler stops leaves nothing to come back from.
    window.setTimeout(function () { if (pendingBack === mark) { pendingBack = null; } }, 600);
  }, true);

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- one choice of role and sales motion */
  function setRole(k) {
    if (k !== 'all' && !roleById[k]) { k = 'all'; }
    state.role = k;
    store.set('role', k);
    $$('[data-pick="role"]').forEach(function (s) { s.value = k; });
    applyFilters(); renderRoles(); renderMatrix(); renderWeek(); renderFacts();
  }
  function setMotion(k) {
    if (!motionById[k]) { k = 'ent'; }
    state.motion = k;
    store.set('motion', k);
    $$('[data-pick="motion"]').forEach(function (s) { s.value = k; });
    applyFilters(); renderMatrix(); renderWeek();
  }
  $$('[data-pick]').forEach(function (s) {
    s.addEventListener('change', function () {
      if (s.getAttribute('data-pick') === 'role') { setRole(s.value); } else { setMotion(s.value); }
    });
  });

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- the skills list */
  var rows = $$('details.sk');
  var statusEl = $('#sk-count');
  var clearBtn = $('#sk-clear');
  var emptyEl = $('#sk-empty');
  var kindBtns = $$('[data-kind]');

  function visibleRows() { return rows.filter(function (el) { return !el.hidden; }); }

  function fitText(s) {
    var parts = [];
    if (state.role !== 'all' && s.roles[state.role] > 0) { parts.push(s.roles[state.role] === 2 ? 'Built for this role' : 'Also useful'); }
    if (s.seg) {
      var v = s.seg[state.motion][0], f = s.seg[state.motion][1];
      parts.push(v === 0 ? D.value['0'] : D.value[String(v)] + ', ' + D.freq[String(f)].toLowerCase());
    } else {
      parts.push('First run, and again as needed');
    }
    return parts.join('. ');
  }

  // Each skill's foot names the skill before and after it among the rows now showing.
  function labelFeet() {
    var vis = visibleRows();
    vis.forEach(function (el, i) {
      $$('[data-step]', el).forEach(function (b) {
        var t = vis[i + Number(b.getAttribute('data-step'))];
        var word = b.getAttribute('data-step') === '1' ? 'Next' : 'Previous';
        b.disabled = !t;
        b.textContent = t ? word + ': ' + t.getAttribute('data-id') : word;
      });
    });
  }

  function applyFilters() {
    var shown = 0;
    var q = state.q.trim().toLowerCase();
    var anyKind = Object.keys(state.kinds).some(function (k) { return state.kinds[k]; });
    rows.forEach(function (el) {
      var s = byId[el.getAttribute('data-id')];
      var ok = (state.role === 'all' || s.roles[state.role] > 0) &&
        (!anyKind || state.kinds[kind(s)]) &&
        (!q || el.getAttribute('data-text').indexOf(q) !== -1);
      el.hidden = !ok;
      if (ok) { shown += 1; }
      $('.fitm', el).textContent = fitText(s);
      $$('[data-m]', el).forEach(function (t) { t.style.fontWeight = t.getAttribute('data-m') === state.motion ? '800' : ''; });
    });
    $$('.grp').forEach(function (g) { g.hidden = !$$('details.sk', g).some(function (el) { return !el.hidden; }); });
    var active = state.role !== 'all' || anyKind || q !== '';
    statusEl.innerHTML = 'Showing <b>' + shown + '</b> of <b>' + rows.length + '</b> skills' +
      (state.role !== 'all' ? ' for ' + esc(roleById[state.role].name) : '') + ', rated for ' + esc(motionById[state.motion].name.toLowerCase());
    clearBtn.hidden = !active;
    emptyEl.hidden = shown !== 0;
    labelFeet();
  }

  function clearFilters() {
    state.kinds = {}; state.q = '';
    $('#q').value = '';
    kindBtns.forEach(function (b) { b.setAttribute('aria-pressed', 'false'); });
    if (state.role !== 'all') { setRole('all'); } else { applyFilters(); }
  }

  kindBtns.forEach(function (b) {
    b.addEventListener('click', function () {
      var k = b.getAttribute('data-kind');
      state.kinds[k] = !state.kinds[k];
      b.setAttribute('aria-pressed', state.kinds[k] ? 'true' : 'false');
      applyFilters();
    });
  });
  $('#q').addEventListener('input', function (e) { state.q = e.target.value; applyFilters(); });
  $('#q-form').addEventListener('submit', function (e) { e.preventDefault(); });
  clearBtn.addEventListener('click', clearFilters);
  $('#sk-empty-clear').addEventListener('click', clearFilters);
  $('#sk-open').addEventListener('click', function () { rows.forEach(function (el) { if (!el.hidden) { el.open = true; } }); });
  $('#sk-close').addEventListener('click', function () { rows.forEach(function (el) { el.open = false; }); });

  // The foot of an open skill: step to the one before or after, or close it. Either way the reader keeps
  // their place: the row they land on is put at the top and handed the focus.
  document.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
    var b = e.target.closest ? e.target.closest('[data-step],[data-shut]') : null;
    if (!b) { return; }
    var el = document.getElementById('skill-' + (b.getAttribute('data-of') || b.getAttribute('data-shut')));
    if (!el) { return; }
    var land = el;
    if (b.hasAttribute('data-step')) {
      var vis = visibleRows();
      land = vis[vis.indexOf(el) + Number(b.getAttribute('data-step'))];
      if (!land) { return; }
      land.open = true;
      try { window.history.replaceState(null, '', '#' + land.id); } catch (err) { /* the address is a convenience */ }
    }
    el.open = false;
    var sum = $('summary', land);
    focusQuiet(sum);
    try { sum.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'instant', block: b.hasAttribute('data-step') ? 'start' : 'center' }); } catch (err2) { sum.scrollIntoView(); }
  });

  // The same foot on a ChatGPT for Sales skill: its list has no filters, so the neighbours are fixed.
  document.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
    var b = e.target.closest ? e.target.closest('[data-gstep],[data-gshut]') : null;
    if (!b) { return; }
    var el = document.getElementById('gpt-' + (b.getAttribute('data-of') || b.getAttribute('data-gshut')));
    if (!el) { return; }
    var land = el;
    if (b.hasAttribute('data-gstep')) {
      land = document.getElementById('gpt-' + b.getAttribute('data-gstep'));
      if (!land) { return; }
      land.open = true;
      try { window.history.replaceState(null, '', '#' + land.id); } catch (err) { /* the address is a convenience */ }
    }
    el.open = false;
    var sum = $('summary', land);
    focusQuiet(sum);
    try { sum.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'instant', block: b.hasAttribute('data-gstep') ? 'start' : 'center' }); } catch (err2) { sum.scrollIntoView(); }
  });

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- the skills list: the run card */
  // Three conditions change what to say and what to hand over: whether Claude can reach the skill's tools,
  // whose book the request covers, and whether it runs on request or on a schedule.
  var runCards = $$('.run');
  var atEls = $$('[data-at]');
  var LEVEL_NAME = { 3: 'Inputs in place', 2: 'Works, with gaps', 1: 'From files', 0: 'Little to read' };
  var TOOL_SWITCHES = D.switches.filter(function (x) { return x.group === 'tools'; }).map(function (x) { return x.k; });

  function markSlots(text) { return esc(text).replace(/\[([^\]]+)\]/g, '<span class="slot">[$1]</span>'); }
  function setRun(card, name, v) {
    $$('[data-set="' + name + '"]', card).forEach(function (b) { b.setAttribute('aria-pressed', b.getAttribute('data-v') === v ? 'true' : 'false'); });
  }
  function renderRun(card) {
    var st = {};
    $$('[data-set][aria-pressed="true"]', card).forEach(function (b) { st[b.getAttribute('data-set')] = b.getAttribute('data-v'); });
    var say = $('.sayt[id]', card);
    var key = (st.scope === 'team' && st.time === 'sched') ? 'both' : (st.scope === 'team' ? 'team' : (st.time === 'sched' ? 'sched' : 'base'));
    var html = markSlots(say.getAttribute('data-' + key) || say.getAttribute('data-base'));
    if (say.innerHTML !== html) { say.innerHTML = html; }
    $$('[data-when]', card).forEach(function (el) {
      var w = el.getAttribute('data-when');
      el.hidden = w === 'sched' ? st.time !== 'sched' : w !== st.src;
    });
  }
  document.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
    var b = e.target.closest ? e.target.closest('.run [data-set]') : null;
    if (!b) { return; }
    var card = b.closest('.run');
    setRun(card, b.getAttribute('data-set'), b.getAttribute('data-v'));
    if (b.getAttribute('data-set') === 'src') { card.setAttribute('data-touched', '1'); }
    renderRun(card);
  });
  // Each card starts from the reader's readiness settings: on files when Claude cannot reach the skill's tools.
  function syncRun(match) {
    var anyTool = TOOL_SWITCHES.some(function (k) { return sw[k]; });
    var sketch = match ? D.presets.filter(function (x) { return x.k === match; })[0].name : '';
    var from = sketch ? 'These are the settings of the sketch \u201C' + esc(sketch) + '\u201D.' : 'These are your own settings.';
    runCards.forEach(function (card) {
      var s = byId[card.getAttribute('data-run')], r = evalSkill(s);
      if (!card.hasAttribute('data-touched')) { setRun(card, 'src', (s.tool.length ? r.toolOk : anyTool) ? 'conn' : 'files'); }
      renderRun(card);
    });
    atEls.forEach(function (el) {
      var s = byId[el.getAttribute('data-at')], r = evalSkill(s);
      el.innerHTML = '<strong>' + LEVEL_NAME[r.lvl] + '.</strong> ' + esc(reason(s, r)) + ' <span class="note">' + from + ' <a href="#readiness">Change them</a>.</span>';
    });
  }

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- fit: the role rows */
  var roleRows = $$('.rolerow');
  function renderRoles() {
    if (state.role === 'all') { return; }
    roleRows.forEach(function (r) { r.open = r.getAttribute('data-role') === state.role; });
  }
  // "What to configure first for this role" carries the row's role into the guide.
  roleRows.forEach(function (r) {
    var a = $('.go a', r);
    if (a) { a.addEventListener('click', function () { setRole(r.getAttribute('data-role')); }); }
  });

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- fit: the grid */
  function renderMatrix() {
    var m = state.motion;
    var cells = {};
    $$('.mx .cell').forEach(function (c) {
      $$('.chip', c).forEach(function (x) { x.parentNode.removeChild(x); });
      cells[c.getAttribute('data-v') + '-' + c.getAttribute('data-f')] = c;
    });
    var counts = [0, 0, 0, 0];
    var start = [], fallback = [], rule = [];
    D.skills.forEach(function (s) {
      if (!s.seg) { return; }
      var v = s.seg[m][0], f = s.seg[m][1];
      var off = state.role !== 'all' && s.roles[state.role] === 0;
      cells[v + '-' + f].insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend', chip(s, off));
      counts[v] += 1;
      if (off) { return; }
      if (v === 3 && f >= 3 && s.cls === 'R') { start.push(s); }
      if (v === 2 && f >= 3 && s.cls === 'R') { fallback.push(s); }
      if (v >= 2 && s.ov === 2) { rule.push(s); }
    });
    $$('.mx .cell').forEach(function (c) { c.classList.toggle('e', !$('.chip', c)); });
    $$('.mx .ql').forEach(function (q) {
      var any = $$('.mx .cell.q-' + q.getAttribute('data-q')).some(function (c) { return !c.classList.contains('e'); });
      $('.none', q).hidden = any;
    });
    // Where nothing marked Reads and proposes is core for the motion, fall back to what is useful, and say so.
    var startNote = 'Core for this motion, used weekly or daily and marked Reads and proposes.';
    if (!start.length && fallback.length) {
      start = fallback;
      startNote = 'No skill marked Reads and proposes is core here. These are useful, used weekly or daily and carry that marking.';
    }
    $('#pilot-start-note').textContent = startNote;
    var mo = motionById[m];
    $('#mo-name').textContent = mo.name;
    $('#mo-note').textContent = mo.note;
    $('#mo-eg').textContent = mo.desc + ' ' + mo.eg + '.';
    $('#mx-sum').textContent = mo.name + ': ' + counts[3] + ' core, ' + counts[2] + ' useful, ' + counts[1] + ' occasional and ' + counts[0] + ' a poor fit.' +
      (state.role !== 'all' ? ' Skills outside the chosen role, ' + roleById[state.role].name + ', are muted.' : '');
    var none = 'None at this setting.';
    $('#pilot-start').innerHTML = start.length ? start.map(function (s) { return chip(s, false); }).join('') : none;
    $('#pilot-rule').innerHTML = rule.length ? rule.map(function (s) { return chip(s, false); }).join('') : none;
  }

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- fit: through the week */
  var cadEls = $$('[data-cad]'), routineEls = $$('.routine');
  function renderWeek() {
    var m = state.motion, lists = { 1: [], 2: [], 3: [], 4: [], ev: [] }, n = 0;
    D.skills.forEach(function (s) {
      if (!s.seg || s.seg[m][0] < 2 || (state.role !== 'all' && s.roles[state.role] === 0)) { return; }
      lists[s.ev ? 'ev' : s.seg[m][1]].push(chip(s, false));
      n += 1;
    });
    cadEls.forEach(function (el) {
      var l = lists[el.getAttribute('data-cad')];
      el.innerHTML = l.length ? l.join('') : 'None at this setting.';
    });
    $('#cad-sum').textContent = n + ' skills rated core or useful for ' + motionById[m].name.toLowerCase() +
      (state.role !== 'all' ? ', for the role ' + roleById[state.role].name : ', across every role') + '.';
    var shown = 0;
    routineEls.forEach(function (el) {
      var ok = state.role === 'all' || (' ' + el.getAttribute('data-roles') + ' ').indexOf(' ' + state.role + ' ') !== -1;
      el.hidden = !ok;
      if (ok) { shown += 1; }
    });
    $('#rt-sum').textContent = state.role === 'all' ? '' : 'Showing ' + shown + ' of ' + routineEls.length + ' routines for the role ' + roleById[state.role].name + '.';
  }

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- before rollout: the readiness check */
  var boxes = $$('.sw input');
  var sw = {};
  var LEVELS = [3, 2, 1, 0];
  var LEVEL_SAY = { 3: 'with inputs in place', 2: 'with gaps', 1: 'from files', 0: 'with little to read' };

  function readSwitches() { boxes.forEach(function (b) { sw[b.getAttribute('data-k')] = b.checked; }); }

  function evalSkill(s) {
    var missCore = s.core.filter(function (k) { return !sw[k]; });
    var gaps = s.adds.filter(function (k) { return !sw[k]; });
    var toolOk = s.tool.length === 0 || s.tool.some(function (k) { return sw[k]; });
    var lvl = missCore.length ? 0 : (!toolOk ? 1 : (gaps.length ? 2 : 3));
    return { lvl: lvl, missCore: missCore, gaps: gaps, toolOk: toolOk };
  }

  function reason(s, r) {
    var labels = function (keys) { return join(keys.map(function (k) { return (s.as && s.as[k]) || swLabel[k]; })); };
    if (r.lvl === 0) { return 'Built around ' + labels(r.missCore) + ', which you do not keep.'; }
    if (r.lvl === 3) { return 'The kinds of input you ticked are in place.'; }
    var needed = r.gaps.filter(function (k) { return s.must.indexOf(k) !== -1; });
    var quiet = s.quiet || [];
    var decisions = r.gaps.filter(function (k) { return swGroup[k] === 'decisions' && s.must.indexOf(k) === -1 && quiet.indexOf(k) === -1; });
    var others = r.gaps.filter(function (k) { return (swGroup[k] !== 'decisions' || quiet.indexOf(k) !== -1) && !(r.lvl === 1 && s.tool.indexOf(k) !== -1); });
    var out = [];
    if (r.lvl === 1) {
      out.push('Claude cannot reach ' + join(s.tool.map(function (k) { return swLabel[k]; }), 'or') + '. It works from an export or from what you paste.');
    }
    if (others.length) { out.push('Would also use ' + labels(others) + '.'); }
    if (needed.length) { out.push('Needs ' + labels(needed) + ', and will ask once.'); }
    if (decisions.length) { out.push('Without ' + labels(decisions) + ', it reads what it can from your tools or uses a labeled default, and asks only if the answer changes the result.'); }
    return out.join(' ');
  }

  function renderFit() {
    readSwitches();
    var buckets = { 0: [], 1: [], 2: [], 3: [] };
    D.skills.forEach(function (s) {
      var r = evalSkill(s);
      buckets[r.lvl].push(r.lvl === 3 ? '<li>' + chip(s, false) + '</li>'
        : '<li>' + chip(s, false) + '<span>' + esc(reason(s, r)) + '</span></li>');
    });
    var say = [];
    LEVELS.forEach(function (l) {
      $('#t' + l).textContent = buckets[l].length;
      $('#p' + l).textContent = buckets[l].length;
      $('#lv' + l).innerHTML = buckets[l].length ? buckets[l].join('') : '<li class="nil">None at these settings.</li>';
      say.push(buckets[l].length + ' ' + LEVEL_SAY[l]);
    });
    $('#rd-say').textContent = 'Skills at these settings: ' + join(say) + '.';
    var match = null;
    D.presets.forEach(function (p) {
      var same = Object.keys(p.on).every(function (k) { return p.on[k] === !!sw[k]; });
      if (same && !match) { match = p.k; }
    });
    $$('[data-preset]').forEach(function (b) { b.setAttribute('aria-pressed', b.getAttribute('data-preset') === match ? 'true' : 'false'); });
    store.set('sw', sw);
    syncRun(match);
  }

  function applyPreset(key) {
    var p = D.presets.filter(function (x) { return x.k === key; })[0];
    if (!p) { return; }
    boxes.forEach(function (b) { b.checked = !!p.on[b.getAttribute('data-k')]; });
    renderFit();
  }

  boxes.forEach(function (b) { b.addEventListener('change', renderFit); });
  $$('[data-preset]').forEach(function (b) { b.addEventListener('click', function () { applyPreset(b.getAttribute('data-preset')); }); });

  // On a narrow screen the results sit below the switches. A small bar keeps the four counts in view
  // while the switches are on screen and the results are not.
  var pin = $('#rdpin'), swEl = $('#switches'), resEl = $('#rd-results');
  if (pin && swEl && resEl && 'IntersectionObserver' in window) {
    var seen = { sw: false, res: false };
    var io = new IntersectionObserver(function (entries) {
      entries.forEach(function (en) { if (en.target === swEl) { seen.sw = en.isIntersecting; } else { seen.res = en.isIntersecting; } });
      var on = seen.sw && !seen.res;
      pin.classList.toggle('on', on);
      pin.setAttribute('aria-hidden', on ? 'false' : 'true');
      $('a', pin).tabIndex = on ? 0 : -1;
    });
    io.observe(swEl); io.observe(resEl);
  }

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- configure: facts, copy buttons, film, contents */
  var factList = $('#fact-list');
  var factRows = $$('.fact', factList);
  var factNote = $('#fact-note');
  function renderFacts() {
    var g = state.role === 'all' ? null : roleById[state.role].guide;
    $$('.rolecard').forEach(function (c) { c.classList.toggle('on', !!g && c.getAttribute('data-grole') === g); });
    if (!g || g === 'ops') {
      factList.classList.remove('pick');
      factRows.forEach(function (r) { r.classList.remove('hit'); });
      factNote.textContent = g === 'ops' ? 'Revenue operations owns every row.' : '';
      return;
    }
    var n = 0;
    factRows.forEach(function (r) {
      var hit = (' ' + r.getAttribute('data-roles') + ' ').indexOf(' ' + g + ' ') !== -1;
      r.classList.toggle('hit', hit);
      if (hit) { n += 1; }
    });
    factList.classList.add('pick');
    factNote.textContent = (WORDS[n] || n) + ' of the ' + factRows.length + ' facts come first for the role you chose, ' + roleById[state.role].name + '. They carry a lime mark.';
  }

  // Checklist: ticks are a convenience kept in this browser, and the page works without them.
  var ticks = store.get('facts') || {};
  var tickBoxes = $$('input[type="checkbox"]', factList), doneCount = $('#done-count');
  function tally() { doneCount.textContent = tickBoxes.filter(function (b) { return b.checked; }).length; }
  tickBoxes.forEach(function (b) {
    b.checked = !!ticks[b.id];
    b.addEventListener('change', function () { ticks[b.id] = b.checked; store.set('facts', ticks); tally(); });
  });
  tally();

  // Copy buttons. If the browser refuses, select the text so the reader can copy it by hand.
  function flash(btn, word) {
    if (!btn.getAttribute('data-label')) { btn.setAttribute('data-label', btn.textContent); }
    btn.textContent = word; btn.classList.add('is-done');
    window.setTimeout(function () { btn.textContent = btn.getAttribute('data-label'); btn.classList.remove('is-done'); }, 1900);
  }
  function selectNode(node) {
    try { var r = document.createRange(); r.selectNodeContents(node); var s = window.getSelection(); s.removeAllRanges(); s.addRange(r); } catch (e) { /* nothing to select with */ }
  }
  $$('[data-copy]').forEach(function (btn) {
    btn.addEventListener('click', function () {
      var src = document.getElementById(btn.getAttribute('data-copy')), text;
      try { text = src.tagName === 'SCRIPT' ? JSON.parse(src.textContent) : src.textContent; } catch (e) { text = src.textContent; }
      text = text.replace(/\s+$/, '') + '\n';
      var fallback = function () {
        var box = btn.getAttribute('data-fallback') && document.getElementById(btn.getAttribute('data-fallback'));
        if (box) { box.hidden = false; box.value = text; box.focus(); box.select(); } else { selectNode(src); }
        flash(btn, 'Selected: now copy');
      };
      try { navigator.clipboard.writeText(text).then(function () { flash(btn, 'Copied'); }, fallback); } catch (e) { fallback(); }
    });
  });

  // If the film cannot load, say so instead of leaving a dead player.
  var film = $('#film');
  if (film) { film.addEventListener('error', function () { film.classList.add('failed'); }, true); }

  // Contents for the guide: mark the section being read, and fold the narrow-screen list after a choice.
  var cfg = viewByName.configure;
  var tocLinks = $$('.toc a'), heads = $$('.art > h2[id], .art > .hand[id]', cfg);
  if (tocLinks.length && heads.length) {
    var tick = false, cur = '';
    var mark = function () {
      tick = false;
      if (!cfg.classList.contains('on')) { return; }
      var id = heads[0].id;
      for (var i = 0; i < heads.length; i += 1) { if (heads[i].getBoundingClientRect().top <= 150) { id = heads[i].id; } else { break; } }
      if (id === cur) { return; }
      cur = id;
      tocLinks.forEach(function (a) { if (a.getAttribute('href') === '#' + id) { a.setAttribute('aria-current', 'true'); } else { a.removeAttribute('aria-current'); } });
    };
    window.addEventListener('scroll', function () { if (!tick) { tick = true; window.requestAnimationFrame(mark); } }, { passive: true });
    window.addEventListener('hashchange', function () { window.requestAnimationFrame(mark); });
    mark();
  }
  $$('.tocm a').forEach(function (a) { a.addEventListener('click', function () { a.closest('details').open = false; }); });

  /* ---------------------------------------------------------------- start */
  var savedRole = store.get('role'), savedMotion = store.get('motion');
  if (typeof savedMotion === 'string' && motionById[savedMotion]) { state.motion = savedMotion; }
  if (typeof savedRole === 'string' && roleById[savedRole]) { state.role = savedRole; }
  $$('[data-pick="role"]').forEach(function (s) { s.value = state.role; });
  $$('[data-pick="motion"]').forEach(function (s) { s.value = state.motion; });
  var savedSw = store.get('sw');
  if (savedSw && typeof savedSw === 'object') {
    boxes.forEach(function (b) { var k = b.getAttribute('data-k'); if (typeof savedSw[k] === 'boolean') { b.checked = savedSw[k]; } });
  }
  applyFilters(); renderRoles(); renderMatrix(); renderWeek(); renderFacts();
  if (savedSw && typeof savedSw === 'object') { renderFit(); } else { applyPreset('ent'); }

  // Links that carry #section. The host can show this page narrow at first and then widen it, which leaves the
  // first jump far off. Go to the section again while the layout settles, unless the reader has started to scroll.
  var landed = route(false);
  if (landed) {
    var moved = false, until = Date.now() + 3000;
    ['wheel', 'touchstart', 'keydown', 'mousedown'].forEach(function (ev) {
      window.addEventListener(ev, function () { moved = true; }, { passive: true });
    });
    var settle = function () { if (!moved && Date.now() <= until && landed.isConnected) { jump(landed); } };
    window.addEventListener('load', settle);
    window.addEventListener('resize', settle);
    if (document.fonts && document.fonts.ready) { document.fonts.ready.then(settle); }
    [300, 900, 1800, 2600].forEach(function (ms) { window.setTimeout(settle, ms); });
  }
}());
</script>
</body>
</html>
